One may attach arbitrarily many independent modules, including on SAT sign neighbors. This is exact nonlocal normalization, not a rank or basis heuristic. It preserves I21 source semantics, complements DA15's failed affine split and M21's convex/lattice stagnation, and uses no DR15 semantic-equivalence oracle.

Prepend this reduction to the fixed total cooperative portfolio. A polynomial-time exact solver for the strict cores would give a polynomial solver for every input, so the first remaining universal problem may be restricted to these cores. If the resulting portfolio has no polynomial bound, its hard computation must survive this preprocessing; the reduced core encodings have polynomial size and their solve times cannot be uniformly polynomial in their own lengths (else composition would be polynomial). The diagonal lemma applies to the fixed total core solver, not to all imaginable solvers simultaneously. This does not establish that the surviving sequence has a particular hypergraph geometry or label. The NP-completeness result shows that this reduction alone cannot discharge U-PROGRESS; the new precise constructive target is uniform signed-basis compatibility or another paid contracting rule on strict cores. Complete affine closure is still not an oracle, and all failed branches, key accesses and witness lifts remain subject to Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate}. See Rado (1942), \href{https://doi.org/10.1093/qmath/os-13.1.83}{A theorem on independence relations}, and standard deterministic matroid intersection. This is an algebraic extension of the matching-autarky/deficiency idea, not a claim of novelty for the underlying matroid theorem.

The DS15 checker executed binary linear/partition matroid intersection and core discovery, with 91 exhaustive-reference rank-two controls (89 SAT, 2 UNSAT), plus six scaling controls up to 55 coordinates. It verified 3209 lifts and 215391 assertions; 739 matroid calls, 9824 augmentations, 77115 recorded elimination reductions, 194 stagnant singleton scans and 21232 reference words. The one-module Fano pair had minimum visible support three and reduced mass $22$ to $14$; the 12-module pair reduced $110$ to $14$. Scaling decisions enumerate only the seven-variable core, not the large source. The general affine guard absorption, actual strict E4/B4 input adapter, and full portfolio were not executed in this checker. Physical cumulative memory and kernel verification were not measured. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Quadratic polar elimination and a persistent strict-core obstruction (BA15)}\label{sec:ba15}
To attack the signed-basis gap of Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual}, we allow a global simultaneous algebraic cancellation across \emph{all} owners, rather than just singletons, pairs, or private directions. This is distinct from complete semantic closure and does not invoke it. The result below identifies which linear combinations of the original degree-two violation equations can give unconditional information on the actual guarded chart.

\begin{lemma}[Paid quadratic-polar normalization]\label{lem:ba15-polar}
For an explicit rank-at-most-two affine-packet chart, absorb the actual affine guard by Gaussian parameterization. A packet with two independent equations $a_i x=b_i,\ c_i x=d_i$ has Boolean violation polynomial
\[
 q_i=(1+a_i x+b_i)(1+c_i x+d_i)
\]
in the Boolean ring over $\mathbb F_2$, with $x_j^2=x_j$. Acceptance is exactly $q_i=0$ for every $i$; rank-zero/one packets have the corresponding constant/linear polynomials. There is a uniform polynomial-time exact preprocessing which, after mandatory singleton closure, finds any nontrivial $\mathbb F_2$-linear combination of these polynomials whose degree-two part vanishes. Such a combination is either a necessary affine equation, a contradiction, or an identically zero polynomial certifying that one original equation is redundant. Repeated substitution and deletion terminates with either a decision (if contradicted or all packets removed) or a chart whose retained quadratic parts are independent. It preserves the entire original accepted support through the guard lift.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use the unique squarefree degree-at-most-two coefficient vector, obtainable by evaluating at zero, each coordinate vector and each pair of coordinate vectors in the free chart. There are $O(d^2)$ entries for $d$ free variables. Gaussian elimination of the quadratic coordinates, carrying the lower-degree coefficients and optionally the original owner IDs as payload, finds a dependence. If the residual polynomial is the constant one, reject. If it is a nonconstant affine polynomial, impose its zero equation, which lowers the free dimension, and restart singleton closure and elimination. If it is zero, the owner with the new dependent row is a linear combination of earlier owners' equations, so delete only that owner; the others imply its equation. Repeat at stagnation. Same-plane cancellation of different forbidden words is included. Each restart decreases $1+d+m$, where $m$ is the number of live packets; no packet is duplicated and no new nonlinear constraint is emitted. A lift is given by the actual stored affine parameterization, verified against the original chart. The number of coefficient evaluations, Gaussian operations, substitution records, closure restarts and accesses is polynomial in the explicit encoding (and the original $N$ whenever its current chart length is polynomial in $N$); all finite-field operations use polynomial-bit representations. On a serial implementation the same execution has polynomial $T,S$ and $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is a paid unary normalization, not a common global potential for all portfolio rules. It concerns only constant-coefficient linear combinations of the $q_i$, not arbitrary polynomial-calculus multipliers or the complete semantic hull.
\end{proof}
For instance, three distinct rank-two packets with violation polynomials $ab,ac,a(b+c+1)$ have stagnant singleton and same-plane closure yet their sum forces $a=0$. Changing the last constant to zero gives a genuine redundant packet instead. On a separate pair of private bits $d,e$, adding $(1+a)d,(1+a)e,(1+a)(d+e+1)$ forces $a=1$ and produces a contradiction. These are actual necessary deductions, not choices of witnesses.

\begin{lemma}[Linear NAE strict cores defeat root quadratic-polar closure]\label{lem:ba15-core}
For every linear signed 3-uniform NAE registry on distinct triples, the unguarded quadratic parts of the violation polynomials are independent. Moreover, deciding the nonempty strict rank-Hall cores of Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual} is NP-complete even on signed linear 3-uniform NAE registries of maximum variable degree four, with singleton, same-plane, and the entire root quadratic-polar preprocessing stagnant.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a triple $u,v,w$, the quadratic part is $x_u x_v+x_u x_w+x_v x_w$, independent of its forbidden signs. Every pair of vertices appears in at most one triple by linearity; thus each polynomial contains its own unique quadratic coordinates. An invertible affine parameterization preserves independence of the quadratic parts because polar bilinear forms transform by invertible congruence. Apply the established NP-completeness of positive linear NAE-3SAT with every variable occurring exactly four times (Darmann, D\"ocker and Dorn, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03395}{arXiv:2412.03395}, also citing the earlier degree-four result). Use the polynomial rank-Hall core extraction of Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-core} on this source. The retained subset remains linear with maximum degree at most four, and is strictly deficient if nonempty; it can be returned in its original variable names after removing unused variables. It is equisatisfiable by the proved lift. If the extracted core is empty, return the fixed signed Fano YES core of Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual}; the monotone Fano root is a fixed NO core. Both are linear with maximum degree three. No singleton or same-plane deduction occurs at these roots, and the unique-pair argument excludes any nontrivial quadratic-polar combination. The resulting restricted decision problem is in NP: check linearity and degrees, rank-Hall strictness via core recognition, then verify a model. Hence the restriction is NP-complete. This is a reduction to a more precise remaining universal problem, not a proof that another polynomial decision method is impossible.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Low-rank guards restrict global polar cancellations on high-girth cores]\label{lem:ba15-guard}
Let a signed linear NAE hypergraph have maximum variable degree $D$ and Berge girth $g$ (number of hyperedges in the shortest incidence cycle). Let $W$ be any consistent affine guard rowspace of rank $k$. If a nonempty set $J$ of original owners has a sum of violation polynomials that becomes affine on the guard chart, then
\[
 |J|\le 4D^2 k/3.
\]
If additionally $g>4D^2 k/3$, then $|J|\le k$. In particular, for a raw linear NAE source with $g>8D^2/3$, arbitrary one-bit affine branching followed by the singleton and quadratic-polar preprocessing above still has the rank-mass credit failure of Lemma~\ref{lem:da15-one}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $B_J$ be the symmetric zero-diagonal binary polar matrix of $\sum_{i\in J}q_i$. Its off-diagonal entries form the adjacency matrix of the 2-section of the selected triples; linearity makes its number of nonzero entries exactly $6|J|$. Its row weight is at most $\Delta=2D$. If its rank is $\rho$, the union of supports of $\rho$ basis rows covers every nonzero column, so at most $\rho\Delta$ columns are active; symmetry gives the same bound for rows. Consequently $6|J|\le \rho\Delta^2$. If the sum becomes affine under the guard, its polar form restricted to the translation subspace $\ker W$ vanishes. In a basis with that subspace last, the matrix has a zero bottom-right block and only $k$ other rows/columns, so $\rho\le2k$. This proves the first bound without any restriction on guard density or signs.
If $g>4D^2 k/3$, the selected incidence subgraph is a forest. In any component root at a variable node, choose a deepest triple node, and delete it together with its two private leaf variables. The polar matrix block on the two leaves is $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$; its only coupling to the other vertices is through their shared parent variable. Its Schur complement contribution is zero in characteristic two. Hence deleting the triple lowers rank by exactly two. Induction yields $\rho=2|J|$ and thus $|J|\le k$.
For the branching corollary, after singleton closure on either one-bit child, Lemma~\ref{lem:da15-one} gives guard rank at most two, surviving rank-two packets and pairwise distinct projected observation planes. A single such packet has nonzero polar form; two such forms can cancel only if their rank-two rowspaces coincide, since the polar matrix of $(a x)(c x)$ is $ac^\top+ca^\top$ and its image is $\langle a,c\rangle$. The preceding bound would require a dependency on at most two owners, which is impossible. Therefore no additional quadratic-polar deduction, deletion or contradiction occurs in either child; their two-child fixed-power rank-mass obstruction is unchanged. This does not constrain higher-degree algebraic inference, nonlinear rewrites, or unrestricted SAT algorithms.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Implication for the actual missing universal step.}
The exact I21 compiler and DS15 preprocessing can be followed by this paid global closure, but the latter cannot universally discharge Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}: an NP-complete signed linear strict-core class survives even at maximum degree four. Assuming a fixed exact core solver lacks a uniform polynomial bound, Lemma~\ref{lem:diagonal} supplies a hard sequence for that solver on this restricted problem if its restriction is itself nonpolynomial; the NP-completeness statement alone neither implies that hypothesis nor assigns any particular geometry or girth to that sequence. A genuinely different normalization, e.g.\ signed-basis search with actual consistency or a higher-degree inference whose aggregate cost is proved, is still required. A rank decrease on a successful preprocessing plan is not a proof of universal coverage. No assertion of relativizing or nonrelativizing separation is made; no unrestricted lower bound follows. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN.

The BA15 executable finite checker (379 rank-two controls, 123 SAT and 256 UNSAT by reference, plus 14 rank-three single-budget Fano neighbors counted only by reference) checked complete accepted assignment sets before and after closure, including 254 one-bit guarded Fano root charts and 100 seeded affine-packet controls. It recorded 39473 assertions, 747 closure scans, 145 forced rows, 223 deletions, 40368 reference words, 267 nonempty polar subsets and 3204 guarded subset tests, with no discrepancy. Fano root and all 21 sign neighbors have no root deduction; among the 254 guarded Fano root charts, 14 reject by the stronger closure, so the high-girth hypothesis is essential to the branching corollary. The three synthetic controls respectively force one row with four models, delete a redundant packet with five models, and reject. The general degree-four NP-hardness reduction, rank-Hall implementation, high-girth infinite families, strict native compiler, full portfolio and formal kernel verification were not executed here; the general claims above are symbolic proofs with stated premises. Physical cumulative memory was not instrumented.


\section{Joint Fourier--PSD, lattice and rank-Hall stagnation under genuine guards (MS21)}\label{sec:ms21}
We test the actual missing progress in Obligation~\ref{obl:progress} on the rank-two charts of Lemmas~\ref{lem:i21-reverse} and~\ref{lem:ds15-residual}. The usual signed NAE triangle moment identity is $4e_i=1+\sum_{a<b}\eta_{ia}\eta_{ib}s_as_b$, where $s_v=(-1)^{x_v}$ and the $\eta$ are occurrence signs. An exact rational PSD infeasibility certificate for zero expected rejection is sound; for the positive Fano chart, summing the seven triangle equations gives $\mathbf 1^\top G\mathbf 1=7-14=-7$ for any proposed PSD moment matrix $G$ with unit diagonal. This resolves that control, unlike the M21 convex and lattice filters alone. We ask whether adding even the global source-spin triangle SDP to those filters repairs the universal branch-progress gap. The following gives a simultaneous obstruction, including nontrivial parity guards, not merely pins of the global complement symmetry.

\begin{lemma}[An exact positive-definite source-spin moment certificate]\label{lem:ms21-gram}
For any signed NAE registry on a $(3,8)$-biregular incidence graph of girth greater than $50$, there is an explicit rational positive-definite matrix $G$, with diagonal $1$, signed pair moment $-1/3$ on every clause pair, and $|G_{uv}|\le1/3$ on distinct variables, strictly smaller for nonadjacent variables. It has zero entries when primal distance exceeds eight. All triangle inequalities hold. On a subregistry, the restricted matrix keeps the same positivity and moment properties, with all distance and adjacency bounds measured in the original graph (not in the graph after clause deletion).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use primal distance (one step between variables in a common clause), and let $\xi(u,w)$ be the product of occurrence signs along the unique shortest incidence path for distances at most four. Define the integer matrix $Z_{uw}=(-1)^h4^{4-h}\xi(u,w)$ for $h=\operatorname{dist}(u,w)\le4$ and zero otherwise. For overlapping supports of two rows at distance $h\le8$, their union and its connecting paths lie in the radius-$24$ incidence ball at the first variable; girth greater than $50$ makes this ball a tree. In particular all signs in their inner product have the common path sign. The signed-free diagonal squared norm is $4^8[1+16\sum_{t=1}^4 14^{t-1}16^{-t}]=282496$. With $r=-1/4$, the signed-free off-diagonal dot product divided by $4^8$ is the sum of the following two expressions:
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{h}\ \sum_{\substack{t\ge0\\j+t,h-j+t\le4}} a_{jt}r^{h+2t},
\qquad
\sum_{j=1}^{h}\ \sum_{\substack{t\ge0\\j+t,h-j+1+t\le4}}14^t r^{h+1+2t},
\]
where $a_{j0}=1$ and $a_{jt}=2(8-d_j)14^{t-1}$ for $t>0$, with $d_j=1$ at the two endpoints and $2$ otherwise. The first sum counts branches at path variables, the second at path clauses (one extra variable per clause). Multiplying by $4^8$ gives for $h=1,\ldots,8$ respectively $-94920,28432,-7132,1656,-290,52,-7,1$. Therefore
\[
G=(ZZ^\top+2264 I)/284760
\]
is strictly positive definite, has the stated diagonal and signed moments, and satisfies every triangle inequality $1+\epsilon G_{uv}+\delta G_{uw}+\epsilon\delta G_{vw}\ge0$ for $\epsilon,\delta\in\{-1,1\}$ by the absolute bound. All entries have constant-size rational numerators and denominators. Construction by bounded-radius searches, exact arithmetic and matrix products, and verification of all equations and PSD, are polynomial bit work. This is an exact finite-radius specialization of the signed Gaussian-wave approach of Deshpande et al., SODA 2019, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.05230}{arXiv:1804.05230}, Sec.~5, not an assertion of novelty for the underlying SDP gap.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Simultaneous guard-stable obstruction on strict cores]\label{lem:ms21-guards}
There are arbitrarily large inclusion-minimal UNSAT linear signed NAE registries and same-geometry SAT sign neighbors satisfying Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual}. On each UNSAT registry, one can select $p=\Omega(n_c)$ disjoint relative-parity observations, where $n_c$ is the number of occurring core variables, such that every assignment of any subset of those observations leaves the following specified filters stagnant: singleton/same-plane and two-owner affine-hull closure, BA15 quadratic-polar closure, rank-Hall peeling, M21 local-option full-support convex/lattice filtering, and the source-spin PSD/triangle relaxation coupled to the same local first and pair moments. The entire registry remains UNSAT. In particular neither child of these genuine one-bit guards is certified by these filters or has reduced rank mass $1+2m$. This is a restricted dispatch obstruction, not a lower bound for arbitrary guard choices or normalization.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For every fixed $g>50$ choose a finite $(3,8)$-biregular incidence graph of girth greater than $g$. Existence follows from the random-lift cycle theorem quoted as Theorem~4.3 in Deshpande et al. (the base graph is $K_{8,3}$). With $n$ variables and $8n/3$ clauses, independent uniform occurrence signs give expected number of satisfying words $2^n(3/4)^{8n/3}<1$ because $8\cdot3^8<4^8$. Thus an UNSAT signing exists. Choose an inclusion-minimal UNSAT subregistry, existentially only; there is no claim of a polynomial algorithm to discover it. It is connected, is linear, and has more than $g/2$ clauses: an incidence forest admits a model by assigning outward from a root variable, choosing the two new variables at each clause to satisfy it. A cycle in any subregistry uses at most twice its clause count. Since the original degree is bounded, $n_c$ is unbounded with $g$. Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual} gives the strict Hall condition. Deleting one owner admits a model that violates just that owner; flipping one of its occurrence signs gives a SAT neighbor on the identical geometry, hence with identical rank-Hall conditions. Lemma~\ref{lem:ms21-gram} applies to both signings before restriction. I21 compiles either to strict E4/B4, but this moment certificate is asserted only on the reverse source chart, not on all native edge variables.

Select a set of core variables with pairwise primal distance greater than $24$ measured in the original graph. Greedy radius-$24$ packing yields at least $n_c/B$ variables for an absolute constant $B$ (the primal degree is at most $16$). Pair them arbitrarily, discarding at most one. For a subset of $q$ pairs impose $s_a s_b=\sigma_{ab}$, where $\sigma_{ab}\in\{-1,1\}$. These are independent affine observations of the core rowspace because the core is connected and the pairs are disjoint. Use $g_a=Ge_a$ for the original columns. The selected anchors have Gram submatrix identity and disjoint support. The guarded matrix
\[
G'=G+\sum_{(a,b)}\sigma_{ab}(g_a g_b^\top+g_b g_a^\top)
\]
is PSD: subtract the outer products of the selected anchor columns by the Schur complement, then add $(g_a+\sigma_{ab}g_b)(g_a+\sigma_{ab}g_b)^\top$ for each pair. The diagonal is unchanged, and $G'_{ab}=\sigma_{ab}$ with the two columns linked by exactly that sign. All other altered entries are between anchor supports, where the original entry was zero by the distance-$24$ separation. They have magnitude at most $1/3$ unless they are the paired anchor entries. No original clause pair is altered. Every triangle inequality still holds: without a paired anchor pair use the absolute bound, and with one use the exact signed column equality and $|G'_{uv}|\le1$. This is an exact joint guard construction, not independent marginal conditioning.

Use zero first moments and the uniform distribution on the six allowed signed local words at every owner. Its pair moments agree exactly with $G'$ and all occurrence means agree; every local option has positive weight. The guards are satisfied by the pair marginals of $G'$, including the signed mean relation. For the separate signed integer-affine filter, choose any surviving local option and extend its bits to a global Boolean word satisfying the imposed pair guards; each owner contains at most one anchor. Every unpinned NAE option set has difference lattice $\mathbb Z^3$ (each coordinate unit difference occurs between legal words). Thus the occurrence word of every other owner is representable as an integer affine combination of its legal local options. These signed combinations meet all star quotas and the guard equalities $x_a-x_b=0$ or $x_a+x_b=1$. The chosen local option can have coefficient one with the others zero. This shows full support for the convex/PSD filter and separately for the integer-affine filter on the \emph{same} chart. It does not assert an integral PSD solution or positive probability for an arbitrary joint owner configuration.

Let $H$ be the guard rowspace in binary coordinates. Every nonzero member is a union of selected far-pair endpoints. For any one or two owners, $H\cap(U_i+U_j)=0$: a row from a single plane is supported on a clause pair, and a sum of two such rows is either a pair at distance at most two or a four-set with two points in one clause. None can be a nonempty union of separated anchors. Thus each owner still has rank two, distinct planes stay distinct, and every one-/two-owner projection is unchanged up to affine recharting. Two distinct original planes are independent by linearity, so their joint legal hull is full; the stated local closure gives no equation. In addition, absorbing these specific guards by substituting one variable for its paired partner plus a constant preserves linearity of the signed NAE registry: no clause contains two anchors, and a repeated projected pair from two distinct clauses would either have already repeated before substitution or would require two distinct anchors sharing a neighbor, at original distance at most two. Both are impossible. By Lemma~\ref{lem:ba15-polar} and the unique quadratic-pair argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:ba15-core}, the entire constant-coefficient quadratic-polar closure is stagnant under every subset of the separated guards, not merely under low-rank ones. Since $H\subseteq\sum_i U_i$ and has rank $q$, $d'(E)=d(E)+q$, while $d'(J)\le d(J)+q$ for every proper $J$. The strict inequality persists and rank-Hall peeling leaves the entire core. Every guard is consistent as a binary chart, but no guard can make the UNSAT source feasible. Run the stated certified closure initially, after guard substitution and at stagnation; all these deductions are guarded, and the branch observations are not unconditional facts. This proves the simultaneous obstruction.
\end{proof}

For completeness, a single absolute pin $s_v=\tau$ can also be handled by retaining $G$ and taking first moment vector $\tau G e_v$; the extended matrix including a constant coordinate is PSD by the Schur complement. On a signed clause with signed means $m_i$, its minority-$i$, majority-sign-$t$ word has weight $1/6+t(m_j+m_k)/4$, with the other two indices $j,k$. These weights reproduce all means and pair correlations $-1/3$. At a clause not incident to $v$, at most one variable is adjacent to $v$ by high girth, so all six weights are strictly positive; at an incident clause exactly the three pin-compatible options have positive weights. This is not a substitute for the genuine relative guards in Lemma~\ref{lem:ms21-guards}.

\paragraph{Aggregate and physical implications.}
The specified filters cannot supply Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate}'s branch-credit decrease on these separated queries: both children retain the same raw rank mass. A policy that blindly enumerates all $p$ selected guards on the UNSAT chart without other pruning has $2^p$ leaves. Neither this policy nor the existence of these certificates excludes a different adaptive split, a different representation, higher-order moments, exact nonlinear support queries or nonlocal deductions. No universal simultaneous hard sequence for the entire portfolio is asserted. The polynomial arithmetic above includes Gram construction, guard recharting, failed local closure and verification, candidate anchor discovery and all explicit guard records; polynomial peak storage and cumulative storage at most $(T+1)S$ apply to one such relaxation job, not to an exponential branching tree. The strict core can be discovered by the paid DS15 reduction, but inclusion-minimal core extraction in this existence argument is not offered as a solver. An actual decrease of the physical quotient $\dim(L/L_2)$ still requires an authenticated native restriction/lift. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN. The next target is a paid higher-order identity or nonlocal compatibility contraction which acts on the same core, rather than unconditionally treating pair moments or integer-affine support as exact global support.

Executed MS21 checks: eight explicit finite incidence-tree counts (1,114,967 incidence vertices total), 4,731 exact local moment laws, Fano and 21 single-sign neighbors (2,816 reference words), 66,477 assertions; a separate disjoint four-triple SAT control tested all nine partial separated-guard charts by exact rational-equivalent PSD elimination, 7,920 triangle inequalities, 36,864 reference words and 9,774 assertions. No finite large-girth UNSAT instance was generated; the infinite existence and guard theorem are symbolic. No full native compiler, whole portfolio, measured CM or formal kernel build executed.


\section{One-violated-owner repair normal form is NP-complete (BH15)}\label{sec:bh15}
DO01 supplies single-budget SAT neighbors of critical monotone NAE cores. A proposed way around the raw field and syndrome width barriers is to start from a known model of such a neighbor and restore the missing rejection word through a local-to-global flip repair. We show that the existence of this anchor alone is insufficient to settle Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}. Unlike a large nominal state count, the following is an exact polynomial reduction to the proposed repair problem itself.
\begin{lemma}[Exact two-closed-class repair formulation]\label{lem:bh15-flip}
Let a signed ternary NAE registry have a supplied word $w$ violating exactly one owner $e_*$. Write $z=x+w$ over $\mathbb F_2$. Each owner other than $e_*$ becomes the relation
\[
R(a;b,c):\quad z_a\in\{z_b,z_c\},
\]
where $a$ is its unique minority literal position under $w$. The exceptional owner becomes positive NAE. Thus exact repair is equivalent to partitioning all vertices into two sets each closed under every implication $\{b,c\}\to a$, with the exceptional triple meeting both parts.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
After switching by $w$, each satisfied owner's literal signs are a nonconstant ternary word. Complementing all three signs leaves NAE unchanged; normalize the minority to one and the other two to zero. The only forbidden flip triples are $(1,0,0)$ and $(0,1,1)$. These are exactly the two failures of the displayed relation. A violated owner's literal signs are constant and can all be normalized to zero. Membership of both tails in either color class forces the head into that class, proving the closed-set identity. Every solution lifts by XOR with the known anchor; actual conditional pins and guards must be translated too. No independent gluing of partially closed classes is justified.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Hardness of repair even with a supplied single-budget model]\label{lem:bh15-repair}
Deciding signed NAE-3SAT given an actual word violating exactly one specified owner is NP-complete even when all triples have distinct vertices, their hypergraph is linear, maximum variable degree is four, and the supplied word is all zero. Equivalently, the supplied word satisfies the single-budget relaxation allowing its rejected monochromatic word at that owner. For every fixed integer $g\ge2$, the repair problem is also NP-complete on signed linear hypergraphs of Berge girth greater than $g$ with a constant degree bound depending on $g$ (not necessarily four). All reductions preserve both labels and have polynomial source witness lifts.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Start from arbitrary signed ternary NAE, available uniformly from 3-CNF by Lemma~\ref{lem:i21-source}, allowing repeated positions at this intermediate stage. Replace each variable by two representatives $X_i,Y_i$. Introduce three selectors $s_0,s_1,s_2$ and impose $R(s_j;X_i,Y_i)$ for each $i,j$. Add the designated exceptional owner $\operatorname{NAE}(s_0,s_1,s_2)$. All $R$ relations accept zero, while this exceptional owner rejects it. In any satisfying assignment the selector word contains both bits, so the three $R$ relations force $X_i\ne Y_i$. For each source triple use the literal representatives $a,b,c$ (positive occurrences use $X_i$, negative use $Y_i$), and impose $R(\bar a;b,c)$, where $\bar a$ denotes the opposite representative, not a new independently assigned bit. Once the complement constraints hold, this is precisely the original signed NAE. Conversely a source model extends by complementary representatives and any of the six nonconstant selector words. The base registry has $B=2n+3$ variables and $M=3n+m+1$ clauses and an explicitly supplied near-model, with exactly six base lifts per source model.

For degree and linearity, use a constant-size equality gadget. In the seven-line Fano plane each pair of vertices lies in exactly one line. If all lines were nonmonochromatic there would be exactly seven monochromatic pairs, whereas any binary partition of seven vertices has at least nine. Delete a line; all models of the remaining six lines have the three deleted-line terminals equal. An explicit legal word is zero on those terminals and one on the other four vertices (each other line meets the deleted line once). Switch all occurrence signs by that word, so all zero is legal and the terminals remain constrained equal; complement symmetry also makes all one legal.

Replace every occurrence position in the base registry by its own leaf, keeping the actual sign. For each variable $v$ with $t$ occurrences form a backbone $v_0=v,v_1,\ldots,v_t$, and at step $j$ attach an independent switched equality gadget with terminals $v_{j-1},v_j,\ell_j$ (the occurrence leaf). The remaining four vertices of each gadget are private. Each legal assignment synchronizes every leaf with its base bit. Conversely any base assignment lifts by giving all vertices of each gadget that same bit. Each backbone vertex has degree at most four, each leaf degree three and each private vertex degree three. Distinct gadgets share at most one backbone vertex, distinct external triples are disjoint, and an external triple intersects a gadget in at most one leaf. Thus every pair occurs at most once and no triple has repeated positions, regardless of repeated positions in the source. The all-zero word violates just the exceptional external triple. The explicit registry has $B+18M$ variables and $19M$ clauses, with polynomial bit identifiers. Both directions of the reduction and witness verification are paid polynomial work.

For the fixed-girth variant take a finite inclusion-minimal non-2-colorable 3-uniform hypergraph of Berge girth $>g$, which exists by DO01 (see \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/642829b64fbe938b3b7e02047e7272117f307031}{DO01}). Delete any edge. The three deleted-edge terminals must agree in every legal word, and a legal word with these terminals zero exists by minimality and complement symmetry. Switch by that word to acquire an equality gadget with both constant legal words. Every path between distinct terminals uses at least $g$ hyperedges, else restoring the removed edge gives a cycle of length at most $g$. Use this gadget instead in the same occurrence chain. A cycle within one gadget is already longer than $g$; a cycle using multiple gadgets or external clauses must traverse some gadget between two distinct terminals, as the external triples are disjoint and the chain of gadgets has no incidence cycle outside their interiors. Such a cycle has at least one outside edge, so also has length $>g$. For each fixed $g$ the gadget and its known witness are finite constants and can be fixed by exhaustive search before defining that reduction; no efficient varying-$g$ generator or uniform degree bound independent of $g$ is claimed.

Membership in NP follows by checking the explicit promises (including supplied word and, for a fixed $g$, absence of short Berge cycles) and verifying a candidate assignment. The reduction supplies all witnesses needed for the input promise without solving the original instance.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Consequences for universal progress.}
This is an equivalent sharpened constructive target: a uniform polynomial algorithm for the closed-class separation in Lemma~\ref{lem:bh15-flip} on the promised class would decide arbitrary 3-CNF via Lemma~\ref{lem:bh15-repair}. The anchor, one bad owner, bounded degree and linearity do not establish such a bound. The rank-two root also survives singleton/same-plane and two-owner affine-hull closure: every legal owner has full affine hull and distinct linear triples have disjoint observation planes, hence full joint hull for a pair. Run paid closure before any decision and again after deductions and at stagnation; do not assume other global closure rules stall on the compiler outputs. No guarantee is asserted of minimal UNSAT, arbitrary large girth at degree four, or of simultaneous evasion of DS15, BA15, MS21 and other exact reductions. In particular Fano itself admits PSD rejection. The reduction is not a lower bound on unrestricted computation. DP01 count-box and S06 flow hypotheses are not consequences of a supplied anchor. All source acquisition, signs, guards, dictionary, compilation, reconstruction and verification costs are polynomial for the reduction; on that same serial computation $CM\le(T+1)S$. There is no proven polynomial aggregate cost for the unknown repair search itself. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN.

The executable BH15 checker (53 source controls, 44 SAT/9 UNSAT, 31 small exhaustive base comparisons, 124387 assertions, no discrepancy) checked 2040 actual compiled witness lifts and all 128 equality gadget words. All 21 single-sign Fano neighbors had ten models; all 14 single-budget neighbors had five. Across their 70 supplied words all 420 guarded Fano repair charts with an unequal oriented pair pinned in the bad triple were UNSAT, but local coordinate support closure rejected none and forced no extra coordinate. This is a finite hostile control against the two-closed-set extension shortcut, not a proof of impossibility of stronger affine or PSD inference. The compiled roots also stagnated under coordinate closure. High-girth construction, exhaustive large compiled UNSAT evaluation, strict native portfolio, physical cumulative-memory measurements and formal kernel verification were not executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/215ee73bacfe81230134628df31a2210b20ba0c6}{BH15 report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/cd77cdd83b970034e8febc0f535dbdfa09dce21c}{checker} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ceda301431e0e259cdfe1b34f49cae24d714fc6f}{results}.


\section{Provenance-sensitive DAG credit and bounded-batch obstruction (DP16)}\label{sec:dp16}
This attacks Obligation~\ref{obl:progress} beyond the fixed rank-mass potential: can an arbitrary polynomial credit and canonical sharing rescue the separated-guard fallback of Lemma~\ref{lem:ms21-guards}? We distinguish the affine image of the actual observation dictionary from a semantic SAT-equivalence key (Lemma~\ref{lem:dr15-merge}). All assertions below are restricted to the stated dispatch/keys, not lower bounds for unrestricted SAT.

\begin{lemma}[Exact bounded-batch support under separated relative guards]\label{lem:dp16-batch}
Let a signed NAE 3-uniform registry have Berge girth greater than $L$ (length counted in owners), and choose $2p$ anchor variables at pairwise primal distance greater than $L$. Pair the anchors disjointly. Under any partial assignment of the $p$ relative parity guards, every batch $J$ of at most $L$ owners has full joint ambient observation support $\mathbb F_2^{2|J|}$ and exact accepted observation support equal to the product of its three-word local acceptance sets. In particular its satisfying preimage has full affine hull in the guarded affine chart. Thus even exact affine closure from such batches, repeated to stagnation, makes no deduction and cannot reject any such chart.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The incidence subgraph of $J$ is a forest. On each connected component with $j$ owners it has $2j+1$ variables, and the span of the two pair-difference rows per owner is the whole even-weight subspace on those variables (the pair graph is connected). Its rank is $2j$, so the batch observation rows are independent. Each component has primal diameter at most $|J|$ and hence contains at most one anchor. The guard rowspace $H$ is generated by disjoint pair rows; every nonzero combination has a nonempty set of anchors as support. No such combination lies in the batch rowspace, which is zero on unused coordinates and has even weight on each component. Hence $H$ and the batch rows have independent combined ranks. Every batch observation word extends to the actual affine guard, regardless of its right-hand side. Taking the product of the three accepted words at each owner gives the exact batch support. Three distinct words of $\mathbb F_2^2$ affinely span the plane, so the product spans the batch image; its complete preimage spans the guarded chart because it contains full fibers of the surjective affine map. The claimed closure therefore stagnates initially and after each guarded split. This does not model arbitrary nonlinear learning or iterative consistency on overlapping larger auxiliary relations.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exponential distinct authenticated observation cosets]\label{lem:dp16-credit}
For a connected source registry with observation matrix $M$ stacking the two actual labelled pair-difference rows per owner, the $3^p$ partial guards of Lemma~\ref{lem:dp16-batch} have mutually distinct affine images $M\{x:Hx=b\}$. In particular, all $2^p$ fully guarded images are distinct, even after arbitrary invertible affine reparameterization of the free source coordinates and quotienting the global complement. Consider a dispatch that, on an UNSAT source, branches on the next unassigned selected guard (any adaptive ordering), retains these authenticated image keys and does not prune any chart before all $p$ guards are assigned. Every exact OR-branch evaluator of this prescribed dispatch must visit at least $2^p$ distinct leaves. No potential satisfying Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate} can certify it when $p/\log N$ is unbounded, even if the potential depends on the entire state and not just rank mass.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Connectedness gives $\ker M=\operatorname{span}(\mathbf 1)$, so each relative guard $a_j$ is a linear functional $\ell_j$ on $\operatorname{im}M$, obtained by Gaussian elimination of $M^\top$. Disjoint supports make the $a_j$ independent; the map $y\mapsto(\ell_j(y))_{j=1}^p$ on $\operatorname{im}M$ is surjective. The affine image for a partial guard is exactly the inverse image of its corresponding subcube in $\mathbb F_2^p$. Distinct partial guards give distinct subcubes and hence distinct images. This remains true in any free-coordinate basis; the actual observation labels are not permuted. The images can be keyed by a canonical row basis and reduced coset offset, in polynomial bit work per acquired key, without semantic equivalence testing.
On the specified UNSAT dispatch both child answers are necessary unless an extra pruning rule is invoked. Each complete valuation follows exactly one path under the adaptive order. Distinct complete valuations give distinct final keys, so even maximal exact-image memoization cannot share these leaves. Iterating the aggregate credit inequality charges at least one credit for each leaf, hence $\mu(s_0)^c\ge 2^p$, contradicting any fixed polynomial upper bound when $p/\log N$ is unbounded. More generally any globally allocated numerical reserve paying at least one operation per distinct materialized key is at least $2^p$. This argument allows a different certified terminal, stronger pruning, a semantic equivalence proof, or a nonlocal factored evaluator to bypass this dispatch; it does not require them to acquire these keys.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Simultaneous domain and a growing-batch variant.}
For every fixed $L$, take the MS21 family with incidence girth exceeding both $50$ and $2L$ and increase the original primal anchor separation to $\max(24,L)$. Its greedy packing still gives $p=\Omega(n_c)$ and all MS21 filters still stagnate; Lemma~\ref{lem:dp16-batch} adds even exact $L$-owner affine closure. Thus no polynomial potential, not only the DA15 rank mass, pays for this particular no-other-pruning dispatch. Separately, on the DO09 bounded-degree logarithmic-girth family (without importing the degree-eight PSD certificate), choose $L=\lfloor\epsilon\log_2 n\rfloor$ for sufficiently small fixed $\epsilon>0$. Greedy packing uses balls of size at most $(2D+1)^{L+1}$ for variable degree bound $D$, yielding $p=\Omega(\sqrt n)$ and therefore $2^{\Omega(\sqrt n)}$ distinct complete keys despite growing-batch stagnation. This is a statement about the exact batch operation, not a claim that enumerating all $\binom mL$ batches is polynomial. Bounded-degree explicit source size is polynomial in $n$; high-girth existence and minimality are existential, not a polynomial hard-instance generator. I21 supplies the separate native adapter; these cosets refer to its reverse chart.

\begin{lemma}[Local-summary merging can change the decision label]\label{lem:dp16-unsound}
For any fixed $L$, there are connected signed NAE sources and two consistent rank-$p$ guard assignments with identical batch supports in Lemma~\ref{lem:dp16-batch}, identical guarded dimension and owner rank mass, but opposite SAT labels. Thus a universal merge key based only on those summaries is unsound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Take an inclusion-minimal UNSAT NAE registry of Berge girth greater than $L$ (the existence argument in Lemma~\ref{lem:ms21-guards} suffices) and delete one owner $abc$. Every model of the remaining gadget has the omitted signed literal triple monochromatic, since otherwise it models the original source. For the positive DO09 family this enforces $a=b=c$; in the signed case flip the three coordinate gauges so the removed owner has positive signs, adjusting all other occurrence signs. The gadget has a model for both common terminal values by global complement. The terminals lie in the same connected component: otherwise complementing one component would violate the forced equality. Discard components without terminals if any. Chain copies by identifying one terminal of a copy with another of its neighbor, using no other shared coordinate. Every terminal along the chain has the same bit, and either bit extends independently to every copy. Gluing at cut vertices preserves the girth, and anchors chosen at path terminals with at least $L+1$ gadgets between them are at pairwise primal distance greater than $L$. Use $2p$ such anchors; disjoint relative guards are independent as affine rows. All-zero guard RHS is satisfiable by the glued model; any nonzero RHS is impossible by terminal equality. For all full guards, Lemma~\ref{lem:dp16-batch} gives identical local accepted and ambient observation supports, unchanged rank two for each owner, and identical dimension. The opposite labels refute this proposed merge. The chain is easy by exact gadget decomposition (and for a fixed gadget this is a polynomial terminal); it is deliberately not a simultaneous hard family.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Paid constructive repair and remaining universal target.}
The preceding chain can be eliminated without branch expansion: compute each fixed gadget's exact two-terminal equality relation, propagate along the shared terminals, combine all guard equations by Gaussian elimination, reconstruct each gadget using its stored witnessed lift, and verify source. This is polynomial in the explicit chain length for fixed gadget; a growing gadget requires its own paid support algorithm. DP15 sparse projections and GD21 exact decomposition can therefore bypass the hostile summary-key example. For the strict MS21 cores no analogous universal decomposition is established. A new required progress rule must avoid the exponentially many authenticated guard cosets without merging on inadequate local summaries, e.g. via a proved nonlocal identity or decision-preserving quotient with an aggregate bound. The ordinary CNF maximum-deficiency FPT result of Szeider (\href{https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcss.2004.04.009}{JCSS 2004}) is a useful lead for DS15 signed-basis compatibility, but its clause-variable deficiency is not the rank-Hall deficiency of these affine packets; I21's ordinary CNF adapter does not supply a parameter-preserving reduction. No such transfer is assumed.

Executed DP16 finite checks: positive Fano zero models, 21 sign neighbors ten each; the six-owner Fano-minus-line gadget has ten models, all terminal-equal, and both common bits. Chains of 13, 37 and 61 variables, with $p=1,2,3$, respectively, give $3,9,27$ different canonical partial coset keys, $2,4,8$ full keys, and $2/1,4/5,8/19$ SAT/UNSAT partial labels by the certified gadget projection and gluing. The checker executes all singleton and pair support scans before/after each represented guard, 55,638 batches, 3,232,544 local assignment evaluations and 111,346 assertions, no disagreements. It uses exhaustive local enumeration, not full large-chain enumeration or a general solver. No large high-girth UNSAT generation, full portfolio, physical peak/CM instrumentation or kernel build. Per-key Gaussian work, guard acquisition, key lookup and all consumer edges must be charged; serial polynomial storage for one job does not pay an exponential number of jobs. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Exponential signed-basis candidates at deficiency one, and exact triple closure (RT21)}\label{sec:rt21}
The DS15 residual lemma reduces the remaining constructive problem to signed independent owner-row transversals. We test whether deficiency one, even with no one- or two-owner repair direction and after the BA15 quadratic closure, bounds the number of distinct maximum-transversal candidate words. It does not. The same explicit construction has a cheap exact nonlocal repair, which must remain available to the portfolio.

\begin{lemma}[A deficiency-preserving four-owner replacement]\label{lem:rt21-gadget}
Write a rank-two forbidden packet as $(a x,b x)=s$, where $a,b$ are independent binary rows and $s\in\mathbb F_2^2$. Introduce three private bits $p\in\mathbb F_2^3$. Label their eight points by binary integers and partition them into four affine lines
\[
 L_0=\{0,1\},\quad L_1=\{4,6\},\quad
 L_2=\{3,7\},\quad L_3=\{2,5\}.
\]
Their directions are respectively $1,2,4,7$. Let $A_0p=(p_1,p_2)$, where $p_0$ is the least significant bit. Replace the old packet by the four rank-two forbidden packets
\[
 A_0p+(a x,b x)=s,\qquad p\in L_j\quad(1\le j\le3).
\]
The new gadget has exactly two private lifts for every allowed old word, and zero for a forbidden word. It increases owner count and observation rank by three, and preserves the strict deficiency-one condition of Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual} when applied to any owner of a strict deficiency-one registry. It also preserves inclusion-minimal UNSAT.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The three unshifted lines forbid exactly the complement of $L_0=\ker A_0$. On $L_0$ the shifted packet is precisely the old forbidden event. Thus every legal old word permits both points of $L_0$ and a forbidden old word permits neither. The four packets have private rowspaces of dimension two with distinct one-dimensional kernels. Any two of these private rowspaces span all three private directions. The three unshifted packets span the private space; subtracting private rows from the shifted packet recovers $a,b$. Thus the complete gadget spans the old observation plane plus the three private coordinates.

More generally replace any subset of owners in one stage, with disjoint private blocks. For a subregistry of new owners, let $J$ be the old owners retained unsplit or having all four descendants included. For each partially included gadget let $t\in\{1,2,3\}$ be its number of selected packets. Its private projection has rank at least $t$. Distinct private blocks are independent, and the fully included gadgets also generate the original planes of $J$ on the old chart. With $f$ fully included gadgets, the rank of this subregistry is therefore at least $R(J)+3f+\sum t$. A proper new subregistry has $J$ proper, hence $R(J)\ge |J|$ by strict deficiency one. Its rank is at least its owner count; the full new registry has rank exactly owner count minus one. This proves the strict condition.

For minimal UNSAT, deleting an unsplit owner uses an old deletion witness and legal private lifts everywhere else. Deleting a particular descendant of a split owner uses an old word violating only that owner. At such an input the four forbidden private lines partition the entire cube, so either of the two points of the deleted line violates only that descendant. Other gadgets have legal lifts. Thus all single deletions are SAT while the full parent remains UNSAT.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exponential candidate count after certified pair and polar closure]\label{lem:rt21-candidates}
Start with the positive Fano NAE registry on seven triples, quotient its complement symmetry by fixing one reference coordinate, and recursively replace \emph{every} owner by Lemma~\ref{lem:rt21-gadget}. At depth $k$ the root has
\[
 m_k=7\cdot4^k,\qquad r_k=m_k-1,\qquad
 G_k=(m_k-7)/3
\]
owners, rank and cumulative gadgets respectively. Each root is inclusion-minimal UNSAT. Every change of one forbidden two-bit word to one of its other three values is a SAT neighbor on the identical observation planes. At every unguarded root and such neighbor, singleton, same-plane and all two-owner exact affine-hull closure, BA15 constant-coefficient quadratic-polar closure, and DS15 rank-Hall peeling are stagnant. No nonzero observation direction touches fewer than three owners. At least $2^{G_k}$ distinct actual chart words, even with a single specified violated leaf, occur as candidates induced by maximum signed independent transversals on the UNSAT root.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The Fano root has rank six and strict deficiency one by Lemma~\ref{lem:ds15-residual}. It is minimal UNSAT: choosing ones exactly on the vertices of any designated line yields a word violating just that line, since every other line meets it once. Every nonconstant direction partitions its seven vertices into two nonempty classes, with at least six crossing pairs. Each crossing triple accounts for exactly two such pairs, and each pair occurs in a unique triple, so at least three planes are visible. The reference quotient removes exactly the invisible complement direction. Lemma~\ref{lem:rt21-gadget} now gives the rank, criticality and counts of gadgets by induction. A deletion witness for any final owner also satisfies that owner after any different forbidden value is substituted.

For $k\ge1$, packets from distinct last-stage gadgets have independent rowspaces since their projections to their respective private blocks are injective and disjoint. Inside a gadget, the shifted packet has two independent old rows and thus is independent of each unshifted packet. Two unshifted packets have union rank three and forbid two lines of distinct directions; if they are disjoint, their complement is four points but is not an affine plane, since two nonparallel lines cannot partition an affine plane. If their signs have changed so that they intersect, the complement has five points and again has full affine hull. This proves two-owner stagnation under any single sign change, as well as singleton and same-plane stagnation. At depth zero the same conclusion follows from the independent Fano planes. For polar closure, the shifted packet of each gadget has a nonzero old-private mixed polar block which occurs in no other packet of that stage. Its coefficient in a vanishing linear combination must be zero. The three remaining private polar matrices are independent: in dimension three the map from alternating two-form coordinates to the kernel-direction vector is a linear isomorphism on the coefficient space, and the three directions $2,4,7$ are independent. Signs do not alter the polar part. The depth-zero assertion is BA15's unique-pair argument. Strict deficiency makes the complete registry its DS15 core.

If an old direction is visible at an old owner, at least one of its new owners is visible: invisibility to all unshifted packets forces the private direction to zero, after which the shifted observation is the old one. Hence an old nonzero direction touches at least three new owners by induction. If the old part is zero, a nonzero private direction touches at least three of its four packets because their kernels are distinct. This argument permits arbitrary combinations of old and private changes.

For the candidate bound, fix a base deletion witness and a final leaf descending from its violated owner. At each stage choose either point of the legal line $L_0$ at every satisfied owner, and either point of the line corresponding to the designated violated child at the unique violated owner. These are two independent choices per gadget, and each resulting word violates exactly the designated final owner. Thus there are at least $2^{G_k}$ distinct near-models. On any such word, the good opposite rows of each of the $m_k-1$ other owners span that owner's plane, as proved in DS15. Strict deficiency gives the Rado conditions for these owners, so an independent good-row transversal of size $m_k-1=r_k$ exists. Its opposite equations determine the word uniquely. Hence enumerating and even perfectly deduplicating all actual maximum-transversal candidate words can still cost exponentially many occurrences at deficiency one. This is an output-size barrier to that specific exhaustive policy, not a lower bound against finding a SAT word, excluding all candidates symbolically, or changing representation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform paid triple-hull repair for the expansion]\label{lem:rt21-repair}
The preceding family, including all single-sign neighbors, is decidable by a polynomial-time procedure without being given the expansion hierarchy. In particular for the UNSAT roots and the neighbors obtained by changing a shifted descendant all the way down from a base owner, exact affine-hull closure on batches of at most three owners contracts the entire expansion to an observation chart of rank at most six (or decides earlier).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For each subset of at most three current rank-at-most-two packets, acquire its actual joint observation basis by Gaussian elimination; enumerate at most $2^6$ joint states. Compute the exact affine hull of the locally allowed states, detect an empty support, or impose one certified necessary affine row if the hull is proper. Substitute the row globally, remove inactive packets and reapply singleton closure after every deduction, then restart the pair/triple scans at stagnation. All deductions are unconditional on the current chart; any input guard would be retained. At most the current chart dimension many independent rows can be added, and inactive packet deletion never increases the owner count. All scans, unsuccessful tests, verification, Gaussian substitution, stored row/guard and lift records, and reverse solving cost polynomial bit time and space in the explicit original chart length. There is no branching or semantic hull oracle. The same serial execution satisfies $CM\le(T+1)S$ under step-boundary accounting.

For the original three unshifted lines their joint allowed support is exactly $L_0$, an affine line, so the local scan discovers its two equations. Under any previously acquired affine chart, this joint allowed support is still an affine set (possibly empty); deleted inactive members are tautologies on that chart. If any of the three packets is nontrivial and their joint relation is consistent, their relation must have a proper local affine hull: otherwise the affine relation would be the entire chart and each packet inactive. Thus at stagnation their constraints are all discharged and the private observations $A_0p=0$ are entailed by the chart. The surviving shifted packet is then precisely the old packet under the chart. Induction upwards through the expansion (not a requirement to discover or store the hierarchy) gives the assertion for unaltered gadgets and shifts propagated down from the base. The final nonlinear observations have rank at most six; enumerate those at most 64 joint words, lift by Gaussian back-substitution, and verify the original input.

For a single sign change in an unshifted descendant, only its ancestor path can contain an altered gadget; all other last-stage gadgets contract as before. At every level, outside this path all three unshifted descendants are unaltered after exact contraction, and their private coordinates can be eliminated. Keep the at most one exceptional gadget per level, all six base coordinates and at most three private coordinates per exceptional gadget. No path recognition is needed: apply the same triple scan to all current packets, including those in unaltered subtrees. At stagnation, by the preceding induction all private observations outside the exceptional ancestor path that can participate in those subtrees are fixed to their corresponding lines. at most the old base rows plus the private observations of the exceptional path can appear in nonlinear packets. All other private rows are fixed to $L_0$ modulo the chart. Their rank is at most $6+3k=O(\log m_k)$, so direct enumeration on the acquired live observation basis remains polynomial. The single altered leaf can also make an early contradiction, which is sound. No claim that arbitrary charts pass this terminal follows.
\end{proof}
For an explicit credited macro with the four-owner pattern authenticated, contraction removes six units of rank mass and has an exact two-choice private lift; no guessed basis candidate or hidden branching is needed. The generic triple scan is instead a polynomial preprocessing (each strict row reduces free dimension; each inactive deletion reduces owner count), followed here by a recognized low-rank terminal. It is not claimed that raw rank mass falls after every single intermediate affine row. Thus this is both a direct falsifier of a proposed deficiency-only exhaustive bound in DS15 and a precise repair for the planted obstruction. The missing universal U-PROGRESS is now the discovery of an actual signed-basis consistency shortcut or another exact contraction on strict cores \emph{without} such cheaply acquired support identities. A complete semantic key or complete hull query is not free by DR15. For strict physical transfer one can explicitly convert the affine row computations to XOR circuits, then to 3-CNF with auxiliary variables, and invoke I21. This preserves decision labels with polynomial size but does not assert that reverse normalization retains the same rank-Hall or pair-closure invariants on the resulting native chart.

Executed RT21 checker: all 16 gadget external/sign fibers, 24 I21 local B4 and clause-split controls; 14 exhaustive single-replacement Fano/SAT-neighbor charts, 14,449 Hall subset checks, 7,217 direction checks, 14 polar checks, 84 maximum-transversal candidate checks and generic pair/triple closure. At depth one the two 28-owner charts contract from rank 27 to rank six using 14 forced rows; depth two/three (112/448 owners) only rank and sampled lifts were executed. Overall 22,037 assertions, 518,272 local observation words, 7,296 reference words, 13,358 closure scans, 56 forced rows, no discrepancy. Full native compilation, general exceptional-path execution, whole portfolio, formal kernel and measured cumulative memory were not executed. P versus NP remains OPEN.


\section{Cooperative circulation repair: exact progress, missing acquisition (S21)}\label{sec:s21}
This section sharpens U-PROGRESS using the cooperative circulation proposal in issue \#327 (comment 7154153606), the BH15 single-budget hardness theorem (Lemma~\ref{lem:bh15-repair}), I21 source adapters and DP16 aggregate-credit audit. The proposal has an actual polynomial aggregate potential, but its acquisition premise cannot be inferred from integral flow or residual reachability.

\begin{lemma}[Conditional monotone synchronization on two circulations]\label{lem:s21-cycle}
Let two explicit directed integral circulation networks have fixed arcs and other arcs of capacity-interval width one. Their projections $X,Y\subseteq\{0,1\}^L$ use actual, separately indexed unit arcs, one per synchronized coordinate on each side (fixed shifts or bit complements may be decoded). Given $x\in X,y\in Y$ with feasible flow witnesses and $x\ne y$, if a common word $z$ exists, a nonempty batch of residual cycles on the two sides can be flipped simultaneously to preserve every current agreement and strictly decrease the number of disagreements. The batch is computable in polynomial time given actual flow witnesses for $z$ on both sides, not from the promise alone.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Subtract each current circulation from a target circulation for $z$. The oriented difference on width-one arcs is a balanced integral residual subgraph with each changed arc used once, so decompose it into arc-disjoint directed cycles by successive walks and removal. On an agreed projected coordinate either both sides change or neither; when both change, link their unique cycles in an auxiliary undirected graph. On a mismatched coordinate exactly one side changes, since the decoded target bit is common. Choose any connected component containing a cycle changing such a coordinate, and augment every cycle in that component once. Arc disjointness preserves all bounds and conservation. Links ensure an agreed coordinate changes on both sides or neither. Every changed mismatched coordinate becomes agreed; at least one does. Computing the difference, cycle decomposition, graph, connected component, augmented flows and verifying all original bounds uses polynomial bit work in the explicit graphs and dictionary. This proof is existential without the unknown target flows; it does not supply a residual-cycle search algorithm.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[One-mismatch repair is NP-complete for general synchronized unit-width circulations]\label{lem:s21-hard}
For explicit networks as in Lemma~\ref{lem:s21-cycle}, given individually feasible initial flows whose projected words disagree on exactly one coordinate, deciding whether there exists a monotone repair is NP-complete. This remains true with each first-side connected component a constant-size signed ternary clause circulation and each second-side connected component a variable-copy cycle. A single-coordinate support scan on either side forces no synchronized bit.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Apply BH15 to obtain a signed ternary NAE formula with distinct occurrence positions and an explicit near-model $w$ violating exactly one clause. Register one bit per literal occurrence, storing the underlying variable value (before the sign). On side $X$, for each clause take two vertices $u,v$ and three independently indexed occurrence arcs of bounds $[0,1]$, from $u$ to $v$ for positive occurrences and in reverse for negative occurrences. If the number of negative occurrences is $k$, add a fixed arc $u\to v$ of value $k$ and a return arc $v\to u$ of bounds $[1,2]$. Conservation says that the return value is exactly $k+\sum_{\rm positive}x_i-\sum_{\rm negative}x_i$, the number of true literals. Thus precisely the legal NAE words lift, uniquely on the return arc. Parallel arcs are allowed; if desired subdivide them with private degree-two vertices, preserving capacities and values. On side $Y$, group the occurrence coordinates by underlying variable; a directed cycle through those labelled arcs forces all values equal. For a variable with one occurrence, add a second auxiliary arc to make a two-arc directed cycle. Both constant assignments on each variable cycle are feasible.

Take the $Y$ flow from $w$. On $X$ retain the occurrence word of $w$ except at one occurrence of its unique violated clause, and flip that occurrence; the formerly monochromatic signed word becomes legal, and all other clauses remain legal. The initial flows are explicit and disagree on exactly one actual coordinate. A strict monotone repair must bring the disagreement count to zero, hence yield a common word. Conversely, a common word yields a cycle-batch repair by Lemma~\ref{lem:s21-cycle}. By the exact two-sided decoder, a common word exists iff the BH15 source is satisfiable. The graph sizes, signs, fixed arcs, dictionary, initial witnesses and reconstruction are polynomial in source length; the reduction does not solve the source. Membership in NP follows by verifying a pair of final flows with equal projected words; equivalence to a cycle batch follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:s21-cycle}. Each NAE clause separately admits either value at every occurrence (by global complementation), and each variable cycle admits both constants, so marginal single-coordinate support closure is stagnant. This is not a claim that stronger cooperative closure stalls. Nor does this reduction preserve the special four-partition geometry of the next paragraph.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Exact aggregate closure criterion for the actual four-partition spine.}
Darmann, D\"ocker and Dorn, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03395v3}{arXiv:2412.03395v3}, prove NP-completeness of positive linear NAE-3SAT with four supplied clause partitions. Pair the first two and the last two partitions. For each pair form a bipartite directed graph with one variable arc of bounds $[0,1]$ from its first-partition clause to its second-partition clause, and a hub with arcs of bounds $[1,2]$ into the first clauses and out of the second clauses. Integral circulation is exactly satisfaction of the pair; the hub balance follows by summing clause balances. Feasible initial circulations can be found by standard lower-bound circulation reduction to max flow; if either network infeasible, reject. Synchronize the two actual variable-arc dictionaries.

The missing acquisition statement is now precise: a fixed uniform procedure, with a fixed polynomial bit-time cap in the original input length, returns a verified monotone cycle-batch improvement whenever the current two flows disagree and the two original networks have a common word. It may return failure on negative instances. The cap must hold even for inputs outside that success promise (enforce it by simulation if a bound is proved only on the promise). This is an \emph{OPEN obligation}, not an oracle or an assumed consequence of reachability. It is sufficient for P=NP: apply paid unconditional closure first, after each deduction and at stagnation before invoking repair, preserving any actual guards; use the repair on the original authenticated flow registry, not on an altered class without a proved adapter. Check each returned pair for bounds, conservation, dictionary, unchanged prior agreements and strictly smaller Hamming disagreement. If a call times out, fails or returns an invalid improvement, reject (on SAT inputs this cannot happen by the proposed guarantee). At most $L$ successful calls occur, and at zero disagreement directly verify the source. For correctness, all current feasible pairs on a SAT source satisfy the promise regardless of prior successful choices. For UNSAT, no common pair can pass the final verification. The strictly decreasing potential $1+\operatorname{dist}(x,y)$, unlike the raw rank-mass branch potential, gives a polynomial aggregate number of calls with no guard tree or semantic merge; all failed calls and their verification are charged. Conversely, P=NP would yield this search by standard NP witness search for a common flow pair followed by Lemma~\ref{lem:s21-cycle}. Thus the proposed acquisition guarantee on the genuine four-partition class is equivalent to P=NP; a single-query NP-hardness result on that narrower class is not proved by Lemma~\ref{lem:s21-hard}.

\paragraph{Ledger and limits.}
For explicit source size $N$, the compilation and initial flow acquisition are polynomial. Local certified marginal closure is paid by checking both pins per actual coordinate via flow feasibility; if any is removed restart, at most $2L$ value removals. Other deductions must be separately justified and charged; one may use this bounded closure portfolio without requiring full semantic forced-information closure. No branch is used in the conditional repair algorithm. With a hypothesized per-call cap $p(N)$, total time is at most $L p(N)+\operatorname{poly}(N)$ (including repeated polynomial closure and checks), and peak space polynomial on the same sequential realization; $CM\le(T+1)S$. Without the hypothesis the repair search and its aggregate time remain OPEN. Existence of a suitable cycle component given a common target, and polynomial time for each ordinary flow instance, do not establish efficient joint acquisition. No unrestricted lower bound follows from this reduction; DISJ sanity is preserved by both-label reduction, deterministic configuration and simultaneous DTISP are conditional only for the unknown solver, and no nonrelativizing or circuit lower-bound claim is made.

Executed S21 finite controls: 102 near-models (68 SAT, 34 UNSAT), including Fano and all 21 single-sign variants and 80 seeded BH15 base selector constructions; 9,872 local flow rows, 60,672 exhaustive joint words, 79,488 assertions. The checker verifies the local balance formula, both marginal bit supports, one-mismatch initial projections, and both-label equality to the source, including six selector lifts per source model. It does not implement a polynomial repair search, a general max-flow backend, actual cycle-batch extraction, strict native replay, a complete cooperative closure portfolio, formal kernel checking or measured CM. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ab057271e1106f13482e8d7e679c7969bad46f33}{checker} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/96802c12424b3f0f55a39be3c8cc059a3857fd18}{results}. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Certified criticality as an NP-complete decision domain (DO16)}\label{sec:do16}
DO09 constructs existential critical high-girth cores, BH15 (Lemma~\ref{lem:bh15-repair}) supplies one near-model, and S21 isolates the acquisition cost of repair. We strengthen the diagonal quantifiers of Lemma~\ref{lem:diagonal}: one can uniformly supply a near-model for \emph{every} owner and still retain arbitrary decision complexity. This is a reduction to a precisely defined promise, not a lower bound on any particular portfolio or a proof of U-PROGRESS.

\begin{lemma}[Certified critical-or-SAT normal form]\label{lem:do16-critical}
There is a uniform polynomial-time reduction from 3-CNF to signed NAE-3SAT with three distinct variables per owner, accompanied by a table of actual assignments, one for each owner, violating that owner and no other. The output is satisfiable iff the source is satisfiable. In particular every UNSAT output is inclusion-minimal UNSAT, and every one-owner sign change is SAT. Decision on this certified domain is NP-complete. The supplied table is polynomial in the original input length; no SAT oracle is used to acquire it.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Normalize the source clauses $C_i=\bigvee_{a=1}^{k_i}\ell_{ia}$ ($0\le k_i\le3$), deleting tautologies and repeated literals. For no remaining clauses output any single nontrivial NAE triple with an explicit violating word. Otherwise let $m$ be the clause count and introduce $m$ fresh selector bits $s_i$. Set $D_i=\bigvee_{j\ne i}s_j$. Construct the following CNF:
\[
 C_i\vee D_i,\qquad
 \neg s_i\vee D_i\vee\ell_{i1}\vee\cdots\vee
 \ell_{i,a-1}\vee\neg\ell_{ia}\quad(1\le a\le k_i),\qquad
 \neg s_i\vee\neg s_j\quad(i<j).
\]
At selector weight zero only the original clauses are active. At weight one, say $s_i=1$, the first clause is false if all literals of $C_i$ are false; otherwise the clause for the first true literal is false. Exactly one clause is violated at this selector word for every source assignment. At weight at least two some pair clause is violated. Therefore satisfying words have all selectors zero and project to exactly the original models.

For the first clause in group $i$, take selector $e_i$ and make every literal of $C_i$ false. For its clause indexed by $a$, use selector $e_i$, set the preceding literals false and the $a$th true. Distinct source variables in the clause ensure consistency. For a pair clause $(i,j)$ use selector $e_i+e_j$ and any source word; all group clauses then have true $D$ or false antecedent, and exactly that pair clause fails. This explicitly constructs a deletion witness for every clause, even when the source is SAT. There are $O(m^2)$ clauses and $O(m^2)$ literal occurrences, including the long group clauses.

Convert each long clause $(l_1,\dots,l_k)$ to a private chain $(l_1,l_2,z_1),(\neg z_1,l_3,z_2),\dots,(\neg z_{k-3},l_{k-1},l_k)$. For a true parent clause set $z_j=1$ iff its first $j+1$ literals are false; this satisfies all children. For a false parent clause and deletion of child $h$, numbered from zero, set $z_j=1$ iff $j\le h$, $1\le j\le k-3$; exactly child $h$ fails. For a clause of length $k<3$ use $3-k$ fresh bits and all their $2^{3-k}$ sign combinations as padding. When the parent is false, each desired child can be uniquely falsified by setting the padding bits to falsify its literals. These transformations are equisatisfiable for each fixed old word, preserve every supplied deletion witness by the stated local lifts, and produce distinct-variable triples. All private blocks are disjoint.

Finally introduce a fresh common reference bit $r$ and replace each 3-clause $(a,b,c)$ by $\operatorname{NAE}(a,b,z)$ and $\operatorname{NAE}(\neg z,c,r)$ with a fresh $z$. With $r=0$ the pair has an extension iff $a\vee b\vee c$ is true: if $a=b=0$, $z=1$ is necessary and $c=1$ then suffices; if $a=b=1$, $z=0$ suffices; if $a\ne b$, $z=0$ suffices. Global complement normalizes any satisfying word to $r=0$. When the parent is false, $z=0$ violates only the first child and $z=1$ violates only the second. Thus the deletion-witness table lifts without an oracle. Signed NAE rejects exactly one relative two-bit word, so changing any one forbidden word accepts that owner's deletion witness. Every output owner has six allowed words on three distinct bits and hence full singleton affine hull; singleton unconditional closure at the unguarded root is stagnant. No assertion of stagnation of stronger rules is made.

All output sizes including the explicit table (at most $O((n+m^2)m^2)$ Boolean entries before identifiers), compilation, witness construction, verification and inverse dictionary are polynomial in the encoded source length. The promise table can be checked by evaluating every owner on every row and membership in NP follows by verifying a satisfying assignment. A solver may use the table as authenticated side information, not as an UNSAT certificate. Local closure is charged; if stronger deductions change the promise one must either preserve the original authenticated chart or prove applicability on the new chart.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fixed-girth linear refinement of the certified promise]\label{lem:do16-girth}
For every fixed integer $g\ge2$, the same certified domain remains NP-complete with a linear signed NAE hypergraph of Berge girth greater than $g$. No fixed maximum-degree bound is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use a fixed critical non-2-colorable 3-uniform hypergraph of girth greater than $g$, available by DO01/DO09. Delete one edge and select two of its vertices as terminals. Every legal word has the two terminals equal; by complement both equal values are possible. Choose an inclusion-minimal subset of the remaining edges enforcing equality of these two terminals. This subset is satisfiable, and removing any one of its edges allows unequal terminals in both orientations by complement. Since the gadget is a finite constant for fixed $g$, precompute these witnesses exhaustively once as part of the reduction, not per input. Every path between its two terminals has at least $g$ hyperedges, since restoring the original deleted edge would otherwise make a cycle of length at most $g$.

Replace every occurrence of a variable in Lemma~\ref{lem:do16-critical} by a separate leaf, linked to its original variable hub by an independent copy of this two-terminal equality gadget. Internal vertices are private and external NAE triples are disjoint. Legal assignments project exactly to the old source and lift using equal-terminal gadget witnesses. For an external owner deletion, lift its old near-model through all equalities. For deletion of any edge inside an occurrence gadget, start from the old near-model for the external owner of that occurrence. Flip only that occurrence leaf; its formerly monochromatic external triple is now legal. Use the precomputed unequal-terminal witness for the deleted gadget edge and equal-terminal witnesses everywhere else. This produces a word violating exactly the deleted edge in the full output (a word satisfying that edge would contradict equality). Distinct external triples and gadgets meet only in individual terminals; any Berge cycle outside one gadget traverses some gadget between its two terminals and at least one additional edge.
