Complete the new prefix by Lemma~\ref{lem:ds16-complete}, extending the current word by the fixed auxiliary bits to a word $y$. The color-$q$ clause containing $v$ is a filler with two opposite auxiliary bits. Flip just $v$ in $y$ to produce $x$. All clauses of colors $(p,q)$ are legal in $x$ (the newly activated $e$ is repaired and its unique partner at $v$ has two opposite bits). All clauses of the remaining two colors are legal in $y$. Relabel colors to the desired fixed pairing if needed. S21's flow compiler gives explicit integral flow witnesses for these two words with exactly one actual disagreement. Every query is in the genuine geometry. By Lemma~\ref{lem:s21-cycle}, a common model gives a strict monotone batch endpoint, and at one disagreement any strict improvement is a common word.

Run the hypothesized procedure at most once per activation requiring repair; verify both side flow bounds and conservation, actual common bits and every original completed clause. On failure, timeout or invalid output the completed prefix must be UNSAT by the guarantee; the partial prefix and final source are then UNSAT by exact completion and monotone constraint addition. Otherwise restrict the witness to partial variables and continue. At the end the source projection from Lemma~\ref{lem:ds16-slack} gives an actual verified satisfying word. This is a polynomial adaptive witness-acquisition reduction, not a many-one reduction to the decision version, and does not assume fixed seed or fixed graph across queries. Under P=NP standard witness search for the joint NAE instance gives the converse, followed by the explicit S21 flow lift.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Aggregate ledger and exact scope.}
There are at most $m'$ prefix activations where $m'$ is the polynomial-size partial registry. The potential $1+$remaining owners decreases at every activation; only one child is carried forward and no branch occurrences are hidden. Each completion has at most $57n'$ vertices, where $n'$ is the size after slack creation. The unguarded paired charts each have a verified feasible word and its complement, proving full marginal coordinate support; each rank-two NAE owner has full singleton affine hull. These certified closure scans are paid initially, after accepted changes and at stagnation before invoking search. No semantic closure oracle or unrecorded guard is used. Stronger joint deductions may be applied only with paid certificates and preserved provenance. Given the hypothesized fixed per-call cap $p$, total time is bounded by $m'p(\operatorname{poly}(N))+\operatorname{poly}(N)$, including discovery, source compilation, closure, unsuccessful calls, verification, composition, access and reconstruction; all explicit intermediate sizes, same-run peak $S$ and $CM\le(T+1)S$ are polynomial. The unknown acquisition cap is exactly the remaining issue. The theorem does not establish a superpolynomial lower bound, a fixed-graph or guarded promise result, a many-one hardness result for single-mismatch decision, or a solution of P versus NP. It rules out treating one disagreement as an independently established easy universal search parameter. DISJ both labels are preserved, and unrestricted complexity barriers and DTISP/configuration remain unresolved.

\paragraph{Executed common-source controls.}
Using the M22 plane and BS15 ring templates with identity source maps, the checker verified all 512 plane words, the 12 $G$ models, perfectness, linearity, filler compatibility, one-mismatch input pairs, actual per-color quota and hub balance, complement support and all recovered witnesses. M22 plane (9 original vertices) rejected at partial prefix 33 after 23 one-mismatch calls; maximum completed size 2010. BS15 $q=1,2$ (36 and 72 original vertices) accepted after 41 and 62 calls; maximum completed sizes 3549 and 5601. The 10,744,203 assertions include 3275 reference DPLL nodes and 5672 propagation scans. Reference DPLL solves only the partial registry, then the exact fixed filler lift verifies the entire completed chart; it is not a polynomial repair routine. Source $x_0$ pin witnesses checked on SAT outputs via complement, not a pinned acquisition theorem. No complete portfolio, native replay, kernel or measured physical CM executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a5a56d0fd5a81b7befe42fa4b2ac6e839c59ad30}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/48558486b65d453a37d983c72279ecf9c382c05b}{code}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/317c703907f30b37c94b1c44dfb2ea4a6c333046}{results} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/b8b05805b96a959c2af27d37af54ed1a3586ae1d}{handoff}.

\paragraph{Joint stagnation protocol.}
Use the same M22/BS15 authenticated source IDs and DS16 reproducible prefix/completion/seed/pairing maps (empty guard; actual source pins separate). Algebra/Hub/Multisign: joint quadratic and non-PC certificates on prefix with paid lifts to completed chart. Matching/Interfaces: remove transparent fillers using exact interface provenance, acquire one joint move. Wheels: native exact adapter for these gadgets, not assumed. Guarded DAG: preserve prefix sharing and actual guard scopes. Potential/Obstruction/Red Team: audit the adaptive search reduction and all failed call and consumer costs. Solver/Synthesis: combine accepted deductions, repeat certified closure, compare standalone and joint operations on the same jobs. Requested peer work not claimed executed. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN; no public release.


\section{Correlation-to-parity repair and selector-cycle separation (MS22)}\label{sec:ms22}
We attack the actual positive-promise acquisition in S21, using M22's genuine four-partition flow geometry, GD22's witnessed ordinary-block seams, and the separate guarded MS21 strict cores. This is a decision/witness attempt, not a flat support representation. DA16/BW16/BD16 fixed-degree barriers and I22's raw-width obstruction do not themselves exclude the following spectral acquisition.

\begin{lemma}[Robust local correlation repair]\label{lem:ms22-round}
Let $G$ be a supplied rational PSD unit-diagonal source-spin matrix. For each signed NAE owner choose a pair $e=uv$ of minimum error $\delta_e=(1-t_eG_{uv})/2$, where $t_e=-\eta_u\eta_v$ is its desired opposite-literal parity. Include protected pair guards with their actual parity and require zero error on them. A single absolute pin can be realized by global complement; alternatively use an actual reference spin and exact pin edges. If the chosen signed graph is consistent, parity propagation produces an actual source model respecting these guards. In particular it is consistent if $mE<1$, where $E=\sum_{i=1}^m\min_{e\in i}\delta_e$. With all signed triangle inequalities imposed on $G$, the stronger sufficient condition is $E<1$. These statements concern pair guards (and reference pins), not arbitrary higher-arity affine guards.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Consistency assigns a spin per component and propagates all edge parities; every owner then has opposite literals. Verify source and guards. A contradictory simple cycle has product of desired parities $-1$. Transport unit Gram vectors along the prescribed signs; the starting vector is transported to its negative. The triangle inequality for Euclidean norm gives $2\le2\sum_{e\in C}\sqrt{\delta_e}$. Guard edges have zero error and at most $m$ selected owner edges occur on a simple cycle, so Cauchy gives $1\le m\sum_{e\in C}\delta_e\le mE$. For the stronger bound the signed triangle inequalities imply
\[
\delta_{uv}(t)+\delta_{vw}(r)\ge\delta_{uw}(tr),\qquad
\delta_{uv}(t)=(1-tG_{uv})/2 .
\]
Successive composition along the cycle gives $\sum_{e\in C}\delta_e\ge\delta_{uu}(-1)=1$. Thus $E<1$ excludes contradiction.
\end{proof}
If exact clause moment sum equals $-1$, the signed three-by-three Gram block has correlations $a,b,c$ with $a+b+c=-1$. Its determinant is $2(1+a)(1+b)(1+c)=16\prod_{e\in i}\delta_e$. Thus singularity of every local block suffices (global rank is irrelevant), since then $E=0$. A SAT input always has an exact rank-one matrix of error zero. The new sharpened \emph{OPEN} S21 acquisition target is a uniform polynomial-bit algorithm which returns a verified PSD/triangle matrix with $E<1$ on every positive input, within a fixed polynomial cap also on negative inputs. It would construct a common word and the S21 cycle-batch lift gives strict monotone progress; the existing S21 aggregate bound then applies. Conversely P=NP supplies a rank-one matrix by witness search. Minimizing $E$ is concave minimization (sum of minima of affine forms), not ordinary convex SDP optimization. No such acquisition bound is proved. Checking a supplied matrix and error, and testing parity consistency even without the threshold, is polynomial; failure to round is not an UNSAT certificate. Selected witness parities are not globally necessary equations.

\begin{lemma}[Exact integral selector system and polynomial fractional cycle separation]\label{lem:ms22-cycles}
Introduce one selector $z_e$ per owner pair, with $\sum_{e\in i}z_e=1$, $z_e\in\{0,1\}$, and fixed guard edges selected at one. For every parity-inconsistent simple cycle $C$ impose
\[
\sum_{e\in C}(1-z_e)\ge1 .
\]
Integral feasibility is equivalent to source feasibility under the recorded pair guards. For fractional selectors in $[0,1]$ all these cycle inequalities admit deterministic polynomial-bit separation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A legal word chooses one actual opposite-literal edge per owner. Such edges and guards cannot form an inconsistent cycle. Conversely the inequalities forbid any inconsistent cycle in the selected graph, so parity propagation gives a legal word. To separate fractional vectors, assign nonnegative length $1-z_e$ to each edge and form the two-sheet graph with transitions $(u,b)$ to $(v,b\oplus [t_e=-1])$ and their reverses. Find shortest paths from $(u,0)$ to $(u,1)$ for every $u$. A contradictory closed walk of length less than one decomposes into simple closed walks, at least one with negative sign product; nonnegative lengths imply that cycle also violates the inequality. Return its actual edge identities. Conversely every violating cycle supplies such a path. Exact rational shortest paths and reconstruction have polynomial bit cost. Parallel edges are distinguished by owner and guard provenance.
\end{proof}

For a common relaxation link $z_e\le1-\delta_e$ to the PSD moment constraints. In an actual local NAE word exactly two pairs are unequal; choosing between them uniformly gives $z_e=(1-\delta_e)/2$ under a local distribution. On the \emph{same} MS21 strict UNSAT cores of Lemma~\ref{lem:ms21-guards}, including every stated separated relative guard, use its exact $G'$ and uniform six local words. Each actual owner edge has $\delta_e=1/3$, $z_e=1/3$; each guard has $z=1$. Every local option and selector has positive mass in the local distribution. Guard edges form a matching, and no owner pair joins two anchors; linearity excludes parallel owner pairs. Every contradictory simple cycle has at least two actual owner edges, hence length at least $4/3$. All selector-cycle inequalities hold simultaneously with the specified PSD/triangle, local convex/lattice and rank-Hall relaxation. Thus polynomial separation of all these cuts does not make the relaxation complete. This is a restricted feasibility/full-positive-support obstruction, not a claim that arbitrary selector pin probes, higher-order rules, or all algorithms stagnate. It is not a four-partition hardness transfer, and does not identify MS21 with DA16's degree-hard family.

\paragraph{Executed common controls and strict counterexample.}
Use the exact M22 plane on $3i+j$, the four BS15 partitions and original one-disagreement words, exceptional block zero, and the actual root or $x_0$ pins. Local coordinate closure is run first and forces no further coordinate. Plane has 102/51 feasible side words, zero common words (512-word reference), and joint PSD contradiction $\mathbf1^\top G\mathbf1=9-24=-15$; uniform fractional selectors pass cycle separation (minimum contradictory length two). On BS15 $q=1,2,4,8$, the unconditioned mixture of the two near-models fails greedy parity repair (not a guard-compatible moment certificate for pinned runs). Build exact legal Gram mixtures using the GD22 six ordinary-block words and periodic exceptional block, used as supplied test witnesses, not acquired by our algorithm. All 12 heavily weighted mixtures satisfy $mE<1$ and reconstruct valid words; the 12 uniform mixtures also round, despite full-rank local blocks and failure of the sufficient threshold for $q>1$. Of 216 seeded nine-model mixtures, 157 round successfully and 59 decline with a parity conflict.

More sharply, on the actual $q=2$ ring, assign GD22's six ordinary-block words in their listed order weights $(1,2,1,3,3,1)/11$. The Gram matrix is a convex combination of six verified whole-source models (same exceptional block). The independently minimum-error selected edges contain the odd cycle
\[
45,48,52,49,46,43,40,61,60,45.
\]
Every edge on this cycle is a \emph{unique} minimum for its actual owner; errors along it sum to $13/11$. Thus no tie-breaking can repair this particular independent selection rule. All fractional selector cuts nonetheless pass (minimum contradictory length two with $z_e=(1-\delta_e)/2$). Complement transports the control to the other seed pin. This is not merely an SDP integrality gap: independently best local edges can be incompatible even in the convex hull of actual global SAT moments. GD22's exact seam bypass remains valid.

Executed 834 primary and 705 supplementary assertions, 645 deterministic weight trials to the strict counterexample. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/af3db80de39dd7b5afae06ca361c15577278950e}{primary checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/da3b6eb497aac2252339a8fd560a974d25bdfedd}{results}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/460d399e099a81404d36eb00be1c0aea28e84217}{strict-cycle checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/75dcc7cbb7fa3d34b83b23ef530f523c5ca6cea7}{results}. No general SDP/LP optimizer, actual flow backend, complete closure or native physical compiler was executed in this iteration.

\paragraph{Aggregate ledger and parallel obligation.}
Polynomial-bit rational matrix verification (including exact PSD elimination), all triangle and source equations, parity checks, cycle separation, source/flow lift and guarded certificate verification are paid. Failed attempts, guard and source map retention, DAG accesses and witness reconstruction are charged; on the same serial execution $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is polynomial per supplied polynomial-size certificate, not a bound on acquiring a low-error matrix or on repeated conflict-learning rounds. No physical quotient decrease is claimed without an authenticated native lift. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN. Literature context: Deshpande et al., \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.05230}{arXiv:1804.05230}, establishes the separate random-regular SDP refutation gap; no result here assumes a polynomial algorithm for concave optimization.

Coordinate existing peers on the same BS15 ring and plane, actual guard/source maps: Algebra/Hub seek low-error acquisition or non-PC identities; Matching/Interfaces supply exact occupancy/circulation cuts; Wheels supply native lifts; GD/BUILD Multisign DAG use certified conflicting selector cycles as guarded errors and charge factored consumers; Obstruction/Red Team/Potential audit the fractional gap and iteration counts; Solver/Synthesis integrate each accepted deduction into both sides, renew paid closure and compare standalone versus combined on identical inputs. Separately use MS21 guarded cores and DA16 degree-hard sources without assuming coincidence. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/13420b3f88b26a210740095e8f44fd839c71e2ea}{MS22 report} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/18fe1310687cfd9216ea7f8d4245a48a35419703}{handoff}. No unexecuted peer contributions claimed; no public release.



\section{Planar algebraic contraction and a crossover obstruction (BA16)}\label{sec:ba16}
The S21/DS16 one-mismatch acquisition problem remains the load-bearing instance of U-PROGRESS. BW14's locally planar wheel replacements and M21's disjoint wheels do not control planarity of their synchronized global source. We investigate whether a joint matching/algebra terminal can use planar topology plus a low-rank exceptional syndrome, and whether local crossover replacement could supply that topology universally. The classical unguarded planar NAE decision result is Moret (1988), \href{https://doi.org/10.1145/49097.49099}{DOI}. The extension below uses the signed perfect-matching polynomial and its cofacial identities (Cai--Gorenstein, \href{https://doi.org/10.4086/toc.2014.v010a007}{Matchgates Revisited}, Theorem 2.1, equations 2.1--2.6).

\begin{lemma}[Planar signed energy with logarithmic exceptional syndrome]\label{lem:ba16-planar}
Let an explicit signed ternary NAE registry have $n$ variables and $m$ owners. Replace each owner by its three labelled pair interactions, counting multiplicity. Suppose a set of exceptional interactions $E_1$ is supplied or acquired within a fixed polynomial cap, whose removal leaves a planar multigraph $E_0$. Let $W$ be the binary span of the incidence parity rows $e_u+e_v$ of $E_1$ and the rows of a recorded actual affine guard $Gx=b$. If $h=\dim W\le C\log_2(N+2)$ for a fixed $C$, exact guarded decision, counting, witness acquisition and individual arbitrary affine parity probes are uniformly polynomial-bit procedures. The exception set and embedding are verified, not assumed discoverable universally. The unguarded result does not require any bound on vertex degree.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
With actual signed literal bits $x_u+\eta_{iu}$, let $d_e(x)$ indicate equality of the two literals at an owner pair. The energy $L(x)=\sum_e d_e(x)$ equals $m+2B(x)$ where $B$ is the number of violated owners. Thus the coefficient of $t^m$ in $Z(t)=\sum_{Gx=b}t^{L(x)}$ is the exact guarded model count. Every pair factor is $t^{d_e(x)}=A+B_e\chi_{e_u+e_v}(x)$ with $A=(1+t)/2$, $B_e=\pm(t-1)/2$, and $\chi_a(x)=(-1)^{a^\top x}$. The sign is determined by actual literal signs. Loops give constants. At any positive integer evaluation point $t$, expand the product of exceptional factors and guard indicators $(1+(-1)^{b_j}\chi_{G_j})/2$ as $\sum_{a\in W}c_a\chi_a$. Gaussian coordinates in $W$ give an array of $2^h$ coefficients updated by two-term convolutions in $O((|E_1|+\operatorname{rows}(G))2^h)$ arithmetic operations, including zero/duplicate rows. Then
\[
 Z(t)=\sum_{a\in W}c_a Z_{0,a}(t),\qquad
 Z_{0,a}(t)=\sum_x \chi_a(x)\prod_{e\in E_0}t^{d_e(x)}.
\]
Expansion of planar factors and spin summation give
\[
 Z_{0,a}(t)=2^n A^{|E_0|}\!
 \sum_{\substack{S\subseteq E_0\\ \partial S=a}}
 \prod_{e\in S}(B_e/A).
\]
Here $\partial S$ is the actual mod-two odd-incidence vector. It is not an enumeration of $S$: replace each planar vertex by a disk gadget with one port per edge and signature equal to one iff the selected ports have parity $a_v$. A ternary even-parity gadget is a planar $K_4$ with outer port triangle (outer edge weights 
/3$, central spokes 
$); the odd-parity gadget is the port triangle with edge weights 
$. Their signatures count perfect matchings after deleting selected ports. Binary even parity is a single unit edge; binary odd is a length-two unit path; unary even is one unit edge with one port, unary odd is one isolated port; nullary parity is constant. Larger arity splits into a planar chain of ternary parity gates in cyclic port order, with a unique internal parity word for each allowed external word. Join matching ports across each original edge by a new edge of weight $B_e/A$ (internal chain connectors have weight one). A perfect matching uses precisely the selected connectors, and the internal weighted sums equal the parity signatures. The resulting planar graph has polynomial size and rational bit weights. Its exact signed matching sum is computable via a planar Pfaffian orientation; determine the overall sign using positive weights on the same nonzero edge support (or a supplied matching), not an absolute value of the signed Pfaffian. Disconnected and isolated vertices are handled componentwise. This construction charges all graph assembly and matching computation. With at most $3m+1$ distinct positive integer $t$ evaluations, rational interpolation recovers the coefficient; all denominators, powers, coefficient arrays and determinant/Pfaffian arithmetic have polynomial bit length by the polynomial graph size and bounded evaluation points (exact fraction-free or rational minor-based arithmetic). One extra parity probe adds at most one to $h$.

For reconstruction do not accumulate absolute pins. Choose a nonloop planar edge, test its two relative parity values by substitution $x_u=x_v+\epsilon$ and contraction of that edge. This is a planar multigraph minor; transform exceptional and guard rows by the same affine substitution, whose linear image cannot increase their joint rank. Retain all objective constants, signs, parallel labelled edges and the original target coefficient. The counting test selects a positive fiber. Repeat at most $n$ times. If no planar edges remain, the whole remaining objective and guard depend only on the at most $h$ observations (plus constants); enumerate their words, solve the affine system and choose a valid one, then lift through the recorded contractions and check the original source. This also handles disconnected components and absolute pins represented by guard rows. No affine basis change is allowed to silently destroy the certified embedding. With BD14's witnessed affine-hull method, an anchor plus at most $n$ independent necessary-row or independent witnessed-difference parity queries yields complete affine hull on the same eligible input; newly certified necessary rows can remain recorded consequences rather than enlarging the guard used in subsequent equivalent count queries. Run ordinary certified closure first, after each accepted deduction and before any dispatch/decline; use closure consequences only with actual provenance. A conservative total bound is $2^{h+1}\operatorname{poly}(N)$, including all witness and closure probes, verification, reconstruction, discovery within the supplied cap and every consumer. Peak space is polynomial on the log-rank domain, and on the same serial run $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is a paid terminal, not a universal proof that one can acquire $E_1$ with the required rank.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[No cofacial exact crossover, even support-only]\label{lem:ba16-cross}
Consider any unpinned signed ternary NAE network with planar incidence graph embedded in a disk and four distinct actual boundary terminals in cyclic order 
,2,3,4$. Let $f(x_1,x_2,x_3,x_4)$ count private extensions and put $a_j=f(0,\operatorname{bin}_3(j))$, $0\le j<8$. Then
\[
 a_0a_5+a_3a_6=a_1a_4+a_2a_7.
\]
In particular no such network, of any size or auxiliary degree, has support exactly $x_1=x_3,\ x_2=x_4$ (nor its fixed signed translate).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Global complement gives $f(x)=f(x+\mathbf1)$. The local signed NAE Fourier factor is $(3-\sum_{u<v}(-1)^{\eta_u+\eta_v}\chi_{e_u+e_v})/4$. Ignore the common 
/4$ per owner. In incidence coordinates its signature has value 3 at zero, signed unit values at pair selections and zero at odd selections. Realize it by a planar $K_4$: the central spoke to port $i$ has weight equal to the pair value for the other two ports; each opposite triangle edge has reciprocal weight, so the undeleted graph has matching sum 3 and the two-deleted cases give the required signed values. Variable parity gadgets are those of Lemma~\ref{lem:ba16-planar}, with an extra external port for each boundary character. All incidence connectors have weight one. The unnormalized Walsh transform $H_y=\sum_x f(x)(-1)^{y^\top x}$ is a common scalar multiple of the resulting planar matchgate signature, by summing each original spin. The four external ports retain their cyclic order. Theorem 2.1 of Cai--Gorenstein applied to complementary odd strings 
000,0111$ gives
\[
H_{0000}H_{1111}-H_{1100}H_{0011}
+H_{1010}H_{0101}-H_{1001}H_{0110}=0.
\]
For even $y$, $H_y=2\sum_{j=0}^7 a_j(-1)^{(y_2,y_3,y_4)\cdot\operatorname{bin}_3(j)}$. Substitution gives $32(a_0a_5+a_3a_6-a_1a_4-a_2a_7)=0$. An exact crossover has only $a_0,a_5>0$, regardless of multiplicities of extensions, impossible. Fixed literal gauge changes on terminals preserve planarity and recover this same obstruction for signed crossings. No claim is made about an additional nonplanar affine guard, unary field, arbitrary B4/E4 native predicates, or nonlocal decoding.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exponential flat planar cover and raw exception-rank obstruction]\label{lem:ba16-cover}
For $k$ disjoint alternating four-terminal crossing blocks on one disk boundary, an exact union of projected unpinned signed planar NAE supports, each contained in the independent crossing relation, requires at least $2^k$ nonempty pieces, even with arbitrary private variables and differing planar graphs. Separately, for every genuine positive linear four-perfect-partition source on $n\ge3$ vertices, any removal of pair interactions making the unchanged primal graph planar has exceptional parity rank at least $(n+6)/8$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Marginalize all other boundary coordinates of any nonempty piece. The four chosen ports remain in cyclic order, the projected counts are nonnegative and complement-symmetric, and the matchgate identity holds. Since every projected word obeys crossing, either $a_0=0$ or $a_5=0$. Thus each piece fixes the relative bit of the two independent wires at every block, and contains at most $2^k$ of the $4^k$ target words. Counting gives the lower bound. This is a flat support-cover bound, not a decision or factored-sharing bound; a factorized equality registry is trivial to solve. Arbitrary nonlocal guards can encode crossings themselves; their low-rank cover complexity remains an open question.

For the second assertion, every vertex occurs four times and every pair occurs at most once, so the primal graph is simple 8-regular with $4n$ edges. A planar simple subgraph has at most $3n-6$ edges. Thus $|E_1|\ge n+6$. The binary incidence rank of $E_1$ is $\sum_C(|C|-1)$ over nonisolated connected components. Each has at most $4|C|\le8(|C|-1)$ edges, giving $|E_1|\le8\operatorname{rank}(E_1)$. An absolute seed pin retained as a guard does not change the raw edge graph or this bound. Consequently no raw exception-rank choice can dispatch Lemma~\ref{lem:ba16-planar} with a fixed logarithmic cap on the entire growing S21 class. The argument does not apply after a genuinely nonlocal source rewrite; in particular the known GD22 seam bypass solves BS15. It is not an unrestricted runtime lower bound.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Executed common residuals and collaborative next obligation.}
The exact integer Walsh coefficient checker (not a Pfaffian implementation) ran ordinary singleton coordinate closure at root and either actual seed pin, compared every character and single parity guard on the Fano and M22 plane roots, all 21 Fano sign neighbors, seven full owner deletions and DS16's plane-minus-two-lines gadget, and checked 64 signed planar two-owner fans and 81 positive crossing multiplicity choices. Fano/plane zero models, sign neighbors ten each, 14 one-word budget neighbors five each (obtained by filtering the deleted-owner support, not a complement-symmetric terminal). DS16 gadget on actual ports $0,1,3,4$ has counts six each at 
01,1010$ only: one relative pattern, so it does not supply two independent crossing wires. Total 59,660 assertions, 289 closure scans, 46,124 Fourier dictionary updates and 20,352 reference word checks. The finite coefficient checker explicitly enumerates a growing Fourier dictionary and is not the polynomial planar evaluator; no FKT backend, arbitrary multirow guard implementation, full portfolio, native replay, kernel or physical CM executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/5c0d3c490e702432b29ef5afc6417be04e85a651}{code} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/99771d017003fb8610f4aa35d05280afcb4c9d5c}{results}.
