\section{Seed-preserving conflict-free cut and aggregate block contraction (BH16)}\label{sec:bh16}
The S21 and DS16 acquisition gap and BH15 near-model normal form motivate applying recent sparse conflict-free-cut algorithms. The critical distinction is the actual seed: a nontrivial unseeded cut need not repair the violated owner.

\begin{lemma}[Exact anchored local-conflict graph]\label{lem:bh16-cut}
Given a signed linear NAE chart with a supplied word $w$ violating only the full NAE owner $\{s,t,u\}$, set $z=x\oplus w$. Each good owner has the BH15 relation $R(a;b,c)$, with $a$ its minority literal and $z_a\in\{z_b,z_c\}$. Make an undirected host edge $ab$ and $ac$ and declare these two edges a conflict pair, retaining owner IDs. Then a repair is equivalent to a conflict-free cut separating at least one pair in $\{s,t,u\}$. For a fixed designated pair, it is also equivalent to choosing one edge from every owner pair so that the designated terminals are disconnected in the selected graph.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Both edges cross a cut exactly when $z_a$ differs from both $z_b,z_c$. A legal cut therefore has at least one noncrossing edge per owner; selecting such edges leaves different-colored terminals disconnected. Conversely color selected components consistently, assigning different colors to the designated terminal components. The bad full NAE owner is legal exactly when its three flip bits are not constant. Global complement permits one orientation of each pair unless other guards are present; actual labels and lift $x=z\oplus w$ remain authenticated. Linearity ensures no repeated host edge and disjoint local conflict pairs. Multiple bad full owners require joint feasibility under a pair demand from each, not independent pair answers; at most $3^b$ demand lists for $b$ bad owners, charged. One-sided owners require separate semantics.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Seeded sparse conflict-cut hardness]\label{lem:bh16-sparse}
Deciding whether a conflict-free cut separates specified terminals $s,t$ is NP-complete even on simple connected hosts with local conflicts forming an exactly one-regular conflict graph and $|E|\le2|V|$. This is a many-one reduction; no maximum host-degree four restriction is asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For an arbitrary signed NAE source introduce complementary representatives $X_i,Y_i$ and two seed bits $s,t$. Relations $R(s;X_i,Y_i)$ and $R(t;X_i,Y_i)$ force the representatives unequal under $s\ne t$. Each source NAE is exactly $R$ on the complement of its first literal representative and its other two literal representatives. Every such constraint accepts zero, and satisfying assignments under the seed guard decode to source models and lift in either seed orientation. Apply BH15's sign-switched six-line Fano equality chains to split all occurrences, retaining the seed hubs. Each gadget accepts zero and synchronizes actual copies, so each resulting good owner is again an $R$ relation, and the registry is linear. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:bh16-cut}'s graph translation to these good owners only. Connect separate components if necessary using a fresh center $a$ and one vertex $b,c$ from each of two components with $R(a;b,c)$; this is freely extensible and preserves linearity. If the resulting graph has $n$ vertices and $m$ owner pairs, append $k\ge\max(1,m-n)$ pendant wedges $R(a;p,q)$, with two private new leaves per wedge, chaining from a previous leaf. Every old assignment extends by $p=q=a$ and every new model restricts to an old one. Now $|E|=2(m+k)\le2(n+2k)=2|V|$. Simplicity, local perfect conflict matching and connectivity are preserved. Construction, witness lift and checking are polynomial. Membership in NP is direct.
\end{proof}
In contrast, the unseeded theorem of Kalyanasundaram and Kumar, \href{https://arxiv.org/html/2611.15846v1}{arXiv:2611.15846v1}, Definition 1 and Theorem 2, asks for any nontrivial conflict-free cut and reports a polynomial algorithm under this density bound. In our reduction a final private leaf can always be separated alone without violating any conflict, even on seeded negative inputs. Thus its use as a repair oracle would lose the seed. Sparse density itself is not a universal progress criterion; no contradiction with the unseeded theorem, no unconditional lower bound, and no growing-girth or degree-four host claim follows.

\begin{lemma}[Exact seeded block OR and aggregate credit]\label{lem:bh16-block}
Let a finite explicit undirected graph have only local conflict pairs (unpaired edges permitted), and no additional constraints except a designated seed separation $s\ne t$. If $s,t$ are in distinct components, a component coloring suffices. Otherwise form the block-cut incidence tree (include bridges as blocks). On the unique $s$-to-$t$ path let $B_i$ be its blocks and $a_i,b_i$ their consecutive entry/exit vertices. The seeded problem is satisfiable iff some $B_i$ has a conflict-free cut separating $a_i,b_i$, retaining only pairs whose two edges belong to $B_i$. Each positive answer lifts in polynomial time. A bridge on the path is immediately positive. For nontrivial block subqueries take $\mu(G)=|E(G)|-1$. Whenever at least two such blocks occur on the path or a single path block omits any parent edges, their aggregate credit satisfies $\sum_i\mu(B_i)\le\mu(G)-1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A valid coloring with $s\ne t$ must differ on consecutive entry/exit vertices of at least one path block. Its restriction satisfies all retained conflicts. Conversely take one local separating coloring. Each component of $G-E(B_i)$ meets $B_i$ in at most one vertex by maximality of the block; extend constantly with that attachment color, and use arbitrary constants on unattached components. Only edges in $B_i$ may cross, so every discarded conflict has a noncrossing edge (including conflicts spanning blocks); the chosen separation transports to $s,t$. A bridge cut crosses only one edge. Distinct blocks are edge-disjoint, hence for $k\ge2$ nontrivial blocks $\sum_i(|E(B_i)|-1)\le |E(G)|-k\le\mu(G)-1$; for one proper block the deleted edges supply the unit decrease. A single full block gives no strict contraction and the rule declines. Graph construction, block discovery, path, conflict IDs, child dictionaries and lifting are polynomial in explicit source size. Apply paid closure on the parent and after accepted deductions and on child charts; guards implied by the original seed problem may be rechecked on lifted witnesses. Arbitrary additional guards do not transfer by this proof; use a separately proved adapter or decline.
\end{proof}
This is an exact polynomially discoverable aggregate normalization under Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate}, not a polynomial algorithm for biconnected seeded cores. The initial at most three seed calls from a bad triple have a constant paid multiplicity, not an assumed rank-mass contraction. All child occurrences, failed block tests, maps, verification, closure and reconstruction are charged; the conditional serial bound on cumulative memory remains $CM\le(T+1)S$. In particular padding from Lemma~\ref{lem:bh16-sparse} is removed by the block rule. A general polynomial solver for biconnected seeded subqueries would solve the full seeded graph language by a polynomial Turing reduction, with a corresponding implication to P=NP. This does not assert a many-one hardness result for biconnected graphs or prove that any prescribed diagonal family remains hard.

\paragraph{Shared executed controls and outstanding acquisition.}
BH16 checker: 3,092 assertions, 620 support-closure scans, 42,752 reference words, 13,892 selector words, 30,180 local exhaustive words, 310 block queries, 12 large-block declines, 39,248 adjacency reads and 76 verified block lifts. Fano actual IDs 012,034,056,135,146,236,245: all 70 restored anchors and 210 designated pair guards UNSAT, zero local closure contradictions. All 21 sign neighbors have ten source models; one near-anchor per source gave 63 SAT pair witnesses. Fourteen relaxed single-word neighbors have five models each (not a direct application of the full-owner cut rule to one-sided owners). Connected pendant padding, up to 135 vertices, preserves both labels and is removed from seed paths. A three-block chain yields a witness. M22 plane (IDs $3i+j$) has an anchor with two bad owners 012 and 678; nine pair-demand combinations reject by finite enumeration. Independently recognizing the complete positive pair graphs $K_7,K_9$ gives joint cut bound $\lfloor n^2/4\rfloor<2m$, certifying Fano and plane after local closure, in agreement with I22 and DA16. BS15 actual rings $q=1,2,4,8$ have bad owner $\{0,12,24\}$ and every designated pair lies in one large block; all twelve calls decline. The explicit BS15 witness and complement were verified, not acquired by the block rule. Local enumeration capped at block size twelve in the checker is not a general terminal; no GD22 seam replay, complete native/portfolio execution, growing-girth generation, physical CM or formal kernel check performed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/5218c59ab4631861a5755733030e59bc0957e5c4}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/652f1f30a4913195254ba4576a86cb7d9d0c1ca5}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/528b3ec7734cae119d520e4c4fb1e1b51992fd1d}{results}.
The next load-bearing target is joint nonlocal acquisition on the same biconnected seed cores, with actual occupancy/syndrome, guards and source maps. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN; no proof claim.


\section{Adaptive two-variable interface credit and biconnected seeded hardness (RT22)}\label{sec:rt22}
BH16's local-conflict translation gives $R(a;b,c)$, meaning $a=b$ or $a=c$, and its block-path normalization declines on one full block. We strengthen the obstruction to that specific criterion and give a complementary adaptive composition rule, not a universal terminal.
\begin{lemma}[Sparse biconnected seeded completion]\label{lem:rt22-completion}
The seeded decision language in Lemma~\ref{lem:bh16-sparse} remains NP-complete for simple biconnected hosts with $|E|\le 2|V|$, local conflicts forming a perfect matching of host edges, linear source triples and minimum source owner-degree two.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Retain the actual source registry and seed pair. Along a cyclic ordering of its $n\ge3$ original vertices, attach to each consecutive pair $u,v$ a private five-variable gadget with owners
\[
R(u;p,q),\quad R(v;r,s'),\quad R(p;r,t'),\quad R(q;s',t').
\]
Every boundary word extends by $p=r=t'=0$, $q=s'=1$; every augmented solution restricts to an original solution. The gadget host contains the cycle $u-p-r-v-s'-q-u$ and ear $p-t'-q$, hence is biconnected. The cycle of attached gadgets stays connected after deleting any original or private vertex; adding original edges preserves this. All new variables occur in two owners, old vertices in at least two added owners, and linearity is preserved because added owners contain at most one old vertex and distinct gadgets use fresh private variables. Repeat gadgets on old pairs until $k\ge\max(n,m-n)$, where $m$ is the original owner count. Then $n'=n+5k$, $m'=m+4k$ and $|E|=2m'\le2n'$. All host edges are distinct and paired exactly once. The extension and its verification are polynomial. Apply BH16 hardness; membership in NP is direct. This is neutral padding which stronger rules may remove, not a lower bound for unrestricted computation or a degree-four physical claim.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Adaptive exact two-variable separator]\label{lem:rt22-separator}
Partition a complement-invariant constant-valid $R$ registry into nonempty owner sets $A,B$ sharing at most two variables, with both seed vertices in $A$ and no independent additional guards. If the interface has two variables $u,v$, one exact seeded decision on $B$ with $u\ne v$ yields its full projected support: all four words on SAT, equality on UNSAT. In the latter case identify $u,v$ in $A$, rejecting if the required seed collapses. In the former retain $A$. Exactly one subsequent seeded decision on the resulting $A'$ suffices for the parent, and a witness lifts. A one-variable or empty interface extends freely by constants.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The two constant assignments always extend and complement exchanges the unequal words. An exact positive $B$ witness, its complement and the two constants supply every allowed boundary lift. In the negative case no unequal boundary word extends, so identification in $A$ is exact. Simplify tautological repeated-variable owners and absorb $R(a;b,b)$ as the necessary equality $a=b$. Apply paid certified closure initially, after deductions and at stagnation; retain the actual source quotient map and seed. The conditional $B$ query is not an unconditional assertion that $u\ne v$; only its exact support summary is imported. Verify the lifted word against all parent owners. UNKNOWN is not UNSAT. Additional absolute or affine guards and multiple seed demands need separate adapters; a single absolute pin can be met by complement only if the seed is relative and no independent orientation is required.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Adaptive occurrence credit]\label{lem:rt22-credit}
With $m$ owners use $\mu=2m-1$ for $m\ge1$ and $\mu=1$ for a zero-owner terminal. The two sequential calls in Lemma~\ref{lem:rt22-separator} satisfy
\[
\mu(B)+\mu(A')\le 2m-2=\mu(\mathrm{parent})-1.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Each side has at least one parent owner, and quotienting cannot increase its owner count. Even if $A'$ is empty its credit is at most that of the original $A$. Hence the displayed bound follows by addition. This adapts the occurrence-tree proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate} to an ordered adaptive continuation: the second child depends on the exact first result but the sum bound holds on every actual execution. There is no duplicate large $A$ call. Scan vertex pairs of the primal graph (clique per owner), compute connected components after deletion and verify ownership and seed locations. All adjacency scans, failed tests, closure, quotient maps, child descriptors, actual seed provenance, retained witnesses and reconstruction are polynomial per occurrence in original $N$. On a recursively covered domain with uniformly polynomial terminals, at most $2m-1$ occurrences are charged; same serial execution has polynomial peak space and $CM\le(T+1)S$. No such coverage is established for arbitrary irreducible seed cores.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Common residual verification and remaining obstruction.}
RT22 independently ran 926 assertions, 2826 singleton closure scans, 140585 reference word visits, 40450 separator pair tests and 40019478 adjacency reads. The neutral gadget has boundary extension counts $(10,9,9,10)$; twelve local comparison cases include both equality (Fano-minus-line) and full-support sides. All 70 actual Fano one-bad anchors give 210 negative designated pair queries, no singleton closure rejection, and 70 alternate positive seed controls. All 21 sign neighbors have ten source models. For anchor $(1,1,1,0,0,0,0)$ the same good registry has negative seed $(0,1)$ and positive seed $(0,3)$, giving opposite labels for arbitrarily many neutral cycle gadgets. At $k=7,14,28,56$ all host articulation deletions, density, degree and positive lifts were verified; negative augmented labels follow by exact projection. M22 plane IDs $3i+j$ have zero models, two minimum violations 012 and 678, and joint cut bound $20<24$. On the actual BH16/BS15 map $q=1,2,4$, all nine seed-pair searches decline: no eligible raw two-variable primal separator, despite verified supplied common witnesses for two pairs per ring. GD22 supplied seam bypass was not reexecuted. Neither a full recursive solver nor complete affine closure, native replay, formal kernel or measured CM was executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/19aa720dccd0836645d00e98b3d11baa1275fa37}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/5e67d3bbc7b6d14973ba4bce45301c852386b4e9}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/1fd4721911450c8afaf6b3a95f8935a5137b376f}{results}. Joint next target is a paid nonlocal normalization of the same seed cores after block and separator decline, with matching occupancy, algebra and guarded DAG collaboration; no universal progress or unconditional COST. U-PROGRESS, SEM/CLOSE and public release remain BLOCK.


\section{Exact joint cut-moment face and its linear-extension obstruction (S22)}\label{sec:s22}
The S21 conditional progress lemma and DS16 genuine-geometry single-mismatch search equivalence isolate target-free common-word acquisition. M22, DA16, I22, MS22 and BH16/RT22 motivate using actual joint owner-pair observations rather than independent flow marginals. The following exact identity narrows the requested acquisition to a single face query but rules out a general compact explicit LP of the full moment hull.
\begin{lemma}[Exact target face on the common pair registry]\label{lem:s22-face}
Let $A,B$ be the two pairs of partitions of a positive linear ternary NAE source, with identical actual variable IDs and guard $G$ (possibly empty). Register every unordered variable pair in every owner in either side. Put $d_{uv}(x)=[x_u\ne x_v]$ and let $P_A,P_B,P_{AB}$ be the convex hulls of the corresponding actual pair vectors for words satisfying respectively $A,B,A\cup B$, all under $G$. For $e=\{u,v,w\}$ define
\[
V_e(d)=1-\tfrac12(d_{uv}+d_{uw}+d_{vw}),\qquad L_B(d)=\sum_{e\in B}V_e(d).
\]
Then $P_A\cap P_B=P_{AB}=P_A\cap\{L_B=0\}$, including empty hulls.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
On Boolean words $V_e$ is exactly the indicator of a monochromatic triple and is nonnegative. Every joint word belongs to both hulls. Every point in $P_A$ with $L_B=0$ is a convex combination of actual $A,G$ words, all of whose positive-weight atoms must have each $V_e=0$ for $e\in B$. Hence those atoms satisfy the entire source and the point belongs to $P_{AB}$. Finally $P_B$ satisfies $L_B=0$, proving the identity.
\end{proof}
When $P_A$ is nonempty its minimum $L_B$ is an integer and is zero exactly for joint SAT. A genuine word at zero is an actual S21 repair when disagreement is one. This is not free optimization or free decomposition of a fractional point. Uniform polynomial zero-face word acquisition on all genuine one-mismatch inputs suffices for P=NP by Theorem~\ref{thm:ds16-one}; ordinary unguarded zero-face decision on the full genuine class is NP-complete by Darmann--D\"ocker--Dorn. No many-one hardness claim for the one-mismatch decision promise follows from DS16.
\begin{lemma}[Exponential linear extension complexity on easy genuine sources]\label{lem:s22-xc}
For each $t\ge2$ there is an explicitly constructible genuine positive linear four-perfect-partition SAT source with $O(t^2)$ vertices and $O(t^2\log(t+2))$ encoded bits, such that each of $P_A,P_B,P_{AB}$ on its full registered owner-pair coordinates (empty guard) has linear extension complexity at least $(3/2)^t$. The source has an explicit polynomial-time joint witness.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For groups $i=0,\ldots,t$, allocate fresh occurrence vertices $u_{ij}$ in group $i$ and $v_{ij}$ in group $j$ for each unordered pair $i<j$, and one fresh dummy $a_{ij}$. Add the disjoint color-zero triples $(u_{ij},v_{ij},a_{ij})$. Connect the $t$ occurrence vertices in each group along a path, adding a fresh dummy to each link to form a triple, alternating colors two and three. Color one is initially empty. Each occurrence is in at most one link of each color; different paths are disjoint, links within a path share at most one vertex, and a link joins distinct color-zero triples. Thus all partial partitions and linearity hold. Add at most two isolated vertices for divisibility by three and complete by Lemma~\ref{lem:ds16-complete}. Its fixed filler lift preserves every partial model and all original pair IDs, in size at most 57 times the partial vertex count. Every group bit vector $\beta$ extends to a joint model by setting occurrence bits to their group bit, each old dummy opposite its first endpoint, and using the fixed filler bits.

For each of the three hulls take the nonempty face on which the sum of cut coordinates on link pairs is zero. Nonnegativity of cut coordinates forces every positive-weight atom on this face to have equal bits along each group path. Its target-pair projection is therefore a complete-graph cut vector on $t+1$ group bits, and every such cut has the explicit joint lift just given. Writing the projected coordinates as $d_{ij}$, with $d_{ii}=0$, the affine map
\[
X_{ij}=(d_{0i}+d_{0j}-d_{ij})/2,\qquad 1\le i,j\le t
\]
gives at each atom $X_{ij}=(\beta_i\oplus\beta_0)(\beta_j\oplus\beta_0)$. All binary vectors of length $t$ arise, so the image of the face is exactly $\mathrm{CORR}(t)$. Face restriction and affine projection cannot increase extension complexity: restrict an extension to the preimage face and compose its projection. By Kaibel--Weltge, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1307.3543}{arXiv:1307.3543}, Theorem 2.1 (2014 revision), $\operatorname{xc}(\mathrm{CORR}(t))\ge(3/2)^t$. This proves the claim for each hull, even though the full joint source is easy.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Scope and charged integration.}
The lower bound concerns explicit linear extended formulations of the full authenticated moment hull, not arbitrary optimization algorithms, PSD, implicit inequalities, nonlinear circuits, factored guards, or the one required zero-face query. It is unconditional and does not imply P$\ne$NP. The family has no unconditional singleton pins by complement, but stronger transformations may remove the registered observations; no irreducibility or one-mismatch promise is claimed for it. A solver should pursue target-specific exact certificates without demanding the full hull. If a uniformly capped zero-face routine gives a common word on every positive one-mismatch query, DS16 requires only polynomially many calls of polynomial encoded size, with verified lifts, closure, failed calls, guard/source checks and all consumers charged, so Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate} applies via its prefix reserve. Unconditional acquisition and COST remain OPEN. No complete semantic closure is presumed.

\paragraph{Executed common-source controls.}
On M22 plane IDs $3i+j$, all three pairings and root / actual $x_0=0,1$ separately, the independent side counts are 102 or 51 and every one of the 36 registered pair coordinates has both values on both sides. Local unit/NAE closure stalls, yet exact exhaustive min-other-owner penalty is two in each direction in all nine cases, agreeing with the I22/BH16 joint cut bound $20<24$. On the actual BS15 rings $q=1,2,4,8$ the root starting side witnesses each have other-side penalty one; the supplied explicit common word and its complement attain zero under each seed pin and root. This is witness verification, not target-free acquisition. Constructed the DS16 completed correlation-face family $t=2,\ldots,7$ and verified every group word and all $2^t$ projected correlation vertices; reverse enumerated the $t=2$ partial face only (208 words). In total 142389 assertions, 48 scoped closure scans, 3072 plane word visits, no new forced pins. No LP/EF solver, native compiler replay, kernel, full cooperative portfolio or measured cumulative memory executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/4df86e848d8d9c122c75546744cc1b9f8cf9610e}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/4ed2831e9707b2de741a6127b23bcfa1fe1de1db}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/6eeca02a751c2685eb77541a673ce556c9044271}{results} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/55a69634c56f10cdb50aa3365e5e0ef56317df6f}{handoff}.

\paragraph{Joint stagnation protocol.}
Keep the actual M22 plane, BS15 ring and DS16 prefix maps, paired witnesses and guards; test the same target-only objective on each. Algebra/hub/multisign: seek paid nonlinear or exact objective-specific identities; matching/interfaces: exploit actual flows and pair IDs without full LP acquisition; wheels: authenticated native lift; guarded DAG: factored zero-face certificates with guard/consumer accounting; obstruction/red team/potential: audit the face reduction, hostile target-only cases and paid reserve; solver/synthesis: integrate verified deductions and reclose, compare marginal, joint cut and supplied-seam costs. Requested peer work is not claimed executed. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST are BLOCK; no public publication.


\section{Adaptive three-variable single-demand credit (DP17)}\label{sec:dp17}
Extend RT22's adaptive two-variable interface without duplicating the larger region. This addresses aggregate contraction, not the still-open DS16/S21 repair acquisition on irreducible sources.

\begin{lemma}[Exact single-demand three-variable replacement]\label{lem:dp17-interface}
A chart consists of constant-valid $R(a;b,c)=(a=b\lor a=c)$ owners, at most one demand (a pair disequality or a three-variable NAE), and no independent additional guards. Equalities may be absorbed with authenticated quotients. Partition its owners, including the demand, into regions $A,B$ with three shared actual variables $U$, and $a,b$ good owners respectively. There is an exact adaptive composition using at most 24 subqueries with at most $b$ good owners each, followed by at most one continuation with at most $a+3$ good owners, all in the same language. The demand can belong to either region. Witness lifts and verification have polynomial bit cost apart from the explicitly charged subqueries.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
All charts are complement invariant. The eight boundary words form four orbits, one constant and three with a unique singleton coordinate. Acquire exact support on $B$, keeping one actual witness per accepted orbit. If $B$ has no demand, constants extend; each nonconstant orbit is one equality identification and one disequality, hence at most three subqueries. If $B$ has a demand, choose an opposite pair from its at most three pairs and one of the two possible orientations of the boundary representative relative to that pair. Identify each boundary coordinate with the corresponding pair member, retaining just the selected pair disequality. This is an exact union over at most six subqueries per orbit. Inconsistent quotients reject; UNKNOWN is not a negative decision. Repeated-coordinate $R(a;b,b)$ forces equality and $R(a;a,c)$ is tautological; repeat paid certified closure after each deduction. All conditional quotient maps are retained.

When $B$ is demand-free, each missing nonconstant orbit is excluded by precisely one $R$ whose first variable is the singleton coordinate, so add at most three owners to $A$ while retaining its demand. If $B$ has the demand, empty support rejects; constant support gives an immediate lift by a constant assignment on $A$. Otherwise all three nonconstant orbits are represented by one NAE, any two by the pair disequality separating those two singleton coordinates, and any one by a pair disequality plus identification of the equal pair. Thus there is only one large continuation. Complement the stored boundary witness to agree with the continuation, glue on the actual shared coordinates, and check every parent owner and the demand. Conversely every parent witness belongs to an acquired orbit, proving completeness. Smaller interfaces may use fresh unconstrained common coordinates or RT22. No independent affine or absolute guards are discarded; this lemma declines them, except that a single absolute pin alone may be oriented by global complement.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform adaptive aggregate credit]\label{lem:dp17-credit}
If $8\le b\le a$, set $m=a+b$, $\mu=m+1$, $c=8$. The preceding rule pays all actual child occurrences:
\[
24(b+1)^8+(a+4)^8\le (m+1)^8-1.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Set $t=(b+1)/(m+1)\le9/17<0.55$. Since $b\ge8$, $(a+4)/(m+1)\le1-t/2$. The convex function $24t^8+(1-t/2)^8$ equals one at zero and is strictly less than one at $t=0.55$ (rational arithmetic). Convexity gives strict inequality for every positive $t$ in the interval. Integral credits give the unit decrement. Fewer calls only improve the bound. Adaptive children obey the same telescoping proof as Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate}, on each actual transcript, charging all calls including failures and repeated small queries, not assuming free memoization.
\end{proof}
Discover the split by scanning sets of at most three variables, deleting them from the primal graph with a clique per actual owner and per demand. Each owner not fully contained in the boundary belongs to one remaining component; boundary-only owners are separate units. A subset-sum dynamic program on explicit good-owner counts up to $m$ (with backpointers) finds a side of size $8\le b\le m/2$, the demand assigned to its actual unit. Discovery, closure, guards, quotient maps, source compilation for the BH16 chart, failed tests, all DAG consumers, exact support acquisition calls, witness reconstruction and verification are charged. Descendants have no more good owners than the parent, and explicit variable maps remain polynomial in original $N$. On recursively covered domains with uniformly polynomial terminals, at most $(m+1)^8$ occurrences are charged, and the same serial execution has polynomial $T,S$ and $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is not a universal guarantee of such coverage or an algorithm for arbitrary E4/B4 charts.

\paragraph{Executed checks and shared stagnation.}
The primary finite reference checker ran 5743 assertions (5329 exact integer credit tests), 1800 reference subqueries, 199602 reference word visits and 3322 repeated-coordinate closure scans. On 120 two-side controls, 44 SAT and 76 UNSAT labels agreed with reference, and all 21 actual Fano single-sign neighbors with transparent three-interface padding accepted while the original rejected. All nonempty complement-orbit relation encodings were checked. On the original BH16 BS15 maps $q=1,2$, actual IDs $3i+j$, anchor $w=i\bmod2$, bad owner $0,12,24$, the new credit gate declines after 276678 total two/three-separator scans across the root NAE (three only) and three pair demands per ring. The supplied common witness was checked, not acquired. Raw Fano is below the credit threshold; M22 plane has two bad owners and is outside the single-demand domain (peer joint-cut certificates remain valid). No $q=4,8$ scan, general recursive solver, native replay, complete semantic closure, physical CM or kernel verification was executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/e99f6a35262516747dcf3f170e601e78fbea6e80}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/6c86c252054fb14e23bbbbbf603a698ec664b087}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/57005787ddb4aa6b5f95d2a91dc417696b7796ab}{results}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/3d081b5cb1da183a69acbc32a3d478ab5021f6b2}{common scan}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/1a41d43b9d7951b525c77c7f0518ebb16c4b24ec}{common results}.

Use these same BH16/DS16 source IDs, relative demands and separate actual source pins. Algebra/Hub/Multisign: non-coordinate interface contraction or target-only zero-face acquisition; Matching/Interfaces: occupancy/syndrome seam beyond primal cuts; Wheels: authenticated native splitter; GD/BUILD DAG: factored support and paid consumers; Obstruction/Red Team: hostile four-interface expressivity and credit; Solver/Synthesis: import the exact $R$ replacement and combine with block, two-cut, joint-cut and supplied-seam rules, reclose after every deduction, compare standalone and combined work on identical jobs. Requests are not executed peer contributions. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN; no publication.


\section{Source-discovered cyclic seam acquisition (BS16)}\label{sec:bs16}
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:bs16-discover}
For every BS15 ring given its four perfect partitions, under arbitrary variable/owner/partition permutations and coordinate pins, GD22's seam plan and a common word (or exact infeasibility) are deterministically obtainable in polynomial bit time without a supplied ordering. The resulting terminal is exact on any ternary truth-table registry with a verified plan and declines otherwise.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Try each omitted partition. In the correct three-partition primal graph, the BS15 incidences $C_{i,j}$ give exactly three common neighbors on a within-layer edge (one within and one in each adjacent layer) and two on a cross-layer edge (one in each endpoint layer); there is no short wraparound since $12q\ge12$. The three-common-neighbor edges thus form exactly the disjoint layer triangles. Verify components and quotient cycle. Unwrap at every possible starting layer, prohibit interior cuts spanned by an omitted-partition owner, and use interval reachability with segment lengths at most twelve. A true block boundary works in either orientation. Check that every original owner is assigned once to a block containing all its variables in its own coordinates or the next block's first layer; this final scope verification guarantees exactness even if the heuristic classifier returns a different plan.
For each block test all 64 ordered three-bit seam pairs with exact local search on at most 39 variables, using certified forbidden-word unit closure initially, after deductions and at stagnation, retaining coordinate pins on actual IDs. Keep a local witness iff feasible. Eight-state cyclic reachability for each initial seam requires return to that seam. Every source model gives a closed path and every path glues to a source model because only adjacent actual seam IDs repeat; verify all source predicates and pins. All failed discovery, scope verification, guarded local calls, table consumers, path copies and reconstruction are charged. There are at most $64n$ local queries with a fixed $2^{40}$ search-node cap, at most $512n$ table accesses, polynomial discovery and path-copy work. A conservative bit bound $O((N+2)^6)$ with fixed large constant, polynomial peak space and $CM\le(T+1)S$ follow on this serial realization. No arbitrary affine guard or universal progress is claimed.
\end{proof}
A common word gives S22 penalty zero and S21 paired integral flows by using its bit on each variable arc, owner sum on each owner arc (one or two), and total ones on the return arc. Conservation is exact; balanced integral differences from the starting flows decompose into directed residual cycles, so the full batch attains disagreement zero. Flow occupancy verification executed, cycle replay not executed.
 
Executed BS16: unit closure then I22/BH16 complete-positive-pair cut, then discovered seam, then fixed $n\le9$ fallback. Six Fano/M22 root and actual $x_0$ pin negatives via joint cut (seam alone declines); 21 sign and 14 budget Fano SAT neighbors by bounded fallback; 24 BS15 ring cases $q=1,2,4,8$, root and both pins, original and permuted IDs, all acquire verified common words (cut alone declines). 11869 assertions, 118303 closure scans, 10897191 pattern tests, 57801 local nodes, 5760 seam queries, 30564 discovery edge tests, 72 starts, 5584 table reads. New planted genuine SAT 36-vertex source with exact witness in JSON defeats combined recognizer, refuting its universal eligibility, not arbitrary SAT computation. Supplemental replay/parity controls timed out without completed result. No general native/portfolio/kernel or measured CM. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/e0440704d03611a222bc93301f99b3b86d0cf427}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/29f055dedc75001f3a0419e9e9ed9e0d61568606}{code}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/3d408f496ef04f94fe38b4a248d2c77b27c8dcc1}{results}. Bodlaender's fixed-width discovery theorem (SIAM J. Comput. 1996) is consistent with this scoped result, not a universal width guarantee. Joint stagnation: use the same planted registry and Fano/M22/BS15 maps and actual pins; request nonlinear identities, joint occupancy, native hub lift, factored DAG and aggregate credit audit, exchange exact deductions and reclose. U-PROGRESS and unconditional COST remain OPEN; public release BLOCK.


\section{Fixed-interface expressive completion and diagonal scope (DO17)}\label{sec:do17}
DP17 and RT22 leave open larger constant interfaces; BH16's single-demand language is used here, not an arbitrary native E4/B4 adapter. We remove the fixed-interface expressivity gap, but not the need to acquire support or discover eligible separators.

\begin{lemma}[Exact fixed-interface compilation]\label{lem:do17-orbit}
Let $k\ge3$ and let $S\subseteq\{0,1\}^k$ be an explicitly acquired nonempty complement-invariant relation on actual boundary coordinates. It is the existential projection of a conjunction of $R(a;b,c)=(a=b\lor a=c)$ owners with at most
\[
H_k=(k-2)(2^{k-1}-1)+2(k-1)
\]
good owners and at most one pair disequality demand, which is needed only if constants are forbidden. Lifts for each supported boundary word are constructive. Empty support is immediate rejection.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A binary tree of $R$ constraints, with parent as head and children as tails, has possible root values exactly the set of its leaf values, inductively using disjoint private subtrees. For each missing nonconstant complement orbit, partition the boundary into its two nonempty value classes $L_0,L_1$. Make the two trees on these sets share their root (the actual leaf itself if one set is a singleton). Their joint extension exists iff their possible root-value sets intersect, i.e., iff the boundary is outside the designated forbidden orbit. This uses exactly $k-2$ good owners. Use disjoint private variables for distinct missing orbits. If the constant orbit is missing, two fresh independent trees each on all boundary leaves, with opposite roots, enforce nonconstancy with $2(k-1)$ owners and one pair demand. Induction also constructs private lifts. Each actual boundary coordinate is preserved. A useful hostile control is four-bit even parity: it allows constants and every balanced orbit, but every nontrivial boundary-only $R$ (including a repeated-tail equality) rejects a balanced orbit. Thus no boundary-only $R$ conjunction or equality quotient realizes it. Four two-owner private trees do.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Adaptive fixed-interface paid contraction]\label{lem:do17-credit}
For fixed $k\ge3$, partition a constant-valid $R$ chart with at most one pair/NAE demand and no independent extra guards into regions $A,B$ with $a,b$ good owners and $k$ actual shared coordinates. Let $Q=6\,2^{k-1}$, $H=H_k$, $m=a+b$, $\mu=m+1$, $c=4(Q+H+2)$. If $H+1\le b\le a$, an exact adaptive replacement uses at most $Q$ small child occurrences of at most $b$ good owners and one continuation of at most $a+H$ good owners, all in the same language. They satisfy
\[
 Q(b+1)^c+(a+H+1)^c\le(m+1)^c-1.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Acquire the smaller-side support by complement orbits. For a demand-free $B$, constants extend; each nonconstant orbit is a quotient identifying its two value classes, with one pair demand. If $B$ carries the demand, choose one opposite pair in it (at most three choices) and one of two orientations of the boundary relative to it. Identify each boundary coordinate with the designated pair member and retain this pair demand. Reject inconsistent quotients. This is an exact union of at most six queries per orbit, preserving quotient witnesses. UNKNOWN never means UNSAT. Store a genuine witness per accepted orbit. If support is empty reject; if $B$ carries the demand and constant support is present, a constant word on $A$ glues immediately. Otherwise compile the support by Lemma~\ref{lem:do17-orbit} and attach it to $A$. If $A$ already carries the demand, $B$ is demand-free and its support needs no new demand. Complement the stored $B$ witness to agree with the actual shared coordinates of the continuation and verify the original chart. Both decision directions follow from exact projection. Repeated-tail equality and tautologies are closed with their authenticated quotient maps, and paid certified closure is renewed initially, after deductions, and at stagnation before branching. No independent cross-block guards are discarded; a single absolute pin alone can be oriented by complement.

Put $t=(b+1)/(m+1)\le2/3$. Since $b-H\ge(b+1)/(H+2)$, the continuation ratio is at most $1-t/(H+2)$. The convex function $f(t)=Qt^c+(1-t/(H+2))^c$ has $f(0)=1$ and $f(2/3)<1$: use $(1-x)^c\le 1/(1+cx)$ for $0<x<1$, giving each endpoint term less than $1/2$ for the chosen $c$. Thus $f(t)<1$ for positive $t\le2/3$, and integer credits give the unit decrement. This is a pre-dispatch gate, not a retrospective budget that ignores failed acquisitions.
\end{proof}
For fixed $k$, discover such splits by scanning actual $k$-subsets and a subset-sum over owner counts of remaining primal components and boundary-only owners, as in DP17. The fixed exponent may be enlarged for any finite collection of such rules. Charge all discovery and failed scans, exact child calls including negative calls, private gadget compilation, closure, maps, DAG accesses, support storage and lifts. On recursively covered charts with uniformly polynomial paid terminals, the occurrence bound $(m+1)^c$ and at most constant fresh variables per gadget give polynomial bit sizes, polynomial $T,S$, and $CM\le(T+1)S$ on one serial realization. This does not grant a general polynomial support solver. The preflight threshold can be conservative; an after-the-fact smaller actual gadget is not a free dispatch certificate. For the diagonal theorem, each fixed $k$ with a uniformly scheduled eligible rule can be excluded eventually, not all $k$ uniformly. No bound for growing $k$ or proof that every residual has such a separator follows; U-PROGRESS and the universal COST/SEM/CLOSE obligations remain open.

\paragraph{Executed common-residual handoff.}
The finite checker tested all 272 complement-invariant supports for $k=3,4$, 60 seeded random supports for $k=5,6$, and 384 quotient checks against exhaustive reference. 13,980 assertions, 4,528 coordinate closure scans, 3,186 finite DPLL nodes, 201,702 local gadget words and 52,480 reference words. On the actual BH16 Fano root and all 21 single-sign neighbors (same source IDs), split off the first two good owners: 5 shared bits, 9 supported orbits and 21 replacement owners, labels and lifts agree (0 and 10 source models); no aggregate-credit claim on these small inputs. The M22 actual plane has zero models; its two-demand anchor is outside this rule. On actual BS15 $q=2$, owner 72 is the bad triple $(0,12,24)$, anchor $\lfloor i/3\rfloor\bmod2$ in layer-major ID $i$, empty guard, seam $(0,1,2,36,37,38)$, $a=47,b=48$. Finite DPLL acquired 25 of 32 orbits; 28 new $R$ owners compiled them; a subsequent finite DPLL call on the continuation and gluing produced a verified source witness. This genuinely combines support acquisition and nonaffine $R$ compilation, but does not pass the worst-case credit preflight and is not a polynomial bound for general acquisition. GD22's supplied seam DP remains a separate bypass; no actual $x_0$ pin, native lift, $q=4,8$, general portfolio, semantic closure, kernel verification or physical CM measured. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/bdf6cdb7b42fca005deaabb33df6c6790838a874}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/faa11602abcd4581b342075a7bc18f97a7d51861}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/e2fb317f33341b133fa3427ce564aafea64c64c8}{results}.
Use exactly these BH16 source IDs and guards for parallel requests: algebra/hub/MS smaller exact orbit gadgets or nonlocal certificates; matching/interfaces actual occupancy and seam acquisition; wheels authenticated native lifts; guarded DAG factored orbit support; potential/red-team preflight and adversarial cuts; solver/synthesis integrate and reclose, comparing charged standalone and joint runs. These are requests, not new executed peer contributions. No publication.


\section{Guarded selector consistency versus flat conflict coverage (GD23)}\label{sec:gd23}
This section attacks the MS22 cycle-conflict acquisition and S21/DS16 target-only repair obligations on the same BS16 planted residual, not a different source representation. The following statements do not close U-PROGRESS.

\begin{lemma}[Exact factored parity consistency]\label{lem:gd23-graph}
Suppose each owner is a disjunction of actual pair-parity equations, with a fixed guard consisting of coordinate pins and pair-parity equations. A complete owner selection is feasible exactly when its selected equations and the guard are jointly consistent. The conditional rejection function on the selection bits has a uniformly constructible Boolean circuit of size $O((n+1)^3+m)$ for bounded choices per owner, where $n$ counts actual variables and $m$ owners. This is a representation statement, not a polynomial selector acquisition or coverage theorem.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A signed NAE owner is the disjunction of its three opposite-literal pair equations; $R(a;b,c)$ is the disjunction of its two equalities. A source model supplies a true choice at every owner; a solution of the selected equations satisfies every owner. Encode pins against a common reference vertex. In the doubled graph an enabled equation $x_u+x_v=b$ inserts the edges $(u,i)(v,i+b)$ for $i\in\mathbb F_2$. The selected system is inconsistent iff $(v,0)$ reaches $(v,1)$ for some vertex $v$: an odd-parity closed walk gives a contradiction; absent one, root bits extend consistently along paths, with the reference component oriented to respect pins. Encode reachability by the standard layered transitive-closure recurrence on doubled vertices, using actual selection predicates on base edges and fixed guard edges. There are $O((n+1)^3)$ recurrence gates and $O(m)$ base choice gates. A path conflict retains every edge-choice premise and every guard premise used. Its conclusion cannot be imported as an unconditional source deduction without discharge of the alternatives. Arbitrary dense affine guards require an additional paid adapter; they are not discarded.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exponential no-auxiliary flat conflict CNF]\label{lem:gd23-flat}
There is a linear-size guarded $R$ chart with $4k$ binary selectors for which any exact CNF over the original selectors alone representing feasible selections has at least $2^k$ clauses. Yet the rejecting predicate has a linear-size factored circuit, and the chart itself is easily satisfiable.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use $k$ serial diamonds between endpoints $v_0,\ldots,v_k$, with two distinct length-two paths per diamond. For each of the four edges $uv$ per diamond add a private dummy $d_e$ and owner $R(u;v,d_e)$. The selector chooses the actual equality $x_u=x_v$ when one and the dummy equality $x_u=x_{d_e}$ when zero. Pin $v_0=0,v_k=1$. Dummy edges end at private leaves; contradiction therefore occurs exactly when selected actual edges connect the endpoints, i.e., each diamond contains a complete selected path. The rejecting function is the conjunction over diamonds of the disjunction of the two path conjunctions. There are $2^k$ inclusion-minimal rejected sets $P$ of selected actual edges, choosing one path per diamond. In any exact CNF for acceptance, some clause is false on the indicator of $P$. Its negative literal set $D$ is contained in $P$ and its positive literal set is disjoint from $P$. The indicator of $D$ also falsifies it, so minimality gives $D=P$. Distinct $P$ require distinct clauses. All-dummy selection is feasible. This rules out compulsory flat exact coverage in this language, not auxiliary-variable encodings, lazy selection of sufficient conflicts, or unrestricted decision algorithms; the example is not claimed to be a genuine strict four-partition source.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Paid closure and guarded search.}
Store a parity forest with the reference vertex. For each actual ternary owner enumerate its eight patterns compatible with the forest and retain its allowed patterns. If none, reject; if a coordinate or pair parity is common to all, add that equation and restart closure. Each deduction is valid in the current actual guard, and each new equation joins two components. When every compatible pattern at every owner is allowed, arbitrary free component bits give a witness, checked against the source and pins. At stagnation branch on an unknown parity or coordinate, with disjoint exhaustive guards. Optional two-sided failed-parity probing runs the same closure on both guarded copies: one certified rejection forces the opposite parity, two reject the chart, and a verified witness settles it. A capped decline is not a rejection. There are at most $n$ strict joins per chain; one naive closure scan costs $O(mn)$ elementary steps with forest traversals, with at most $n+1$ scans per closure call. There are $O(m)$ probe candidates per round and at most $n$ forcing rounds per node. The per-node overhead is polynomial, including copying, unsuccessful probes, guard verification and reconstruction, but aggregate branching remains unbounded by any proved polynomial. Serial depth is at most $n$ with polynomial peak storage, and $CM\le (T+1)S$ on the same realization. This avoids a semantic merging oracle and does not solve the universal cost obligation.

\paragraph{Executed joint comparison.}
On 50 identical jobs (Fano/M22 root and actual $x_0$ pins, all 21 sign and 14 budget Fano neighbors, BS16 planted $n=36$ root and both pins, BS15 $q=1,2$ root and both pins), coordinate-only and parity closure DFS each returned 44 SAT and 6 UNSAT. The supplied planted witness was not an input to the solver. The source and pins were checked on every returned witness; small cases independently exhaustively verified. Full failed-parity probing under a $10^6$ pattern-test cap (checked at scan boundaries, overshoot at most $8m$) returned 35 SAT and 6 UNSAT and declined nine larger cases. Coordinate/parity/probe node totals were 428/498/139 but actual pattern-test totals 260776/224472/9205656. The BS16 planted root cost 9000/12296 pattern checks for coordinate/parity search, while the probe variant declined after 1000256. On $q=2$ the parity search used 45296 pattern tests under each guard, with all three probe calls declining. Thus a lower node count or stronger closure need not give lower aggregate work; a fixed cheap-first schedule is an engineering suggestion, not a proof. The previous cut/seam decline on the planted instance is BS16 peer evidence, not rerun. Diamond enumeration for $k=1,\ldots,4$ verified minimal conflicts 2,4,8,16 and every selection's factored predicate. 71,079 total assertions. No $q=4,8$, native adapter, full portfolio, actual matching cycle replay, formal kernel or physical CM executed. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/e901930852a8e9a1726735544d209ea3346a4bc4}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/91482707fb855c52430ebb46ae9df77b398c76b1}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ccaca24700adc336661e8867460e1ec11241b0e8}{results}.

Joint stagnation: preserve the BS16 immutable planted owner registry and actual guards, BS15 and Fano/M22 maps. Algebra/hub/MS: target-only nonlinear certificates or amortized probe bounds; matching/interfaces: joint occupancy/syndrome deductions in these exact guards; wheels: native map and port audit; BUILD DAG: factored conflict provenance and consumer costs; obstruction/red-team/potential: adversarial neighbors and aggregate scheduling audit; solver/synthesis: combine with BS16 cut/seam, reclose after accepted deductions, compare standalone versus integrated work. Requests only, no unexecuted peer contribution asserted. U-PROGRESS, S21/DS16 target-free acquisition, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN. Public release BLOCK.


\section{Independent-side anchored parity selection and actual cycle replay (DA17)}\label{sec:da17}
S21/DS16 target-free joint acquisition remains the load-bearing missing step. BS16's discovered seam declines its actual planted 36-variable registry. M22's marginal hulls cannot repair it. The following exact reduction allows paired matching witnesses to constrain a single shared algebraic selector registry without requiring an initial common word.
\begin{lemma}[Exact anchored parity selection]\label{lem:da17-select}
For every ternary NAE owner on three distinct coordinates, fix any verified legal literal word of that owner, independently of all other owners. Keep its two opposite literal pairs. A candidate word satisfies the owner if and only if at least one of these two pairs is opposite in the candidate word. Consequently the whole source is the union of affine charts obtained by selecting one of these two relative parity equations per owner. Each chart has polynomial acquisition of consistency and witness via parity union-find, but the number of charts and the cost of discovering a consistent selection are not bounded by this lemma.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The opposite-pair set of any legal ternary word is a two-element subset of the three pairs. Any two such subsets intersect, while a monochromatic word has no opposite pair. Literal signs convert a selected opposite pair into an explicit equation on actual coordinates. Each selected equation suffices for its owner; conversely any legal source word chooses at least one surviving pair in each owner. The resulting system is an ordinary graph of relative Boolean parities. In a genuine positive four-partition source, pair the partitions and acquire one perfect matching in each cubic bipartite side graph (Hall and augmenting paths); its incidence word satisfies each side. These two anchors need not agree. Use the anchor of the actual owning side in the construction. This is uniform and exact on that source class, without a common word as advice.
\end{proof}
With a current authenticated parity chart, an implied pair discharges an owner, an inconsistent pair cannot be chosen, and if exactly one pair remains it is necessary under the current guard. If neither remains reject only that chart. Restart closure initially, after each join, and at stagnation. Speculative choices are guarded; no conclusion from a failed attempt is exported unconditionally. A single actual absolute pin can be handled by global complementation; independent extra guards require a separate adapter. A capped greedy rule chooses an unfulfilled owner and a consistent pair minimizing the product of the sizes of the two merged parity components, with fixed tie order. There are at most $n-1$ strict joins per attempt and $O(nm)$ option scans. All failed attempts and verification are charged. It is a sound SAT-witness acquisition routine, not a complete decision procedure. By the exact equivalence and the genuine four-partition NP-completeness theorem of Darmann, D\"ocker and Dorn (\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03395}{arXiv:2412.03395}), a uniform polynomial guarantee of a consistent selection on every SAT source (with a cap on all inputs) would suffice for P=NP. This guarantee is OPEN, not a consequence of the bounded number of parity joins in an individual attempt.
\paragraph{Executed joint acquisition and replay.}
On BS16's exact hostile registry (48 owners, 36 variables, actual IDs, empty guard and separate actual $x_0$ pins), the checker independently acquired both side anchors, without reading the supplied planted witness during acquisition. With 32 fixed owner-order seeds per run, unrestricted three-pair greedy gives 2 successes per guard, while the three two-anchor pairings give 5, 9, and 4 successes per guard respectively. Seed zero fails in all these SAT runs, disproving automatic progress of that greedy dispatch. This comparison uses different total work across modes and is not a probability estimate. All nine pairing/guard cases acquire common words and then implement S21's actual residual-cycle decomposition on variable and hub arcs, link cycles at previously agreeing variable IDs, and augment a linked component containing a mismatch. Capacity bounds, conservation, actual side owners, guards, unchanged agreements and strict disagreement decrement are verified on each batch. A replay can reach a different common word from its acquired target. The root pairing $01|23$ progresses $12,4,2,1,0$; $02|13$ progresses $14,2,1,0$; $03|12$ progresses $14,1,0$. Across all runs there are 46 successful batches and 260 decomposed cycles. The M22 affine plane has zero models by exhaustive 512-word reference, and all 96 anchored trials decline, not reject. Total: 480 attempts, 13435 closure scans, 1335283 choice tests, 12955 joins, 1056 matching arc tests, 8090 owner verification visits. The fixed capped routine, source read, matching, guarded choices, failed trials, replay, data access and reconstruction all have polynomial bit work and peak space in the original input size, with $CM\le(T+1)S$ on one serial realization; this bound applies to a possibly declining routine, not a universal decision procedure. No native signed adapter, Fano, ring scaling, arbitrary dense guards, full portfolio, physical CM or kernel verification executed in this iteration. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/0a938e9ad3068d918d5cd6731a7df8cf0c6fed9e}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/b5240ff29e6ba61d33666b6687fe4fd724b8286d}{code}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/6812be4580e5785de65b54c7e0d5c63ef126a864}{results}.

\section{Actual one-mismatch circulation plateaus (M23)}\label{sec:m23}
Attack S21/DS16's target-free acquisition directly, using the exact BS16 hostile planted 36-variable four-partition registry, on which BS16's combined recognizer declines. This is not a new source compiler or an unrestricted lower bound.

\begin{lemma}[Unilateral plateau obstruction]\label{lem:m23-unilateral}
Let $A,B$ partition the four perfect partitions of a positive linear triple registry into two pairs. Let $x,y$ satisfy their respective paired networks and a common coordinate guard, with $x\oplus y=e_d$. Call an owner on a side blocking if it contains $d$ as the unique minority of that side's word. If both sides have two blocking owners, no nonidentity unilateral replacement of either word with any feasible word can keep Hamming disagreement at most one.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Holding $y$ fixed, a replacement of $x$ at distance at most one is $y$ or $y\oplus e_j$; apart from the unchanged word it flips $d$ alone or $d$ and exactly one $j\ne d$. Flipping $d$ makes each blocking owner monochromatic, and their remaining coordinate pairs are disjoint by linearity. One additional flip cannot repair both. The argument with $x$ fixed is identical. Pins can only restrict these choices. Thus even exact one-sided Hamming-minimizing circulation with arbitrary optimal tie selection cannot move. Each word also fails the two opposite partitions, so a different pairing cannot simply reuse these words as its feasible inputs.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact capped cooperative plateau acquisition]\label{lem:m23-joint}
In the preceding model, augment the unilateral neighborhood by simultaneous flips of a common unpinned coordinate $j\ne d$ in both words, accepting only after checking every actual owner and pin. The resulting moves preserve distance one or produce a verified common word. If a valid common flip is at a neighbor of $d$ in a blocking owner, that blocker disappears and all other blockers at $d$ are unchanged. A deterministic search retaining at most $n^2$ distinct states finds and lifts any terminal reached within its actual cap, with polynomial bit cost. Failure is UNKNOWN for source SAT, not UNSAT.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Both words change identically on a common coordinate; hence their difference is unchanged. Linearity ensures that a neighbor belongs to at most one owner containing $d$; in a blocking owner flipping a majority bit destroys the monochromatic pair of other bits, removing the blocker. Other incident owners can prevent the flip, so universal availability is not implied. At each state all nonidentity unilateral moves are the candidates of Lemma~\ref{lem:m23-unilateral}'s proof, and there are at most $n-1$ extra common flips. Check each against the actual paired registry and guard; a zero-disagreement word is a source witness. Use explicit words, deterministic dictionary and backpointers; with $m$ owners the cap gives $O(n^3m)$ owner checks and $O(n^3+N)$ stored bits up to polynomial indexing and provenance overhead. Charge unsuccessful edges, exact dictionary access, initial and stagnation certified closure, guard checks and path reconstruction. Each side word induces its integral circulation (variable bits, owner sums, return total ones); a feasible difference is a balanced integral residual flow, decomposable constructively into directed cycles in polynomial work. Atomic common-coordinate changes may have disagreement two during serial physical replay; no monotonicity assertion on such intermediate states is needed or made. On this same serial realization both time and peak space are polynomial, with $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is a one-unit successful S21 potential decrement, not a proof that a capped path always exists, can always be acquired, or that the plateau graph has polynomial diameter.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Executed common-source test and scope.}
Use the exact BS16 registry in commit \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/3d408f496ef04f94fe38b4a248d2c77b27c8dcc1}{3d408f4}, no planted word in acquisition. For each pairing $(0,k)$ versus the remaining partitions, $k=1,2,3$, and root or actual $x_0=0,1$ separately, a finite capped reference search fixes $d$ and its eight neighbors to the double-blocker pattern and finds the diagnostic paired start. All nine starts have singleton unilateral neighborhoods (refuting universal unilateral progress on a genuine SAT source even including ties). All nine acquire actual common words under the augmented $n^2$-state cap, in three or four moves, with 9--227 processed states. The code records actual words and path, not only labels. Executed 3806 assertions, 43634 attempted edges, 423 processed states, 971924 owner checks, 188 auxiliary reference nodes and 9171 closure scans. On the identical Fano and M22 affine-plane root/pin controls, complete-pair cut rejects and finite reference confirms; Fano single-deletion controls accept by finite reference. Supplemental finite guard search forces every unpinned nondefect coordinate to be minority in some actual side owner (exactly obstructing all common flips). For 30 tested consistent diagnostic guards it exhausts without finding an isolated joint state, and six guards are inconsistent; 326 reference nodes and 165476 scans, not a universal guarantee. No source semantic closure oracle, general min-cost backend, physical cycle replay, full I21 lift, kernel or measured CM executed. Diagnostic starting-state acquisition is separate from capped repair, not an algorithm for arbitrary positive instances. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ae10b6bf2f7fc7f1033308d43b8e92dafd81286d}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/47733b061b0bec2d7d5fff8d94572ae71fbee745}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a412bf23a0b63f03c886b7bf5bd50217b5502db2}{results}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/1a36d8be4d22c332cf59d69ba8cebe7a230be837}{isolation controls}.


\paragraph{Cross-track integration with DA17 and GD23 on the identical paired starts.}
DA17's Lemma~\ref{lem:da17-select} applies to the legal owner words of each of the nine diagnostic starts, without any common target word. For each owner retain the two opposite pairs in the word of its actual side. Using parity union-find with the actual pin, run mandatory closure (implied pair discharges the owner, one surviving pair forces it in the current guard, no pair rejects that guard), then choose a surviving pair by minimum product of component sizes, maintaining the ordered speculative guard log. Repeat for 32 fixed deterministic owner permutations per start. All nine have verified common witnesses from this independent capped approach; 75 of 288 attempts succeed, with per-start successes 3--13. Seed zero declines on all nine. Executed 9809 closure scans, 922336 pair tests, 752 conditional forcing joins, 8769 speculative joins and 3600 owner verification visits. Thus the joint plateau method and the anchored algebraic method both bypass exact unilateral stagnation on the same inputs; neither's finite cap implies universal coverage. The affine selector construction supplies a complementary route when plateau BFS declines; GD23's paid identity/coverage warning and its separate pattern-test accounting still apply. Costs of both methods are paid if run in a fixed portfolio, rather than charging only successful steps. DA17 executed residual-cycle replay on other starts of the same registry; M23 has not independently replayed that physical adapter. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/fc4588e250d46243209b4e26427d29cb78a5143f}{integration checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/f968c9c169dfd5c04d804586e9951053a2cb2fb2}{results}. In particular do not turn either UNKNOWN into a certified unconditional rejection.
