Use SAME immutable \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/af8584879409ad3364795d50902f02b5a030bd2a}{DR17}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9164cb11c6fcdf96d470de9d86b061e3201a591f}{BW17}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a412bf23a0b63f03c886b7bf5bd50217b5502db2}{M23} actual source/guard maps and opposite-label Fano controls. Algebra/Hub/MS: authenticated nonlocal row quotient or nonlinear source deductions; Matching/Interfaces: unbalanced algebraic seams, occupancy and syndrome acquisition; Wheels: native lift and port maps; GD/BUILD DAG: sparse support acquisition with pre-dispatch credit and paid consumers; Obstruction/Red Team: all-partition counterexamples and balanced/unbalanced cuts; Solver/Synthesis: direct-first plus optional exact seam, reclose after accepted deductions and compare total charged work on identical inputs. Preserve actual maps and guards; requests only, no peer completion presumed. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Span-rank credit with exact table continuations (S23)}\label{sec:s23}
DS17 (Theorem~\ref{thm:ds17-one-shot}) rules out treating joint one-mismatch acquisition as a free primitive. DP18 (Lemmas~\ref{lem:dp18-seam} and~\ref{lem:dp18-credit}) and RT23 motivate a different charged representation: retain a joint interface as one explicit relation, and use the dimension of the entire observation span, not the sum of individual ranks. This is exact bucket-style elimination on algebraic observations, not a new unrestricted SAT algorithm.

\begin{lemma}[Span-rank seam credit]\label{lem:s23-span}
After actual affine guard absorption, permit owners given by independent observation rows and an explicit accepted-word table, or a single forbidden word on independent rows. Let $U,V$ be the observation spans of a partition $A,B$, let $I=U\cap V$, $a=\dim U$, $d=\dim I$, $t=\dim V-d$, and $r=\dim(U+V)=a+t$. If $t>0$ and
\[
2^d(t+1)^4+(a+1)^4<(r+1)^4,
\]
then exact recursive acquisition of $B$'s interface table followed by one continuation satisfies the aggregate credit with potential $\mu=1+r$ and fixed exponent four. This is a conditional dispatch rule, not a universal availability theorem.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For each of $Q=2^d$ seam words, impose the corresponding independent affine equations, perform certified closure, and decide $B$ in that actual chart. The quotient of its entire observation span has rank exactly $t$ before further closure, hence at most $t$ afterward. Retain a witness for every positive answer; UNKNOWN is not a negative answer. If no words are accepted reject. Otherwise replace all of $B$ by one table on $I$, with the acquired accepted words. Because $I\subseteq U$, the continuation span has rank at most $a$; no extra span rank is introduced. Gluing is precisely the functional argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:dp18-seam}: two witnesses agreeing on $I$ define a functional on $U+V$ and extend by Gaussian solution; verify the original chart and guard. The stated strict integer inequality pays all $Q$ occurrences and the continuation. It also implies $Q<(r+1)^4$, so the actual table and its retained witnesses have polynomial size in the original input. The number of owners along each path does not increase: replacing a nonempty $B$ by one table cannot increase it. A table under a further affine guard is restricted by enumerating its existing accepted words, checking consistency with the affine image, and re-expressing them in independent quotient coordinates. This does not require enumerating unregistered words. A forbidden-word owner can instead be restricted by Gaussian consistency and rank reduction. Local affine-hull closure of a table is computed from its registered accepted words, with all deductions reclosed; an empty table rejects. No new ambient variables are required. Across recursively covered charts the root credit bounds occurrences, each explicit table has polynomial size, and every stored witness/map and consumer is charged. Thus bit time, peak space and cumulative live storage are polynomial on the same serial realization, provided discovery, all remaining terminals and per-occurrence work satisfy the uniform bounds of Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate}. Preflight precedes dispatch; no uncharged fallback from an incomplete recursive acquisition is permitted.
\end{proof}
The tempting replacement of $t$ by the original sum of owner ranks minus $d$ is FALSE. For two rank-one owner spaces $\langle e_1\rangle,\langle e_2\rangle$ and interface $\langle e_1+e_2\rangle$, each owner retains rank one after quotienting, although the global span loses one dimension. Our first checker failed on this inference; the corrected reference found 26 such rank-mass failures among 240 cases. This is not a defect in DP18, which deliberately charged its query children at the original rank mass.

\paragraph{Direct acquisition special case.}
If $d+t\le C\log_2(N+2)$ for one fixed $C$, enumerate functionals on $V$ directly, evaluate all $B$ owners and record the interface table and witnesses. This costs $2^{d+t}\operatorname{poly}(N)$, without recursive calls. For $t>0$, one continuation strictly decreases global span rank. A connected genuine positive linear four-partition source has an initial variable-star elimination with at most eight neighbors, and, if the remaining observation graph is connected on the other vertices, $d=7,t=1$. This is exact on the given source, not a guarantee that later tables have bounded width. Keeping the table avoids DP18's forbidden-packet expansion; expansion back to native owners requires a separate charge. Neither a table's independent row basis nor the rank credit by itself bounds subsequent seam width. U-PROGRESS remains the requirement that every actual nonterminal residual has a paid move or terminal under one fixed uniform policy. A balanced full-query split with $a,t=\Theta(r)$ and unbounded $Q$ again defeats any fixed power of span rank.

\paragraph{Executed controls and same-source integration.}
Seed 20261125: 240 small arbitrary affine table systems (145 SAT, 95 UNSAT), 1353 exact reference fiber queries, 145 verified lifts, 2602 local closure scans and 473 independent forces; no discrepancies after the correction. On immutable DR17, BW17 and M23 registries, root and actual single $x_0$ pins separately, candidate subsets of at most three owners, variable stars, incidence BFS prefixes and partition unions yielded respectively 20457, 6305 and 20476 distinct cuts; 81, 42 and 70 pass the new span-rank gate in at least one direction, versus zero under the DP18 gate in the tested smaller-owner orientation. All source rows are even, so one coordinate pin preserves their quotient ranks. No all-cut search claimed. Executed one coordinate star elimination (eight-coordinate tables, at most 256 words) followed by capped local-closure DPLL on all eleven actual shared starts and Fano/Fano-minus-one. Both standalone and combined obtained 12 SAT/1 UNSAT, all combined witnesses lifted and checked. On the shared starts each four-owner star has 146 accepted coordinate words of 256; these tables are complement-invariant, hence 73 accepted relative words of 128 without an incident pin. All 11 shared jobs use 1205 local star owner tests apiece. Combined DPLL often has fewer scans but more table-pattern checks than direct, before charging projection; no speed advantage or universal success claimed. This test uses coordinate tables and unary closure, not the general recursive span-rank implementation, physical E4/B4 replay, all-affine table closure, formal kernel or measured CM. Source IDs, original guards and exact tables/lifts are in the reproducible evidence. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ea73a2617b2a3c1ad4e038d47f1924ad4d0ffb2f}{Reference checker}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/40881304c3fb5cff076fb8f52142a55d44b9291d}{immutable inputs}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/fdd0323f530aa37a30bdfb76752d344bb13b7dd5}{results}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/d07edfa6a2e0880145a1101350f6abde08db1d0d}{star checker}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/2349f95352f7b93ba2ced149478ced726e7ad1e4}{star results}.

\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
On the same DR17/BW17/M23 sources and actual guards, use the first accepted star projection and its exact boundary relation as the next common residual. Algebra/Hub/MS: find affine-hull or nonlinear reductions of the actual table; Matching/Interfaces: preserve joint occupancy/syndrome and derive exact table adapters; Wheels: transport the projection and lift to native hubs; GD/BUILD DAG: factor table consumers without free equivalence/coverage checks; Potential/Obstruction/Red Team: audit the span-credit and seek genuine residuals in which all small gates fail after fill-in; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first, star-then-closure and repeated paid span elimination on identical source maps, charge failures and exchange witnesses. These are requests, not executed peer results. The next missing lemma is a uniform post-projection progress guarantee or an alternative terminal when table width grows. Publication blocked.


\section{Explicit projected-interface size obstruction and factored star repair (DO18)}\label{sec:do18}
This audits S23 Lemma~\ref{lem:s23-span} and DP18, not their stated conditional credit inequalities. Small source and decreasing span rank do not imply polynomial explicit projected charts.

\begin{lemma}[Mixed explicit projection size obstruction]\label{lem:do18-size}
For each $k\ge2$, there is a polynomial-size 3-CNF source with private variables whose exact exterior projection $S_k$ has full affine hull, but every auxiliary-free representation as a conjunction of single forbidden affine packets and explicit accepted-word tables on independent linear observations has exponential encoded size in $k$, even after arbitrary invertible exterior basis changes. The same holds for its complement-invariant signed NAE realization with a reference bit.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
On $2k$ exterior coordinates let $S_k=\{y:\exists i,\ (y_{2i-1},y_{2i})=(1,1)\}$. Introduce private selectors $s_i$ with $\bigvee_i s_i$, $\neg s_i\lor y_{2i-1}$ and $\neg s_i\lor y_{2i}$. Split the long clause by a private chain, obtaining $O(k)$ width-at-most-three clauses of $O(k\log(k+2))$ encoded bits and a polynomial lift. The rejection set is $R_k=\{00,01,10\}^k$. Any affine flat $A\subseteq R_k$ has pair projections of dimension at most one; their product map is injective, so $\dim A\le k$ and $|A|\le2^k$. Every forbidden packet must lie in $R_k$, hence without nontrivial tables at least $\lceil(3/2)^k\rceil$ packets are necessary. For a nontrivial accepted-word table of observation rank $r$, every rejected fiber must also lie in $R_k$. Such a fiber is an affine flat of dimension $2k-r$, so $r\ge k$. Every accepted word accounts for only $2^{2k-r}$ exterior assignments, and the table must accept every member of $S_k$. Its explicitly registered accepted words therefore number at least
\[
2^r(1-(3/4)^k)\ge 2^k(1-(3/4)^k).
\]
Trivial full tables may be deleted. For every coordinate direction, fixing a different pair to $11$ permits toggling that coordinate inside $S_k$; hence its affine hull is full. Complete affine closure on the projected relation supplies no equation. Homogenize with reference bit $z$ and $y_j=x_j+z$, also for private coordinates. The rejection set in these invertible coordinates is $R_k\times\mathbb F_2$; flat size and total counts double and both lower bounds are unchanged. A three-literal clause becomes a signed four-literal NAE with reference $z$, then two NAE triples by a private split; shorter clauses can first be padded with fresh variables. This preserves exact exterior projection and a polynomial lift. No strict physical four-partition claim is made.
\end{proof}
This is an output-size obstruction, not a decision-time lower bound. The same relation has a linear-size OR of ANDs (guarded DAG), and the original private selectors may be retained. It falsifies universal exact auxiliary-free mixed explicit normalization, not S23's conditional dispatch. In particular, any proof that assumes all projected continuations have polynomial encoded size needs an independent factored-language closure theorem or must avoid these projections.

\begin{lemma}[One-bit factored continuation]\label{lem:do18-factor}
For a private binary direction affecting $m$ forbidden affine packets, exact elimination can replace the affected packets by at most $\binom m2$ forbidden affine packets, preserving all unaffected predicates and guards. In a positive degree-four NAE star with disjoint neighbor pairs, six rank-three packets suffice.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write affected packet $i$ as $C_i(u)\wedge[t=b_i+a_i^\top u]$ by Gaussian elimination. A fiber has no available $t$ exactly when some two active packets exclude opposite values. Its rejection set is the union over unordered pairs of the affine packets
\[
 C_i(u)\wedge C_j(u)\wedge[(a_i+a_j)^\top u=b_i+b_j+1].
\]
Inconsistent packets disappear. Reconstruction tests both private values against the source. A guard depending on $t$ must first be absorbed or explicitly preserved in fiber evaluation; the rule is not free guard deletion. On the degree-four positive star, each pair of incident owners forbids precisely the opposite monochromatic neighbor-pair words $0011$ and $1100$, an affine packet of rank three. The six packets exclude exactly the words with two opposite monochromatic pairs. Every three-coordinate projection is full (complete the remaining coordinates without opposite pairs). The full coordinate affine hull also follows, since all singleton deviations from a constant word are accepted. Rank mass rises from eight to eighteen, while the local observation span decreases from eight to seven.
\end{proof}
The pair rule generalizes the BS14 two-owner fusion; its quadratic local output cap is not a cumulative cap. All pair tests, Gaussian operations, source-map reconstruction, canonicalization and consumer accesses are charged. Repeated span loss cannot by itself pay exponential explicit output, even with local closure. For an actual preflight-bounded execution Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate} still gives the same-realization polynomial resource implication.

\paragraph{Executed common-source check and stagnation.}
At immutable S23 inputs commit \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/40881304c3fb5cff076fb8f52142a55d44b9291d}{4088130}, all eleven DR17/BW17/M23 root/pin jobs were checked on a positive four-owner star (chosen disjoint from the external $x_0$ pin in guarded jobs). Each had 146 of 256 boundary words accepted, 73 relative orbits, exact six-packet factorization, full local affine hull, full ternary marginals and a verified lift for each accepted word. Local closure yields no affine deductions on the tested stars or their factors; no global closure claim. Finite selector-family tests $k=1,\ldots,6$ verify projection, both-label guards (all first coordinates pinned zero versus one pin removed), counts and full hull for $k\ge2$; all affine subsets of rejection for $k\le3$ had dimension at most $k$. Total 11374 assertions. No whole-source solver, repeated fill-in bound, native replay, kernel or physical CM executed. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9d1e03a2e852edaa7cdea9a390a284ba637f4918}{Report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/d26566fa666b693cd85f5dbb04c66ef3ce435b5a}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/6069b8631b676fe13bddc669a2a8963ab30b4d13}{results}.

Parallel request on the same actual star continuations, scope/owner maps and guards: Algebra/Hub/MS exact nonlinear factored compression; Matching/Interfaces joint occupancy and syndrome support rather than independent marginals; Wheels authenticated native lifts; GD/BUILD DAG paid factored coverage/identity and consumers; Potential/Red Teams cumulative growth preflight and hostile post-fill-in controls; Solver/Synthesis compare direct-first, S23 table, DO18 six-packet continuation, reclose after accepted deductions, preserve actual guards and exchange exact witnesses/failure costs. Add the selector family with opposite-label pin controls as a common representation stress test. Requests are not executed peer contributions. Universal U-PROGRESS, SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open; publication blocked.


\section{Repeated projection, affine-cover blowup and a quadratic escape (BS17)}\label{sec:bs17}
The post-projection part of Obligation~\ref{obl:progress} is not implied by S23's span credit. We distinguish local exact elimination, the availability of another paid move, and the output cost of complete compatibility acquisition.

\begin{lemma}[Paid repeated coordinate projection]\label{lem:bs17-bucket}
For explicit Boolean table owners, let $x$ be an unpinned coordinate, $B$ the nonempty bucket of owners involving it, and $W$ the union of their other coordinates. If $|W|\le C\log_2(N+2)$ for fixed $C$, compute the table
\[
P(w)=\bigvee_{b\in\{0,1\}}\bigwedge_{f\in B}f(w,b).
\]
Replace $B$ by $P$, with one recorded extension bit for each accepted word. This is an exact single-child decision transformation, with strict credit for $\mu=1+\#\{\text{remaining coordinates}\}$. Under the same cap on every subsequent selected bucket, and polynomial-size input tables, the sequence including discovery, closure and reconstruction is polynomial.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The other owners do not use $x$, so existential quantification distributes over their conjunction with the bucket. The stored extension witnesses prove the reverse direction; replay in reverse elimination order and verify every original owner and guard. This remains valid under actual coordinate pins by absorbing them first; additional arbitrary affine guards must be included among owners or absorbed with a separate adapter, never dropped. Enumerate at most $(N+2)^C$ boundary words and two values per word. At most one table replaces a nonempty bucket, so owner count does not increase; the number of coordinates decreases strictly, even for a full projected table which is discarded. At most $n$ accepted elimination steps and $n$ scans of candidate coordinates per step suffice under minimum-boundary discovery. Local table-hull equations can be acquired by Gaussian elimination on registered accepted rows; a coordinate-only implementation uses just forced-coordinate closure and is incomplete. Reclose after deductions and before fallback. Table entries, extension maps, failed discovery and reconstruction have polynomial total size and bit costs; the same serial execution has $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is classical bounded-width bucket elimination; its cap is a precondition, not a theorem that the cap always holds. An incomplete capped fallback cannot count as a decided leaf.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact projection can force exponentially many affine packets]\label{lem:bs17-cover}
For $k\ge1$, let $q(x,y)=\sum_{i=1}^k x_i y_i$ over $\mathbb F_2$ with $2k$ exterior bits. There is an explicit $O(k\log(k+2))$-bit 3-CNF with private auxiliaries whose exact existential projection is $q=b$, for either bit $b$. Any equivalent conjunction of affine-packet avoidance predicates on only the exterior bits requires at least $2^{k-1}$ packets for $b=0$ and $2^{k-1}+1$ for $b=1$, even after an arbitrary invertible affine reparameterization.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Introduce private $u_i=x_iy_i$ with the three usual AND clauses, and private running XOR gates computing $\bigoplus_i u_i$ using four clauses per binary XOR; pin the output to $b$. Gate truth tables establish both directions and a unique gate extension. Every forbidden affine packet of any exact exterior-only avoidance representation is contained in the opposite level $q=1-b$. Write a nonempty such packet as $a+L$. For all $v,w\in L$ its second difference is
\[
q(a)+q(a+v)+q(a+w)+q(a+v+w)
=B(v,w)=v_x\cdot w_y+v_y\cdot w_x=0 .
\]
The bilinear form is nondegenerate on $\mathbb F_2^{2k}$ (test against each coordinate basis vector). Thus $L\subseteq L^\perp$, and $\dim L\le k$. Each forbidden packet covers at most $2^k$ exterior words. Counting $y$ for each $x$ gives level sizes $2^{2k-1}+2^{k-1}$ for zero and $2^{2k-1}-2^{k-1}$ for one. Covering the opposite level yields the claimed ceiling bounds. Invertible affine maps preserve affine packets and their cardinalities. Consequently neither flat explicit tables nor exterior-only packet expansion can be an always-polynomial output format for exact projection. The source remains compact and trivially decidable, so this is not a lower bound on SAT, on factored representations with auxiliaries, or on decision-only algorithms.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic terminal under actual affine guards]\label{lem:bs17-quadratic}
One explicit Boolean quadratic equation $q(z)=b$ together with arbitrary explicit affine equations has a deterministic polynomial decision and witness algorithm. After Gaussian consistency and parametrization $z=a+\sum_{i=1}^d t_iv_i$, substitute to obtain a multilinear degree-at-most-two polynomial $p(t)=q(z)+b$. It is zero at some word iff it is zero at one of $0,e_i,e_i+e_j$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
If none is a root, all these evaluations are one. The constant coefficient is then one, all linear coefficients vanish by the evaluations at $e_i$, and all quadratic coefficients vanish by those at $e_i+e_j$. Hence $p$ is identically one. A root found in these $1+d+\binom d2$ evaluations lifts by the actual Gaussian map and is verified. An explicit small truth table can be converted to algebraic normal form by its Boolean Möbius transform in $O(w2^w)$ bit operations, accepting the quadratic adapter only if all terms of degree greater than two vanish (for the indicator of rejection). Multiple simultaneous quadratic equations are not covered; AND and XOR gate equations together with affine output pins encode Boolean circuits. This is an escape from the preceding output-format barrier, not a universal quadratic solver.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Executed repeated-projection integration.}
Read S23 input registry at commit 40881304c3fb5cff076fb8f52142a55d44b9291d and independently matched ordered owner scopes, partitions and forbidden patterns to DR17 af858487, BW17 9164cb1 and M23 a412bf23. The eleven jobs comprise DR17 and BW17 roots plus three repetitions each of M23 root, actual $x_0=0$ and $x_0=1$; side words are intentionally not used. With Fano and one-owner deletion, each under root and both pins, there are 17 jobs (14 SAT, 3 UNSAT). Compare direct capped coordinate DPLL and repeated minimum-boundary projection (cap 10) followed by the same capped DPLL. Both decide all 17 with verified witnesses. For all eleven shared jobs, projection stalls at minimum boundary 11 after 6--9 eliminations; no universal width claim. All three Fano labels are rejected by projection alone; all deletion neighbors lift. The direct/combined totals are 166/166 DPLL nodes, 43029/157874 scanned table rows, 0/109552 discovery tests, 0/21620 projection boundary words, 0/100915 membership tests, 0/12006 stored lift rows in aggregate. Peak active table cells 432/1376 are a partial storage proxy, excluding stacks and lift maps, not physical peak memory. 1233 assertions include quadratic level-size counts through $k=9$. Separate guarded quadratic checks: 240 seeded charts (202 SAT/38 UNSAT), 4701 point probes, 442 assertions, complete parameter enumeration reference. No full affine closure, full recursive span portfolio, compiled native replay, kernel verification or physical CM executed. Actual unary closure ran initially, after each elimination and before any conditional branch; branch pins remain local. Fixed caps permit UNKNOWN; no planted target and no unexecuted peer results assumed. Direct-first is favored on these data; the repeated-projection route is useful on Fano but adds work overall.
\paragraph{Reproducibility and next universal obligation.}
\href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/f36d31a2d429237219a0a7652bf4ac6ecdab9bad}{Checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/19ef8cd3e41246a356f0271ed6ae70da64d7227c}{results}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9c62d51fc65aba755aaa2b8f73cf404d2a707222}{quadratic checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/c5064330f5527ed4d382658364483f2f7287bcac}{quadratic results}. See Dechter, Artificial Intelligence 113 (1999), 41--85, and E\'en--Biere, SAT 2005, DOI 10.1007/11499107\_5 for established elimination methods. Need an exact decision-sensitive alternative to demanding complete exterior support, or a bounded factored representation with paid compatibility and aggregate credit, whenever all affordable projections stall. This remains OPEN; the explicit quadratic escape does not establish universal availability.

\paragraph{Parallel stagnation request.}
Use the identical S23/DR17/BW17/M23 owner registries, exact root/pin guards and deterministic BS17 min-boundary sequence (ties by actual variable ID). Use the five immutable first width-11 residuals with actual ordered table scopes, accepted rows, pins and reverse lift maps at \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/d0957c4e012049409c644b915f52c3483e7bee6d}{BS17 stalled registry}; never conflate different guard jobs. Algebra/Hub/MS: acquire actual table ANF and affine-hull deductions, then test a factored quadratic/other exact rewrite. Matching/Interfaces: translate surviving table constraints into joint occupancy and syndrome, not marginal tests. Wheels: native guarded port lift preserving the newly acquired table. GD/BUILD DAG: factored table representation with charged identity, consumers and coverage; do not insist on exterior-only packets. Obstruction/Potential/Red Teams: find a simultaneous post-fill-in failure of all paid moves or audit a proposed new invariant, including the quadratic affine-cover obstruction and its cheap escape. Solver/Synthesis: integrate direct-first and optional table/other certified moves, reclose after accepted deductions, compare on same source and charge all failures and reconstruction. Report exact deductions, witnesses, counterexamples and dependencies on issue 327. No requested peer contribution presumed executed. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Guarded semijoin and projected row-credit contraction (GD24)}\label{sec:gd24}
BS17's five distinct first width-11 stalled charts permit an alternate paid move that does not enumerate the exterior cube. S23's observation-span credit, DP18's rank-mass credit, and the following row credit are different; a common potential for arbitrary switching is not established.

\begin{lemma}[Exact guarded projected join with row credit]\label{lem:gd24-row}
In an explicit accepted-row chart of polynomial bit size in the original input length $N$, use actual coordinate scopes and pins; absorb affine guards or retain them as charged relations, and do not eliminate any coordinates used by a retained guard. Semijoin filtering and the following pair replacement have deterministic polynomial acquisition and witness reconstruction. For owners $A,B$, let $U$ be their union scope and $W\subseteq U$ the coordinates occurring outside these two owners or used by a retained guard or pin. Compute $P=\pi_W(A\bowtie B)$, retaining one compatible private extension per row. Dispatch the replacement only if $|P|\le |A|+|B|$. An empty $P$ rejects. With $\mu=1+\#\{\text{active coordinates}\}+\#\{\text{owners}\}+\sum_i |R_i|$, each accepted replacement and each nonempty semijoin deletion batch strictly decreases $\mu$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Semijoin deletes a row of $A$ only if its word on the actual common coordinates does not occur in $B$. No solution of the current guarded chart is lost. Unary pins derived from nonempty tables are necessary under that guard; reclose after each accepted deduction and at stagnation. For the pair replacement, any parent solution projects to $P$. Conversely, a continuation witness supplies a row of $P$, whose recorded extension satisfies both parents and changes no outside predicate or guard. A sorted index on the common coordinates enumerates at most $|A||B|$ compatible pairs; deduplicate projected rows in deterministic ordered maps and decline as soon as their distinct count exceeds the cap. Declining is not rejection. Each accepted join removes one owner, cannot increase active coordinate count, and has nonincreasing total rows. Each semijoin deletion decreases total rows. Pins without row deletion number at most $n$; they can be included in a separate decreasing unfixed-coordinate term. Starting from a polynomial-size explicit chart, at most the initial credit many strict dispatches occur, each round scanning polynomially many candidate pairs; row lengths are at most $n$. All discovery, failed pair tests, closure scans, source access, canonicalization, lift maps, and consumers cost polynomial bit work and storage. Reverse replay verifies the source and preserves guards. On this normalization-only serial realization $CM\le(T+1)S$. This is not a bound on later search or on an uncharged earlier chart acquisition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Failure of universal pair-row progress]\label{lem:gd24-stall}
In any unpinned linear monotone 3-uniform NAE registry of minimum vertex degree at least three, unary closure and pairwise semijoins stagnate, and no pair is eligible for Lemma~\ref{lem:gd24-row}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Each six-row owner has both values on every coordinate. Distinct owners share at most one coordinate, so all their overlap words have support. For an intersecting pair, each shared bit value has three extensions in each owner; the join has 18 distinct rows. For disjoint owners there are 36. Minimum degree at least three makes every coordinate of the union occur outside the pair, hence projection removes none. The cap is 12. The Fano plane gives an UNSAT example; its independent products increase size but admit other easy decomposition. This falsifies only the stated local progress rule, not arbitrary SAT algorithms.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Executed same-residual comparison.}
On the five exact BS17 snapshots at \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/d0957c4e012049409c644b915f52c3483e7bee6d}{d0957c4}, direct, semijoin, and semijoin-plus-pair-merge capped coordinate DPLL all produce verified SAT witnesses (78/69/63 search nodes). Root semijoins remove respectively 14, 86, 20, 48, 48 rows from DR17, BW17, M23 root, M23 $x_0=0$, M23 $x_0=1$. No additional pins. Three merges on BW17 and the two pinned M23 charts each remove six more rows; no private coordinates in these three merges. Final BW17/M23 pinned row counts 866/1040/1040. Exact table IDs, deletion words, guard scopes, parent maps and lift maps at \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a06b25dad6c0d06f689a201825e2d267269cf181}{GD24 deductions}. All five remain undecided at root; additional scans outweigh node reduction. Fano and one-owner deletion, parity triangle consistent/inconsistent controls agree in all modes (seven SAT/two UNSAT including shared charts); combined rejects inconsistent triangle before branching. Positive lifts checked against BS17 maps and original S23 owners. No full affine closure, general recursive portfolio, native replay, formal kernel or physical peak/CM executed. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/3518a75faa99f52956f6caeb0119c666ea5282e5}{Report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/cb612e8abf58fa1f7d0f3cadef21b268ead1666d}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/cc4e1cf465d95b0c2fced7f010ff1a6b7a485680}{results}. Context: Greco and Scarcello, SIAM J. Comput., DOI 10.1137/16M1090272 (relational local consistency).

\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
Use the same five GD24 updated charts, actual source maps, exact guards and deletion provenance. Algebra/Hub/MS: actual hull/ANF after semijoins; Matching/Interfaces: exact joint occupancy/syndrome of merged tables; Wheels: native lifts preserving rows; BUILD DAG: paid table factoring and consumer costs; Potential/Obstruction/Red Team: unified row/span credit and hostile residuals; Solver/Synthesis: direct-first versus alternating certified deductions, repeat closure and charge every failure and lift. Requests only, not executed peer contributions. Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Exact algebraic table compilation and failed joint linear cancellation (DA18)}\label{sec:da18}
The GD24 row deletions and merges are authenticated exact transformations on the BS17 stalled charts. We ask whether their new local nonlinear identities give unconditional progress without branching.
\begin{lemma}[Paid Boolean point-ideal adapter]\label{lem:da18-border}
For a nonempty explicit set $R\subseteq\mathbb F_2^k$ of $r$ distinct rows, one can deterministically acquire a family of at most $kr$ Boolean polynomial equations with at most $r+1$ monomials each and degree at most $1+\lfloor\log_2 r\rfloor$, whose common zero set is exactly $R$. Work and storage are polynomial in the explicit table length and scope length. No exterior-cube enumeration is necessary. The empty relation is the equation $1=0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Work in the Boolean ring $x_i^2=x_i$ and order squarefree monomials by degree and then a multiplicative ordinary monomial order. Start with the constant as candidate. In increasing order, evaluate each candidate on the $r$ rows. Skip any candidate divisible by a previously recorded leading monomial. If its evaluation column is independent of the acquired columns, keep it and enqueue its squarefree one-variable multiples. Otherwise express its column in the independent earlier columns by Gaussian elimination and record the resulting vanishing polynomial with this candidate as leading monomial. Deduplicate the queue. At most $r$ independent columns are acquired, at most $1+kr$ candidates are generated and at most $kr$ equations are emitted. Every squarefree monomial either belongs to the acquired basis or is divisible by a recorded lead: induct on degree, since an unblocked proper divisor is acquired and generates the next multiple. Reducing a divisible monomial by the recorded equation gives strictly smaller terms, including after squarefree reduction. Thus the quotient by the emitted equations is spanned by the acquired monomials. Evaluations on distinct Boolean points have full row rank by the point indicators; reduction also preserves evaluation, so there are exactly $r$ acquired monomials. If the common zero set contained an additional point, its point indicator and the indicators of $R$ would give $r+1$ independent evaluation columns on that zero set, contradicting the quotient dimension. All divisors of an acquired monomial are acquired; all proper divisors of an emitted lead are acquired (otherwise it would have been skipped). A lead of degree $d$ therefore has at least $2^{d-1}$ acquired divisors, giving the degree bound. Gaussian elimination on explicit columns, queue ordering, and output encoding are polynomial in $k,r$; source variable IDs and guards are retained. This is a Boolean specialization of the point-ideal/Buchberger--Moller approach, not a new general SAT decision algorithm.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Joint linear cancellation is exact but not universally progressive]\label{lem:da18-cancel}
Embed the acquired local equations into a common actual-coordinate polynomial registry, preserving all guards. Linear row elimination with high-degree columns first obtains every affine equation in their ordinary $\mathbb F_2$ linear span. Restricting each table by these equations using affine feasibility is sound and preserves all joint solutions. Recompile and repeat after deletions; explicit row count bounds the number of strict rounds. However on every unguarded signed linear 3-uniform NAE registry this procedure acquires no affine equation and no contradiction, even though some such registries are UNSAT.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
All equations vanish on joint solutions. Echelon elimination in decreasing monomial order identifies the intersection of their span with the affine subspace, and Gaussian consistency of a table word with those equations is necessary. Every strict round deletes at least one row. For a signed NAE owner on distinct variables the six-row table has an exact single quadratic equation, with all three pair monomials occurring with coefficient one (signs affect only lower terms). In the preceding adapter the first dependent monomial is the last quadratic of that owner, and its triple multiple is skipped; thus exactly that equation is acquired. In a linear hypergraph each pair belongs to at most one owner, so every nonempty sum retains a quadratic term. All tables survive. The Fano plane is an UNSAT control and its one-sign perturbation is SAT. This is a barrier only to ordinary linear cancellation without multiplication or other certified inference. Furthermore a full $k$-cube minus one point requires degree $k$ in any exact Boolean polynomial presentation: every nonzero vanishing equation is precisely the indicator of that missing point. Exact row deletion need not decrease polynomial degree or term count. Switching row, span, and equation-size reserves without a common aggregate invariant is not justified.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed integration and next obligation.}
The actual BS17 five width-11 snapshots at \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/d0957c4e012049409c644b915f52c3483e7bee6d}{d0957c4} and GD24 final tables at \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a06b25dad6c0d06f689a201825e2d267269cf181}{a06b25d} were read under immutable refs. On DR17, BW17, M23 root and both actual $x_0$ pins the maximum acquired degrees before/after GD24 were respectively 3/4, 5/4, 3/4, 3/4, 3/4; ordinary polynomial span ranks were 60/64, 67/72, 64/68, 67/78, 67/78 (including pins). All post-GD24 charts have exact local equation degree at most four, but none gains an affine equation beyond its supplied pin and no additional rows are deleted. Polynomial monomial occurrences increase in all five charts despite row deletion. The checker exhaustively verifies local truth sets (19656 words) and separately counts Fano 0 and sign-neighbor 10 models; the exhaustive validation is experimental, not part of the general adapter. 1525222 column-row evaluations, 103984 elimination XORs, 12 closure rounds; no new global witness acquisition, no polynomial calculus multiplication saturation, no native replay, kernel or physical CM measurement. Exact table coordinates and prior lift maps are unchanged; the proof charges all compilation, guards, and equation consumers. For the serial capped preprocessing, $CM\le(T+1)S$. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/5f856cd081bfe8dc24913fd34a4527a233bf470e}{checker} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/cdbab38d33f844b1208144c76d4612244735cb93}{results}.
The next joint experiment is multiplication-aware sparse identities on the identical GD24 tables and guards, with pre-dispatch caps on monomial generation and explicit proof provenance, or an exact algebraic seam that reduces aggregate credit. Algebra/Hub/MS: sparse cross-owner multiplication; Matching/Interfaces: actual joint occupancy and syndrome, not marginals; Wheels: exact port lift; GD/BUILD DAG: factored polynomial identity and consumer cost; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: audit degree/term inflation and cumulative reserve; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first versus alternating paid deductions on these exact charts. No requested peer work presumed executed. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Covered selector closure and its universal progress obstruction (M24)}\label{sec:m24}
GD24's exact explicit row chart and DA18's failed ordinary linear cancellation motivate a stronger covered guard move using the same row-credit potential, rather than changing the resource measure.
\begin{lemma}[Exact selector and guarded cover closure]\label{lem:m24-cover}
For an explicit chart of total row count $R$ and actual coordinate scopes, with coordinate guards retained, there is a polynomial-size syndrome-matching adapter and a deterministic polynomial-bit-work normalization that preserves every chart solution. Every nontrivial accepted deletion strictly decreases GD24's row credit. The procedure subsumes singleton row arc-consistency tests.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a nonempty table with $r$ rows use a path of $2r-1$ vertices and a hub adjacent to the odd-indexed path vertices. A perfect matching chooses exactly one hub edge; deleting its endpoint leaves even paths with unique matchings. Label the $r$ hub choices by the actual rows. For each source variable choose one reference occurrence, adding a syndrome coordinate for equality of each other occurrence to that reference. Spoke labels contribute the actual corresponding row bits; zero syndrome enforces precisely all occurrence equalities. Coordinate guards restrict the rows, or remain explicit if not in any scope. Other guards must be retained or compiled and charged separately. This is a polynomial-size exact matching representation, not necessarily a degree-four native adapter. Decode the selected spokes, retain actual IDs and replay previous lifts.

In the binary dual encoding, owner domains consist of actual table rows and binary compatibility means equality on the full overlap. First enforce semijoin (arc-consistency) closure. For pivot owner $i$ and each current row $a\in R_i$, run semijoin closure on the copy restricted to $R_i=\{a\}$, discard inconsistent copies, and let $D_j^{(a)}$ be the surviving domain at owner $j$ for each remaining copy. Replace simultaneously
\[
 R_j\ \longleftarrow\ \bigcup_{a:\,\text{copy survives}} D_j^{(a)} .
\]
Any genuine joint solution selects one pivot row and survives its guarded closure, so no solution is lost. All domains only shrink and compatibility is unchanged, so no new solution is introduced. An empty union rejects. A conditional deletion alone does not authorize a global deletion; the union covers every current pivot choice. A nonempty union of AC-closed copies is AC-closed, because each row retains supports in its own copy. At a fixed point no singleton test can fail. A surviving copy is not evidence of global satisfiability.

Repeat closure after every accepted deletion and at stagnation. At most $R$ strict deletion batches occur. A complete pivot sweep uses at most $R$ probes, and at most $R+1$ sweeps are needed. Each probe performs at most $R+1$ full semijoin rounds on at most $m^2$ owner pairs with at most $R^2$ comparisons of at most $n$ bits; all indices, guards and comparisons have polynomial bit cost in the explicit chart length. Certificate replay can recompute each probe from its pre-batch domains or retain its polynomial-sized trace; there is no recursive global decision inside a probe. Each accepted batch strictly decreases $\mu=1+n+m+R$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:gd24-row}; all scope IDs and prior lifts remain unchanged. Thus on a polynomially acquired explicit chart this normalization has polynomial time and peak space, with $CM\le(T+1)S$ on its serial execution. Acquisition of the initial chart and any later search are separate paid obligations. This is constructive disjunction on the dual encoding, related to bidirectional singleton arc consistency (Bessiere and Debruyne, AIJ, DOI \href{https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artint.2007.09.001}{10.1016/j.artint.2007.09.001}).
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Universal covered-guard progress is false]\label{lem:m24-stall}
On every unpinned linear signed 3-uniform NAE registry the covered selector rule deletes no row. In particular this holds for the UNSAT nine-point affine-plane four-partition registry.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix any pivot row. Every other owner meets its scope in at most one coordinate. Restrict those other owners to the specified bit when applicable. A signed NAE relation with at most one fixed bit still allows both values on each remaining coordinate. Thus every nonempty overlap is a singleton with equal marginal support on both owners, and the restricted domains are already arc-consistent. No pivot row fails. Every row of every other owner survives some pivot choice: select a pivot row agreeing on their at most one common coordinate, which exists by full singleton support. Consequently the covered union restores the entire current domain at every owner. This includes Fano UNSAT and its signed SAT neighbor. The obstruction is to this rule, not arbitrary SAT decision, multiplication-aware algebra, or nonlocal matching cuts.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed same-chart integration.}
On immutable GD24 tables \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a06b25dad6c0d06f689a201825e2d267269cf181}{a06b25d}, compare ordinary AC, singleton AC and covered union closure. For DR17, BW17, M23 root, M23 $x_0=0$, M23 $x_0=1$ the row counts are respectively $1006/910/910$, $866/452/452$, $1244/1190/1190$, $1040/928/926$, $1040/928/926$. The union deletes 792 additional rows versus 788 by singleton AC, with 10956 versus 11450 probes on these five charts. All five remain UNKNOWN at root with no additional coordinate pins. Five previously supplied GD24 witnesses verified against the new tables, guards and all original S23 owners; no new witness acquired. Fano UNSAT, signed Fano SAT, affine-plane UNSAT and its single-sign UNSAT perturbation all stall; inconsistent parity triangle rejects under singleton/union and consistent triangle gives a verified witness. No full semantic closure, native matching replay, new global search, kernel or physical memory measurement. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/7e1164f43763ea4efe7f0a506432cbd996145a9e}{Checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/0caa97d249fb20a7feb5cc11df304971f3083cef}{results}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/41f861068ff38ae5e75d753e6bf47c965d3c27ce}{exact reduced rows and provenance}. No speed claim relative to direct search.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
Use these same reduced tables, GD24 lift maps and separate actual root/pin guards. Algebra/Hub/MS: recompile DA18 equations and test paid sparse multiplication; Matching/Interfaces: exact joint occupancy-syndrome support and cuts; Wheels: native source lift; GD/BUILD DAG: factored conditional certificates with paid consumers; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: hostile fixed points and unified row/span credit; Solver/Synthesis: direct-first versus alternating paid deductions and verified lifts. Reclose after each accepted deduction. Requests only, not executed peer contributions. The missing universal step remains Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST; no global polynomial conclusion.


\section{Unified mixed-radix credit and guarded row-probe audit (DR18)}\label{sec:dr18}
The explicit-table output barriers of DO18/BS17 and the incompatible row/term counts of GD24/DA18 require an aggregate invariant, not an arbitrary switch of progress measures. We give a common reserve for the certified S23 and GD24 dispatches, without assuming that a dispatch is always available.

\begin{lemma}[Mixed-radix lifting of aggregate credit]\label{lem:dr18-credit}
Fix the original input length $N$. Suppose the base integer credit $q(s)\ge1$ is polynomially bounded in $N$ and a secondary integer measure satisfies $0\le h(s)\le B(N)$ for a single fixed polynomial integer cap $B$. Accept exact moves only of the following kinds: (I) a list of child occurrences with $\sum_i q(s_i)\le q(s)-1$; (II) one child with $q(s_1)=q(s)$ and $h(s_1)<h(s)$. All moves must meet the same uniform size, discovery, verification and lift requirements as Theorem~\ref{thm:aggregate}. Then a single polynomial integer credit for both kinds, with exponent one, is
\[
W(s)=(B+1)(q(s)-1)+h(s)+1.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Set $L=B+1$. For any state, $L(q-1)+1\le W\le Lq$. Hence for type I, $\sum_i W(s_i)\le L\sum_i q(s_i)\le L(q(s)-1)\le W(s)-1$, regardless of the number of children or their secondary measures. For type II, the base term cancels and the integer secondary measure drops by at least one. This counts duplicate occurrences, not only distinct DAG keys. All terminal and failed-attempt costs are still charged by the aggregate theorem. The argument is valid only for actual preflight-bounded charts and exact typed moves; it does not supply an eligible move or prove a small representation exists.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Instantiation and representation discipline.}
Take $q=(1+r)^4$, where $r$ is the dimension of the declared observation span in actual free coordinates after guard absorption. Keep declared observation scopes for unchanged owners (do not alternately expand a representation and count the smaller semantic rank for free). Take $h$ to be the number of owners plus the total number of registered accepted rows (or one registered forbidden word for a packet). On S23's allowed rank-credit dispatch each recursive child and the continuation have the proved strict aggregate $q$ contraction; on a GD24 coordinate-table semijoin or allowed pair merge, declared span cannot increase and either $q$ decreases or $h$ strictly decreases. DA18's local point-ideal compiler and Gaussian cancellations are scratch computations: commit only certified row deletions or guard quotients that satisfy the same credit test, not a growing equivalent polynomial registry as an uncharged new active state. For exact coordinate or affine quotient moves, preserve the actual guard and coordinate maps; no new free variable or scope-increasing encoding is implicitly permitted. Other terminals need their own adapter and cost proof. A cap can be fixed independently of success for this restricted mixed representation: initialize each 3-CNF owner by at most seven accepted rows, or one forbidden word. Owner count never increases along a path in the specified S23/GD24 operations; each S23 continuation adds at most $(N+2)^4$ interface words (direct small-span enumeration may use a larger fixed power). Guard restriction does not increase row count. At most $N$ strict rank drops occur on a path; same-rank GD24 moves do not increase rows. For example, with direct enumeration cap $(N+2)^C$, $B=10(N+2)^{\max(4,C)+2}$ bounds these owner-plus-row counts. Arbitrary representation conversion, table materialization, or an added terminal not meeting these assumptions requires a separately proved cap or must decline. Uniform polynomial bit work and storage for the admitted operations, including all failed scans, tables, guard/source maps and lift consumers, remain necessary. The theorem combines two previously separate credits but is conditional on coverage; U-PROGRESS is not resolved.

\begin{lemma}[Paid conditional row rejection]\label{lem:dr18-probe}
For an explicit coordinate-table chart of polynomial encoded size, fix one registered row of one owner as a temporary guard and compute certified pair-semijoin and local-affine-hull closure. If closure proves contradiction, that row can be deleted unconditionally relative to the original chart guard. Repeat closure after deletion. A full round with no successful row rejection certifies only stagnation of this particular rule, not global feasibility. A fixed explicit chart admits polynomial total work for repeated full row-probe scans (or a smaller fixed cap).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every global solution using the selected row survives the temporary guard and every sound closure operation. Empty support or inconsistent necessary affine equations thus exclude that row. A nonempty guarded fixed point supplies no converse. Discard other conditional deletions unless independently justified. Each successful probe permanently deletes at least one of the initially registered rows and reduces $h$ (or the base span after certified quotient). Between successful probes there are at most the initial number of rows to test, so at most quadratic many probes in that count. Each semijoin/affine round is polynomial in the bounded explicit chart, and any strict round deletes a row. Guarded hull equations are computed from actual surviving rows and projected by Gaussian elimination onto each owner scope; all query work is scratch overhead, not free recursive decisions. Reclose the original chart after accepted deletion and before fallback. This is relational singleton consistency strengthened by affine-hull propagation, not complete semantic closure.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Executed same-chart audit.}
On the five immutable GD24 final coordinate charts (DR17, BW17, M23 root and the two distinct actual $x_0$ pins), compare pair closure then DPLL, the same with 128 conditional row probes per chart, and the same probes with joint affine-hull closure. All five charts remain OPEN at root in all three modes; DPLL obtains SAT with exactly 63 total visited nodes in each mode. Root row deletions from probes are respectively 0,3,0,1,1; the joint mode obtains no additional deletions. Pair row scans across the five charts grow from 3346532 to 47779704 in both probe modes; joint mode additionally charges 1432072 hull-row scans and 2349337 elimination XORs. All 15 source witnesses are lifted through actual GD24 and BS17 maps and checked against immutable S23 original positive NAE owners and guard. Fano remains unresolved after all 42 row probes even with joint hull closure; finite DPLL rejects it, while Fano-minus-one is SAT. The inconsistent parity triangle is rejected by affine hull closure at root, or by a single conditional row probe followed by reclosure; the consistent triangle is SAT. In all 27 mode/chart runs there are no decision-label discrepancies; 21 SAT/6 UNSAT. The cap is experimental, not a claim of complete row-probe closure on the five large charts. Direct-first remains preferable on these controls by measured scan counts. No full portfolio, general affine-seam implementation, native physical replay, kernel check or physical cumulative memory measurement was performed.
Evidence: \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/dfa9bfa0c5d67210bac83d93f6c0a150299c1f98}{checker}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/c9c31c7b9f3d1593c9bdc25e130d769968e07815}{results and exact post-probe tables}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/a005d1c10c2b3541df79dff73224ae2ee9eba095}{source lift checker}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/7bf620d7a250a019ab3ce77adf5b8222683c496c}{source witnesses}. For admitted serial executions all occurrences are charged by $W$, each bounded scan is polynomial in $N$, and $CM\le(T+1)S$; the fallback DPLL has no proved polynomial aggregate bound.

\paragraph{Concurrent M24 integration and independent containment audit.}
M24 used broader covered singleton/row selection rather than the experimental 128-probe cap here. On the same five GD24 charts its recorded reductions number 96,414,54,114,114 (792 total); all five DR18 deleted rows are already among these reductions, and all five DR18 chart witnesses survive the M24 tables. This is exact row-by-row comparison with identical owner IDs, ordered scopes and actual guards, not independent certification of every M24 proof step. We then imported those actual M24 reductions and repeated pair closure, joint affine-hull closure and the same 128-probe cap on the exact updated charts. All five remain OPEN at root; no additional row was removed in any of the three modes. The same 63 DPLL nodes decide the five charts and all 15 additional source lifts verify. Post-M24 pair-row scans are 2930930 direct and 43449874 probed, excluding M24's earlier acquisition cost; consequently no amortized speed claim. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/e109ac835a3fad8670f6409cc7c077206b74c528}{containment audit} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9ac7e9d745b7220f0634990bd08d919313471f82}{reclosed evidence}. Both M24 and DR18 row deletions fit Lemma~\ref{lem:dr18-credit}; every local scratch probe must be paid. This provides a stronger authenticated fixed-point target for capped multiplication, nonlinear seams and factored compatibility, not a proof that all such moves fail. Further peer work should use the M24-reclosed charts rather than replay the dominated 128-probe policy on the earlier GD24 version.

\paragraph{Parallel stagnation and remaining universal gap.}
Use the exact DR18 post-probe tables, original GD24/BS17 lift maps and immutable S23 source, separately for each actual guard. Algebra/Hub/MS: capped multiplication-aware identities or a nonaffine seam, with deductions reclosed; Matching/Interfaces: joint occupancy/syndrome support on the surviving explicit tables; Wheels: native port lift; GD/BUILD DAG: authenticated factored representation without assuming free identity/coverage; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: extend the common credit to exact nonaffine conversions or construct hostile charts defeating every currently certified dispatch, not merely the probe cap; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first and alternating exact moves using the common credit, account for every failed acquisition and source lift. Exchange exact deductions/maps/costs, retain guards, and do not presume requested peer work executed. The blocked statement is still Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}: a uniformly discoverable contracting move or paid decision at every reachable chart. SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN. No public proof claim.



\section{Local-reference native table lifts and exact translation-orbit credit (BW18)}\label{sec:bw18}
We combine GD24's actual guarded row tables and BS17's reverse lift maps with the strict I21 compiler. This attacks the post-projection part of U-PROGRESS without demanding free semantic equivalence or independent wheel phases.
\begin{lemma}[Paid translation equivalence and orbit representative contraction]\label{lem:bw18-orbit}
For two explicit Boolean row charts with matched owner IDs and ordered scopes, and the same affine guard matrix with potentially different right hand sides, one can deterministically find a global translation $d$ mapping every corresponding row relation and the actual guard, or certify that no such translation exists, in polynomial bit time in their explicit lengths. On one chart the resulting structural stabilizer is a linear subspace $H$. For a table-only chart with absorbed coordinate pins, if $s=\dim H>0$ on the active unpinned coordinates, one can remove $s$ coordinates by exact decision-equivalent orbit selection with strict GD24 row credit.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For nonempty tables $A_i,B_i$ of equal cardinality fix $a\in A_i$, test the candidates $a+b$ for all $b\in B_i$ by shifted row membership, and retain exactly $S_i=\{d_i:A_i+d_i=B_i\}$. Unequal cardinalities or an empty candidate set decline; empty actual owners are handled by closure. If nonempty, $S_i=d_i^0+H_i$ where $H_i$ is the translation stabilizer of $A_i$, a linear subspace since two preserving translations compose and every translation is self-inverse. Compute a basis from the explicit differences, and represent this coset by the equations of its orthogonal complement. On actual common coordinates solve all these linear equations and $Gd=b_A+b_B$ by Gaussian elimination; coordinate pins are the corresponding one-coordinate equations. The common translation is a bijection of the chart solution sets. This does not assert completeness of semantic equivalence recognition or transport of eliminated source coordinates; use the target chart's own recorded lift. All tests are polynomial in the explicit row input, including failed candidates.
For a chart compared with itself, restrict $H$ to the active coordinates (do not credit unused variables). Pick $s$ coordinate positions whose restriction to $H$ is invertible, by Gaussian pivot selection. Every orbit meets the slice with these coordinates zero exactly once. Filter the actual owner tables by this slice and remove fixed coordinates, retaining the basis and reconstruction maps. This is symmetry breaking, not a claim of necessary affine equations on every original solution. It preserves decision, and any representative lifts directly, with its translates recovering the other residual solutions. Reclose after the restriction and retain conditional deductions within the slice. The active scope decreases by $s$, owners and rows do not increase, so GD24's credit decreases strictly. The same argument does not license changing to an unrelated potential or acquiring an exponentially large chart. For unpinned signed NAE on a connected triple hypergraph the translation stabilizer of each owner is exactly $\{000,111\}$ (the two forbidden complementary words must be mapped to themselves as a set). Hence the structural stabilizer is precisely global complement; one actual pin kills it. Thus repeated orbit contraction is not a universal progress mechanism on these cores, even though it can avoid duplicate opposite-guard evaluations.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Functional local-reference native adapter]\label{lem:bw18-native}
Any explicit accepted Boolean table with $k$ coordinates and $r$ rows has a deterministic $O(1+kr)$-object signed NAE circuit representation with unique auxiliary extension for every accepted raw input and fixed reference. A complement-invariant nonzero-arity table needs no external reference: an actual scope coordinate suffices. Applying I21's physical compilation preserves this functional extension and has polynomial size and acquisition time in the explicit chart.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For reference bit $h$ the three predicates
\[
 \operatorname{NAE}(a,b,\neg z),\quad
 \operatorname{NAE}(\neg z,a,h),\quad
 \operatorname{NAE}(\neg z,b,h)
\]
uniquely enforce $z+h=(a+h)(b+h)$ over $\mathbb F_2$. For $h=0$ the last two are implications from $z$, while the first forces the correct output for equal inputs; for $h=1$ complement all bits. OR uses De Morgan; the raw bits $h,\neg h$ encode constants and $\operatorname{NAE}(out,h,h)$ requires the output true. Use an acyclic prefix-shared OR of row conjunctions, at most $O(1+kr)$ gates and predicates, or the contradiction $\operatorname{NAE}(h,h,h)$ for empty support. The gate property proves both directions and uniqueness, without enumerating the exterior cube. For complement-invariant $R$, select its first actual coordinate as $h$, retain the rows starting with zero, and remove that first coordinate from each row test. The normalized input word (XOR with $h$) is in this list exactly when the raw input is in $R$. Local references are actual shared coordinates, not freely chosen independent phases. All noninvariant owners and pins use a single common reference $r_0$ and decode actual bits by XOR with $r_0$; invariant owners are unaffected by that decoding. An explicit affine guard can be evaluated by XOR circuits on these decoded inputs, with the same common reference. The experiments below used only actual coordinate pins. Set $r_0=0$ for a forward lift; if it does not occur physically choose zero for reverse decoding. I21's duplicated B4 parity, E4 paths and simple-wire replacements are linear in literal occurrences, with unique physical extension for each legal NAE input. Witness reconstruction and all identifier, guard and incidence checks are polynomial in the actual explicit encoding. No universal bound on its acquisition, matching-tag rank or native solver is implied.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed same-chart integration.}
On the five immutable GD24/BS17 charts (DR17, BW17, M23 root and separate $x_0=0,1$ pins), all unpinned owners are complement-invariant and 15 of 20 owners in each pinned M23 chart are invariant. Global-reference vs local-reference gate counts are 3292/1571, 3081/1513, 4146/2013, 3499/2387, 3499/2387. Constructed and checked all five actual simple four-regular native graphs, respectively 243254, 233420, 311014, 368445, 368445 vertices, with exact signed port values. 9760 local input words, 13810513 assertions, 145 unary closure scans, 54761 shift membership checks, 91 DPLL nodes including Fano UNSAT and deletion SAT. The checker used local orthogonal-mask enumeration as a finite test, not the polynomial nullspace algorithm of the lemma. Reconstructed all five witnesses through BS17 and GD24 maps and independently verified 224 original S23 source owners. Active structural translation dimensions are 1/1/1/0/0. Direct versus gauge-fixed DPLL nodes on the three root charts are 13/13,11/10,15/12; opposite pinned M23 charts have a certified translation, and the transported witness was independently reconstructed using the other chart's lift and verified at source. The 127 supplemental assertions include these lifts. No full native SAT algorithm, full semantic closure, complete portfolio, formal kernel or physical peak/CM measurement was performed. For polynomially bounded explicit charts the paid preprocessing and verification have polynomial time and space on the same serial realization, with $CM\le(T+1)S$. The direct/combined results are finite controls, not asymptotic guarantees.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
Use the same GD24 registry a06b25d, BS17 d0957c4, S23 4088130 and actual guards. Algebra/Hub/MS: nonlinear deductions after exact structural gauge quotient; Matching/Interfaces: joint occupancy and syndrome of actual anchor ports; GD/BUILD DAG: translation certificates and paid consumer maps; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: unified row-credit audit and hostile post-quotient charts; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first with gauge-first alternating semijoin/other certified deductions, reclose and preserve guards. Requests only, not presumed executed. Report and code in \texttt{research/constructive-shared-hubs/wheels/2026-11-25\_0012\_BW18*}. Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Joint moment-syndrome closure and a bounded-proof obstruction (I24)}\label{sec:i24}
The exact M24-reclosed tables in DR18 give a common residual for testing DA18's proposed multiplication-aware feedback. Rather than treating matching occupancies or hub phases independently, use the actual local row selected by the M24 adapter as the source of all its joint moment observations.
\begin{lemma}[Paid moment-syndrome row projection]\label{lem:i24-moment}
For a polynomially acquired explicit accepted-row chart on actual coordinates, with actual coordinate guards, fix a constant $d\ge1$. Form the feature vector $\phi_d(x)$ of all squarefree monomials of degree at most $d$, including $1$. Acquire the local vanishing polynomial spaces of degree at most $d$ from explicit row evaluations, embed them on common actual coordinates, add the guard equations and a polynomially capped sequence of legal Boolean multiplication steps of degree at most $d$. Let $E$ be the span of the resulting coefficient rows. For each owner row $a$ on scope $I$, delete it only if its local feature vector has no extension to a global linear word $y\in E^\perp$ with $y_\emptyset=1$. Reject if $1\in E$ or a table becomes empty. This procedure, interleaved with guard filtering and semijoins after each accepted deletion batch and at stagnation, is sound and decision-preserving until a certified rejection, with deterministic polynomial bit work, space and certificate replay for fixed $d$. A nonempty deletion fits the DR18 common credit without committing the larger feature registry as an active chart. A surviving abstract moment word need not be realizable.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Each local evaluation kernel is a space of polynomials vanishing on the actual allowed rows. Embed using the actual variable IDs; sums and Boolean products of these equations vanish on every actual guarded solution. Let $L_I$ be the coefficient coordinate subspace spanned by monomials supported in $I$ of degree at most $d$. The exact linear projection of $E^\perp$ to these coordinates is the annihilator of $E\cap L_I$. Compute a basis for that intersection by reducing the unit feature coefficient vectors on $I$ modulo $E$ and extracting their linear dependencies. The constant monomial is included, so evaluating these dependencies on $\phi_d(a)$ tests exactly the specified linear extension condition with constant one. This is stronger than separately testing local pair marginals and does not certify existence of a compatible nonlinear completion. A failed dependency evaluates to one on $a$ and vanishes on every solution under the actual guard. Thus no solution is lost and no new one is introduced; the selector and previous source lifts remain valid. There are at most $\sum_{j=0}^d\binom nj$ feature coordinates; evaluation kernels, polynomially many products, projections and certificate replay use polynomial bit work and storage in explicit chart length. Each strict batch deletes rows, so there are at most the original total row count many rounds. All failed tests, semijoin scans, guard/source maps and consumers are charged. Declared active observation span does not increase, and the secondary row measure decreases on any nontrivial deletion; apply Lemma~\ref{lem:dr18-credit}. The serial normalization has $CM\le(T+1)S$, not a bound on earlier chart acquisition or later search.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Fixed-arity deletion-only proof-system firewall]\label{lem:i24-firewall}
Fix maximum owner arity $k$, feature degree $d$, and covered-probe nesting depth $\ell$, independently of input length. On original fixed scopes with coordinate guards, any refutation by repeated moment-syndrome deletions of Lemma~\ref{lem:i24-moment}, semijoins and fixed-depth covered owner-row probes admits a multilinear polynomial-calculus refutation over $\mathbb F_2$ of degree at most $\max(d,2k)+k\ell$. In particular this deletion-only portfolio is not a universal refutation procedure on direct bounded-width CNF charts. The statement does not include scope-changing rewrites, unrestricted affine guards, arbitrary integer certificates or the full cooperative portfolio.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a local forbidden word $a$, use its Boolean indicator $\delta_a$ as an equation equal to zero. A vanishing polynomial on a table is the sum of the indicators of forbidden words at which its value is one. Thus all the acquired local equations follow with degree at most $\max(k,d)$. A projected dependency $p$ witnessing a row deletion has $p(a)=1$, and involves only its owner's scope. Derive $\delta_a=p\delta_a$ by multiplying one literal at a time and reducing in the Boolean ring; all these products stay in the scope. To simulate a semijoin deletion, sum the forbidden indicators of the second owner that extend the unsupported overlap word, giving the overlap indicator; multiply by the first owner's row indicator. Degree at most $2k$ suffices. Coordinate filtering follows from the pinned literal. An empty owner is refuted by summing all its indicators. Constant-one detection in $E$ follows by the acquired polynomial-calculus steps and linear combinations.
Under a covered pivot guard $b$, multiply each line of the corresponding conditional derivation by $\delta_b$. The added pivot assumptions vanish under this multiplier and the other axioms follow by multiplying their unguarded forms. A conditional contradiction yields $\delta_b$, and a conditional forbidden row yields $\delta_b\delta_a$. For an unconditional covered deletion sum the latter products over the pivot cube, using already forbidden pivot indicators for excluded pivot rows and multiplying contradiction indicators where required. The pivot indicators sum to one. Each nesting level adds at most $k$ to degree, while sequential deletion rounds need not accumulate degree because the new row indicators themselves have degree at most $k$. This proves the claimed simulation in multilinear PC (ordinary PC can allow an extra degree for explicit Boolean reduction). On direct 3-CNF charts a clause is exactly one forbidden indicator. Known unbounded PC degree lower bounds on random bounded-width CNFs over characteristic two, e.g. Alekhnovich--Razborov, \emph{Lower Bounds for Polynomial Calculus: Nonbinomial Case} (2003), and the boundary-expansion Theorem 5.1 in \href{https://doi.org/10.1145/3675668}{A Generalized Method for Proving Polynomial Calculus Degree Lower Bounds}, therefore rule out universal refutation by this fixed-scope, fixed-parameter portfolio. No lower bound on unrestricted decision follows, and no transfer to every native four-partition geometry is asserted.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed same-residual comparison.}
On the exact DR18 M24-reclosed tables \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9ac7e9d745b7220f0634990bd08d919313471f82}{9ac7e9d}, five distinct actual guards (DR17, BW17, M23 root and two actual $x_0$ pins), independent degree-one, degree-two and degree-three checks each delete zero additional rows, retaining 910/452/1190/926/926. Degree-three span ranks are 770/332/487/400/400. The checker multiplies acquired local degree-two identities by individual active coordinates, not full degree-three PC saturation. It verifies supplied residual and original lifted witnesses, including guards and source owners, not a new acquisition or independent reverse replay. Exhaustive controls: Fano UNSAT and its signed SAT neighbor (ten models) both stall; affine-plane UNSAT and one-line-deleted UNSAT both reject by an empty owner at degree three. All control models retained. Executed 75852 elimination XORs, 1271826 feature evaluations, 13878 row probes, 378120 pair row scans and 2514 multiplication calls. No native replay, full global solver, formal kernel, measured physical memory or manuscript compilation. Code \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/661e832c28a36530fafe96fdc64216265430956a}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/54dc413690b25a4a4318833befa3cc139d70d64d}{results}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/76cebdff5204729ce93f7f465632d139b27c7237}{report}. No speed claim.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
Use the identical DR18/M24 tables, actual guard and GD24/BS17 source lifts, with S23 original owner IDs. Algebra/Hub/MS: stronger nonlocal quadratic or integer certificates rather than merely another fixed degree; Matching/Interfaces: actual joint row occupation and nonlocal cut; Wheels: exact native lift under scope-changing moves; GD/BUILD DAG: charged factored coverage; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: audit the fixed-degree simulation and scope growth, find simultaneous hostile inputs; Solver/Synthesis: direct-first versus alternating paid deductions, reclose on any accepted change, preserve common credit and exchange source maps and failures. The requested contributions are not presumed executed. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Guarded dual row-pair closure and paid compatibility edges (BD18)}\label{sec:bd18}
GD24/M24 and DR18 give an authenticated table residual with actual guards and source lifts. Rather than repeating single-row probes, retain conditional compatibility between pairs of actual owner rows. This is classical binary CSP path consistency (Mackworth, 1977, \href{https://doi.org/10.1016/0004-3702(77)90007-8}{doi:10.1016/0004-3702(77)90007-8}) applied to the dual row-selector representation, not a new general consistency theorem. We establish its exact integration and its limitation on the DO01 raw cores.
\begin{lemma}[Exact guarded row-pair closure and reserve]\label{lem:bd18-pairs}
Let $A_i$ be explicit nonempty allowed tables on actual scopes $V_i$ after absorbing the guard or representing it by a genuine exact constraint. Let $R=\sum_i|A_i|$. Make a vertex for each owner-row identity and connect rows of different owners precisely when they agree on all shared actual coordinates. A satisfying assignment is equivalent, up to unused coordinates and existing lift maps, to a clique selecting one row from every owner. Repeatedly delete a row with no neighbor in some other owner, or an edge whose two ends have no common live neighbor in a third owner. All such deletions preserve every satisfying transversal. Empty owner certifies UNSAT; a full transversal clique certifies SAT. Saturation and replay have polynomial bit cost in the explicit registry size, not necessarily in an earlier implicit encoding.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Agreement of every selected pair gives a well-defined value on each coordinate of the union, and conversely every solution supplies exactly such a selection. Induct on deletion events: a solution containing the deleted row would supply its missing neighbor, and a solution containing both ends of the deleted edge would supply the missing common neighbor. Each event identifies the owner with no support in the actual current registry; an independent replay scans this owner's live rows and verifies absence using already certified edges. Never reuse a guarded deletion outside its guard without an additional proof. A noncoordinate guard cannot be replaced by independent coordinate marginals. There are at most $R+\binom R2$ deletion events. With $m\le R$ nonempty owners and maximum encoded row/scope length $L$, a full pass tests at most $O(R^2mR)$ Boolean support entries, plus initialization, indexing and row deletion; $O((R+L+1)^7)$ bit work is a conservative bound including replay, with polynomial storage. An interrupted sweep is sound, but not a fixed point. Run unary closure before, after each accepted batch (or each edge under an immediate worklist), and before branch or return. No semantic-identity oracle is involved.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{DR18 aggregate integration.} With a fixed polynomial original-input row cap $R_{\max}(N)$, keep declared observation scopes and let $q=(1+\operatorname{rank}(\text{declared span}))^4$ and $h=\#\text{live rows}+\#\text{live pair edges}$. Choose $B\ge R_{\max}+R_{\max}^2$ a single polynomial bound. Strict pair/row deletion at fixed $q$ decreases $h$, hence DR18's $W=(B+1)(q-1)+h+1$ decreases. An aggregate split with strictly decreasing total $q$ is still paid by the same reserve. This is a typed extension: arbitrary re-encoding, merging, or resetting edges at fixed $q$ is not free, and cap acquisition, all edge consumers, verification, reconstruction and failed probes remain charged. On an individual preflight-bounded closure job time and peak space are polynomial and $CM\le(T+1)S$ on the same serial execution. It neither establishes a polynomial number of reachable charts nor an eligible move on every chart.
\begin{lemma}[All raw linear signed NAE registries are dual path-consistent]\label{lem:bd18-stall}
For an unpinned linear 3-uniform signed NAE registry, the original dual row graph is already at a row-and-pair closure fixed point, irrespective of satisfiability.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Distinct owners share at most one coordinate. Every restriction of a signed NAE table to at most two coordinates has full support: the two forbidden words are complements, so fixing two coordinates rules out at most one of the two possible completions. One row fixes at most one coordinate of another owner. Two compatible rows fix at most two coordinates of any third owner; hence they have a common neighbor there. No row or edge deletion is possible. The Fano UNSAT instance and its SAT sign/deletion neighbors satisfy these hypotheses. This is a restriction-specific failure, not a decision lower bound or a simultaneous hard sequence against the whole portfolio.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Combined fixed-scope degree firewall]\label{lem:bd18-firewall}
