For fixed maximum owner arity $k$, the row-and-pair closure of Lemma~\ref{lem:bd18-pairs} can be simulated in multilinear polynomial calculus over $\mathbb F_2$ with degree at most $3k$. Interleaving it with I24 moment/semijoin closure and fixed-depth covered probes on the same scopes gives a degree bound $\max(d,3k)+k\ell$ under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:i24-firewall}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use $\delta_a$ for a full owner-word indicator. Maintain the equation $\delta_a=0$ for every absent row and $\delta_a\delta_b=0$ for every absent compatible pair; inconsistent overlaps have product identically zero in the Boolean ring. For an unsupported pair $a,b$ and third owner $j$, for every possible word $c$ on $V_j$ at least one of the following holds: $c$ is absent; $a,c$ is incompatible or absent; $b,c$ is incompatible or absent. Thus $\delta_a\delta_b\delta_c=0$ follows from a previously derived equation (or a Boolean identity) by multiplying remaining literals, within degree $3k$. Summing over all words $c$ gives $\delta_a\delta_b=0$, since $\sum_c\delta_c=1$. An unsupported row is the same argument with only one other owner. An empty owner yields $1=0$. For constant $k$ the individual replays enumerate at most $2^k$ words. I24's local equations, products and covered-discharge argument can use these already derived indicators; sequential rounds do not accumulate degree because each retained nogood has degree at most $2k$. Multiplying a guarded derivation by its pivot indicator adds at most $k$ per nesting level, as in I24. This establishes the stated bound, including the pair equations if retained through probes. By the fixed-width direct-CNF degree lower bounds invoked in I24, even the combined fixed-arity, fixed-degree, fixed-depth deletion-only system is not a universal refutation procedure. Scope-changing normalizations, arbitrary guards, different proof systems and unrestricted decision are not excluded.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed same-chart integration.} Input DR18-reclosed M24 tables (9ac7e9d), original GD24 (a06b25d), BS17 (d0957c4), S23 (4088130) and actual separate guards. Across DR17, BW17, M23 root and its two actual pins, additional row deletions 12/0/12/7/7 and explicit pair deletions 81604/9452/126950/90756/90756; 70800074 third-owner tests. DR17 and M23 root stop at the 25000000-test cap and reclose unary support, three others saturate. All five root labels remain OPEN under this closure. Direct versus combined dual row-selector search obtains all five SAT labels with identical node counts 8/6/11/7/10, and support-test totals 564959 versus 430928 (excluding the pair preprocessing). All ten acquired witnesses independently lift through GD24 and BS17 to original S23 owners and actual pins. No speed claim. Fano and deletion stall (128-assignment reference counts 0/10), inconsistent equality/parity triangle is rejected, satisfiable equality triangle preserved. No general complete solver, native replay, formal kernel, or physical peak/CM measurement. Exact per-guard bitmaps, replay and costs in \texttt{research/constructive-shared-hubs/multisign-dag/2026-11-25\_0021\_BD18*}.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.} Use the identical BD18 bitmaps, actual scope order, guard and GD24/BS17/S23 lifts. Algebra/Hub/MS: nonlinear identities or low-rank seams from exact deleted pairs; Matching/Interfaces: actual joint occupancy/syndrome; Wheels: native transport of verified words and pair constraints; GD/BUILD DAG: factor bitmaps with paid guards and consumer edges; Potential/Obstruction/Red Team: attack reserve resets and hostile fixed points; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first with alternating paid moves, reclose after deductions, exchange maps and witnesses. These are requests, not presumed executed. Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Integer square-score feedback and a high-girth obstruction (BA18)}\label{sec:ba18}
\begin{lemma}[Paid integer square-score closure]\label{lem:ba18-square}
On an explicit guarded table chart, select a coordinate set $S$ of size $k$ and signs $w_v\in\{-1,1\}$, putting $z_v=w_v(-1)^{x_v}$. Allocate each pair in $S$ to one owner containing it if possible, with $q$ unowned pairs. For actual owner row $a$, let $f_i(a)$ be the sum of its allocated pair products $z_uz_v$, and $A_i=\{f_i(a):a\in R_i\}$. Every solution obeys
\[
 \sum_i f_i(a_i)+u\in H_k=\{((k-2j)^2-k)/2:0\le j\le k\},\quad
 u\in\{-q,-q+2,\ldots,q\}.
\]
Independent-score Minkowski-sum dynamic programming rejects an empty intersection with $H_k$ and deletes each row whose score lies on no viable path. These are sound deductions preserving the complete actual guarded support, not a sufficient feasibility test. A polynomial number of such squares and repeated certified closure after each deletion cost polynomially in the explicitly acquired chart length, and strict row deletions fit DR18's same-span credit.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Expand $(\sum z_v)^2=k+2\sum_{u<v}z_uz_v$. Each actual solution selects one actual row per owner and one attainable missing-pair sum, so it induces a successful path. Forward attainable sums and backward viable prefixes compute all eligible scores. Every partial sum lies in $[-\binom{k}{2},\binom{k}{2}]$, giving $O(k^2)$ states per layer and $O((m+1)k^4)$ direct transitions; row scoring, ownership discovery and guard checks are additional polynomial scans. The cheaper upper bound is $U=q+\sum_i\max A_i$: reject $U<-\lfloor k/2\rfloor$ or delete a row when replacing its owner's maximum by its score violates this lower bound. At most the initial number of rows strict deletion batches occur. No new active coordinates or rows are introduced; DP tables are scratch space, original lifts retained. Serial polynomial time and storage give $CM\le(T+1)S$. The acquisition cost of the input chart and subsequent search are not discharged by this lemma.
\end{proof}
For positive linear NAE on $k$ selected vertices containing $t$ whole triples, $U\le\binom{k}{2}-4t$. This rejects Fano and the nine-variable affine plane (even with one line deleted). A single-sign Fano neighbor necessarily has original-coordinate monochromatic values on its changed owner: otherwise all seven owners contribute $-1$, which violates the square bound. This is a nonlinear certified deduction missed by the individual GF(2) feature relaxation.
\begin{lemma}[Universal single-square progress is false]\label{lem:ba18-stall}
For any unguarded linear signed three-uniform NAE chart of maximum degree $D$ and Berge girth $g>4D+10$, every signed unit square of Lemma~\ref{lem:ba18-square}, even with any one accepted owner row fixed, has a successful relaxed score path. The same holds if one owner is relaxed to a superset of its NAE relation. Thus this deletion-only closure cannot universally decide the raw registry, including sufficiently late DO09 critical cores and their single-owner SAT neighbors.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $t$ be the number of triples entirely inside $S$, $F=\binom{k}{2}-3t$. Pair ownership is unique. Every full owner can choose score $-1$ for any signs, and any fixed full row scores $-1$ or $3$. Each remaining pair is a missing pair or the only selected pair of its owner, hence has both signs available independently except for a possible fixed partial row. For $k\ge4D+8$, $t\le Dk/3$. The relaxed sum set contains a step-two interval whose lower endpoint is at most $-\binom{k}{2}+2t+4$ and upper endpoint at least $\binom{k}{2}-4t-2$. These straddle $-\lfloor k/2\rfloor$, with its correct parity $\binom{k}{2}\bmod2$. For $k<4D+8$, the full owners and the fixed row's owner involve at most $k+3\le4D+10$ vertices. Their incidence graph is a forest by girth. Rooting at the fixed owner and greedily extending each new triple through at most one earlier vertex realizes that row and all full-owner constraints; unconstrained selected vertices are arbitrary. Partial owners realize the resulting pair products individually, missing pairs match them, giving a successful path. The relaxed owner still permits every NAE extension and its additional fixed rows are also covered. Original singleton/pair affine and semijoin closure stagnates on the unguarded raw linear NAE registry. This excludes neither arbitrary integer weights nor joint-square coupling, scope-changing transformations, other algorithms, or unrestricted polynomial SAT.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed same-chart comparison.} On immutable DR18/M24-reclosed source \texttt{9ac7e9d745b7220f0634990bd08d919313471f82}, five distinct actual guard jobs have 910/452/1190/926/926 rows. Both upper-bound and exact score-DP closure add zero deletions, with all ten supplied residual witnesses checked after closure (not acquired). The finite Fano and affine-plane controls and all twelve plane deletions reject; all 21 Fano single-sign neighbors each lose four rows and preserve ten models; seven Fano line deletions preserve ten models each. Independent finite exhaustive support checks agree. 12466 square probes, 10618296 pair evaluations, 5835830 DP transitions, 136 closure scans. Code and raw evidence at \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/blob/184180737ec794d61c4310b30945369b2ca2c895/research/constructive-shared-hubs/hub-algebra/2026-11-25_0026_BA18.js}{checker} and \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/184180737ec794d61c4310b30945369b2ca2c895}{results}. No new source-lift replay, full solver, kernel verification or measured physical CM. U-PROGRESS remains OPEN. Joint integer score/occupancy constraints and scope-changing transformations are requested on the same authenticated charts; no peer result presumed executed.


\section{Pair-registry normalization and the no-fill safety barrier (DS18)}\label{sec:ds18}
The first open universal dependency is still Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}, not source encoding or the conditional aggregate theorem. Integrate M24's row selectors, DR18's common credit, and I24's stagnant moment closure on the identical five DR18/M24-reclosed charts. The following is a paid stronger closure and a barrier to a tempting complete no-fill oracle, not a universal solver.

\begin{lemma}[Exact pair registry and locally recognizable no-fill elimination]\label{lem:ds18-tree}
On an explicitly acquired coordinate-table chart with coordinate guards absorbed, let selector $i$ have domain $D_i$ of its actual accepted rows. Let $C_{ij}$ be the complete binary registry (initially equality on the full overlap). Arc consistency deletes unsupported rows; path consistency deletes $(a,b)$ in $C_{ij}$ if no current row of some third selector $k$ supports both. These are exact deletions relative to the actual guard. At their common fixed point, selector $k$ may be eliminated without adding any constraint if the family
\[
{\cal S}_k=\{\{c\in D_k:(a,c)\in C_{ik}\}:i\ne k,\ a\in D_i\}
\]
consists of connected subsets of one tree on $D_k$. The condition is deterministically recognizable in polynomial time, and any solution of the remaining registry lifts. No complete semantic closure is assumed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A selection of one row per owner satisfying all initial pair relations glues to a unique word on the union of scopes, and conversely; unused coordinates may be chosen subject to the actual coordinate pins. Every complete solution survives each consistency deletion. The registry retains every previous necessary pair restriction. At a fixed point, every pair of supports chosen by a feasible assignment of the remaining selectors intersects, by path consistency, and each individual support is nonempty by arc consistency. Connected subsets of a tree have the Helly property: root the tree, choose the deepest closest-to-root vertex among the selected subtrees, and use pairwise intersection to see it belongs to every selected subtree. Thus choose a common row for $k$. For zero remaining selectors choose any row; for one remaining selector use arc consistency. Keep the actual support and domain maps at elimination; reverse order reconstructs a word satisfying all eliminated edges. Later restrictions on survivors can only strengthen the premises. This is a decision-equivalent projection, not an assertion that eliminated row values are forced.

For recognition, discard duplicate supports and full-domain supports if desired. On the complete graph on $D_k$, weight $\{u,v\}$ by the number of supports containing both. For every spanning tree $T$, its weight is
\[
 w(T)=\sum_{S\in{\cal S}_k}|E(T[S])|
 \le \sum_{S\in{\cal S}_k}(|S|-1).
\]
Each induced graph is a forest. Equality holds precisely when every nonempty support induces a connected graph. A maximum spanning tree therefore decides the condition, including a numerical deficit when it fails. Empty domains reject; singleton domains are trivial. This is an explicit use of the established tree-convex consistency principle (Zhang, \emph{Consistency and Set Intersection}, IJCAI 2003, \href{https://ijcai.org/proceedings/2003/39}{paper}); compare Cooper--Jeavons--Salamon, \emph{Generalizing constraint satisfaction on trees}, AI 2010, \href{https://www.cs.ox.ac.uk/publications/publication4995-abstract.html}{paper}. We do not claim novelty for tree convexity or path consistency.

Let $R$ be the original total number of acquired rows. There are at most $R^2$ live pair entries, and at most $R$ live rows and $R$ nonempty selectors. Each strict deletion or elimination decreases this count. Repeated full AC/PC scans, failed tree tests, support weight construction, maximum spanning trees, bit-mask operations, all guard and row identity checks, and retained reconstruction maps are polynomial in explicit input length. A conservative naive bit-operation bound for the frozen chart is $O((L+2)^8)$ when $L$ includes its explicit input rows, scopes and pins; this includes repeat scans and all lifts, but not unbounded recursive fallback. Polynomial peak space and cumulative memory follow on the same serial realization. Stop with UNKNOWN if no further permitted move or paid decision applies. The certificate does not cover every residual.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Complete no-fill safety is coNP-complete even after path closure]\label{lem:ds18-nofill}
For general explicit binary CSPs, deciding whether \emph{every} feasible assignment of the remaining selectors extends to a specified selector $k$ is coNP-complete, even on an arc- and path-consistent star with Boolean leaves and unrestricted leaf-leaf relations.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A countercertificate is one feasible remaining assignment whose support intersection is empty; it is polynomially checkable. Reduce from UNSAT of non-tautological CNF $F$, adding three unit clauses on fresh distinct variables. This preserves satisfiability. The center has one value per resulting clause, and leaf $i$ takes a Boolean value $b$. Its support is the list of clauses not yet satisfied by the literal of variable $i$ set to $b$ (including clauses not containing that variable). A complete leaf word extends to the center precisely when at least one clause is unsatisfied. Hence every leaf word extends iff the padded formula is UNSAT. Arc consistency holds: each center clause can be falsified on any one leaf, and each leaf choice leaves at least one of the three new units untouched. Every pair of leaf choices leaves at least one new unit untouched, so extends to the center. Every allowed center-leaf pair extends to every other leaf independently, since each clause is nontautological. All other triples are unrestricted; this is path consistency. Construction and checking are polynomial. The star itself is always satisfiable (pick any center clause and falsify it), so this is an obstruction to assuming complete projection-safety recognition is cheap, NOT a decision lower bound, and not a hardness claim for the genuine native tetravalent subclass. The reduction neither asserts that arbitrary binary compatibility tables already arise as raw equality-on-overlap charts nor rules out sound incomplete recognition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Universal pair-tree progress fails on genuine linear NAE charts]\label{lem:ds18-stall}
For a signed linear 3-uniform NAE registry in which every vertex occurs in at least two owners, the unpinned raw six-row selector encoding is already arc- and path-consistent, and no selector has the tree-convex support certificate of Lemma~\ref{lem:ds18-tree}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Any pair of owners overlaps in at most one coordinate. To extend an allowed pair to a third owner, at most two distinct coordinates of that owner are fixed consistently; every such word extends to signed NAE. A single row also extends to every other owner. Hence AC/PC makes no deletions. For each of the three coordinates of a pivot owner, each value occurs in exactly three of its six rows; since the coordinate occurs elsewhere and overlaps are linear, both corresponding three-element supports occur. The six supports are distinct. A tree simultaneously connecting them needs at least $6(3-1)=12$ total induced edges counted with multiplicity. Distinct rows agree on at most two coordinates, so each tree edge belongs to at most two of the six supports; a six-vertex tree has only five edges, giving at most ten. Contradiction. This excludes the tree certificate on both the original Fano UNSAT registry and its single-sign SAT neighbors, not nonlocal rewrites, actual symmetry quotient, other certificates, or unrestricted decision.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Combined charged ledger and affine feedback.}
Let $W$ be the DR18 common credit of Lemma~\ref{lem:dr18-credit}. Fix a polynomial bound $P$ on the number of live pair entries for all admitted charts (e.g. the square of a fixed row cap). Let $p=P+1$ before pair acquisition, and $p$ equal the live pair count after acquisition, with $0\le p\le P$. The refined credit
\[
 \widehat W=(P+2)(W-1)+p+1
\]
strictly decreases for a one-time acquisition or any pair-only deletion at unchanged $W$; the same mixed-radix argument pays every child of an already strictly aggregate-decreasing $W$ dispatch. Row and selector deletion decrease the secondary active count where the declared span does not increase. No resetting to an uncompiled chart at unchanged credit is allowed. A compiled phase carries its pair restrictions and reverse lift maps; S23/other chart dispatches require an exact transport or certified equivalent representation and a fresh credit check, not merely dropping the pair edges. This pays the additional pair work but does not prove universal eligible progress. Pair masks are joint constraints, not independent occupancies. The affine hull of surviving actual compatible pair words on the union of the two scopes gives necessary equations on original IDs; all such hulls can be acquired from at most $R^2$ words (early stop once full rank), combined by Gaussian elimination, and used to filter rows. Reclose AC/PC after accepted deductions. This feedback is polynomial under the same cap; it can stop without new information. No unrestricted affine guard adapter is asserted here.

\paragraph{Executed same-source comparison and provenance.}
Five distinct actual DR17/BW17/M23 root and $x_0=0,1$ guarded charts were read from immutable DR18/M24-reclosed registry 9ac7e9d, using exact GD24/BS17 and S23 source/lift maps. Direct row-selector AC plus capped search versus AC/PC plus tree tests and the same capped search: both obtained all five SAT witnesses and independently reversed the lifts to the source. Combined deletes respectively 81450/9452/126914/90756/90756 pair entries, and 12/0/12/7/7 rows (399328 pair entries, 38 rows total); all 105 final tree tests fail. Direct and combined each visit 42 search nodes; 49349416 pair-extension tests in the combined root closure, no speed claim. Local affine-hull and then joint actual pair-hull feedback yield no further row or pin deductions beyond the supplied guards (rank 0/0/0/1/1), so the post-pair fixed point persists. Fano UNSAT and deletion SAT both require the capped search; the inconsistent parity triangle rejects under PC, and the equality path eliminates all three selectors with verified reconstruction. No planted witness input. The deduplicated checker verifies 5022 assertions; an earlier artifact repeated the five charts because the source has two pair-mode records per guard, so use the deduplicated evidence. Separate star reduction controls: 82 small source formulas, one UNSAT and 81 SAT; 23512 assertions check the no-fill identity and star arc/path premises. No full portfolio, native replay, formal kernel, manuscript compilation or measured physical cumulative memory. Bit-cost upper bounds apply only to the admitted polynomial-size chart phases. Exact pair masks (actual row bit indexes, guards and owner scopes) and results are available in \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/95b6fa097cebf52cdb045bc086ce9331eb74afc8}{unique-results registry}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/29780d088951357109724df77a8908813441e77b}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/84e7157801851cac0515af2870a88e2fd16eed6f}{pair-hull feedback}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/8b8b54e437498b30ab70e0dacefbf495ba470847}{star controls}. Same-chart pair restrictions now supply new exact cross-owner information even where all unary affine and moment tests stagnate.

\paragraph{Parallel stagnation and diagonal scope.}
Use the five exact pair registries linked in the unique-results file, including original row indices, actual separate guards, GD24/BS17 lifts and immutable S23 source. Algebra/Hub/MS: nonlinear identities on surviving actual pair words, not only individual owners; Matching/Interfaces: impose pair masks as joint occupancy/syndrome constraints, or prove an exact compact transport; Wheels: native realization of surviving pair restrictions with charged port maps; GD/BUILD DAG: reuse exact conditional pair support without free coverage, target compatible support intersection and factored witnesses; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: audit the new mixed-radix cap and construct hostile fixed points or a stronger paid elimination certificate; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first with alternating pair, algebra and matching deductions on these same charts, reclose and preserve guards. These are requests, not presumed peer execution. A diagonal sequence for a fixed total portfolio eventually avoids any certified fully eliminating domain that is actually acquired and executed in a fixed polynomial, but need not avoid the unrecognized safe deletions. The new theorem falsifies universal local pair-tree progress and isolates the complete no-fill oracle as a circular shortcut. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain open.


\section{Guarded pair-fiber affine cancellation and coverage audit (MS24)}\label{sec:ms24}
The load-bearing target is Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}. Use the same actual explicit tables, row IDs, pair bitmaps and separate guards as BD18, with DR18's fixed original-input reserve, not a new independently normalized benchmark.
\begin{lemma}[Exact guarded pair-fiber affine deduction]\label{lem:ms24-fiber}
Let $R_j$ be live explicit rows of owner $j$ on scope $V_j$, with certified pair compatibility graph $E$ preserving every legal word, and exact guard $G$. For a live row $r\in R_i$, put $D_i(r)=\{r\}$ and $D_j(r)=\{s\in R_j:(r,s)\in E\}$ for $j\ne i$. Define
\[
 H_r=G\cap\bigcap_j\pi_j^{-1}(\operatorname{aff}_{\mathbb F_2}D_j(r)).
\]
The hull of an empty set is empty. If $H_r=\varnothing$, delete $r$. Otherwise delete $(r,s)$ whenever $s\notin\pi_j(H_r)$. These are sound exact-support-preserving deletions. For any fully covered owner, every equation of $\operatorname{aff}(\bigcup_{r\in R_i}H_r)$ is unconditional under the current guard. All these operations admit polynomial bit-time discovery and replay in the explicit chart length.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every legal word using $r$ has its other owner rows in the corresponding $D_j(r)$, so it belongs to $H_r$. This proves every deletion sound and, since no new rows or pairs are introduced, preserves exact support of the authenticated registry; no sufficiency claim is made for affine feasibility. Compute local hulls by Gaussian elimination of row differences, lift their necessary equations to actual coordinates and solve the combined affine system, then project its affine solution set to each actual target scope. A complete pivot cover is a disjunction over the actual owner rows. The union hull is generated by all nonempty $H_r$ kernel bases and anchor differences; it is not the actual union support. Empty cover certifies UNSAT. A full sweep has at most $R=\sum_j|R_j|$ pivots, each polynomial in the explicit chart. At most $R+R(R-1)/2$ strict deletions occur on fixed scopes. Reclose unconditional unary support and affine hulls after each accepted batch and at stagnation; a capped incomplete sweep is not a fixed point. DR18's $W=(B+1)(q-1)+h+1$, with fixed polynomial $B\ge R_{\max}+R_{\max}^2$, $h=$ live rows plus live edges, pays every strict same-$q$ deletion. Gaussian scratch, failed probes, stored guards, certificate replay, consumer accesses and existing lifts are charged; an active fused-equation update needs its own reserve accounting. No new scope or row is created by deletions. On an admitted polynomial-size chart this serial closure has polynomial time and peak storage, hence $CM\le(T+1)S$ on the same execution. Neither chart acquisition nor universal subsequent progress follows.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Strict cooperative gain and raw obstruction]\label{lem:ms24-stall}
There are explicit arity-four table charts on which joint unconditional affine hull and BD18 pair path consistency both stagnate but Lemma~\ref{lem:ms24-fiber} forces a gate. Conversely, every unpinned linear signed NAE-3 chart is already a fixed point of this single-pivot conditional hull rule, even with full owner-cover fusion.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Take triples $(012),(034),(135),(245)$ and a common gate $g$. Each owner permits every triple word if $g=1$; if $g=0$ it requires XOR zero except for the fourth owner, which requires XOR one. Each local hull is full because of the unrestricted slice and one word on the other slice. Any two compatible rows fix the gate and at most two bits of any third triple, so a parity completion always exists. Thus ordinary path consistency is fixed. But any pivot with $g=0$ activates all four parity equations; each triple variable occurs twice, so their sum is $0=1$. All gate-zero rows are deleted and $g=1$ is forced. The resulting source has exactly $2^6$ legal words.
For the raw linear signed NAE chart, fixing a pivot row fixes at most one bit of any other owner. The six-word NAE relation has full affine hull; a one-bit fiber contains three of four words and has full relative affine hull. Thus $H_r$ consists of exactly the pivot pins, every compatible neighbor passes, and the union hull over a full pivot owner is the entire source chart. No deletion or equation is obtained. This includes Fano and its deletion neighbor, but Fano is rejected by MS21/BA18's different PSD/square channel. BA18's stated high-girth linear family also stalls under this affine rule; no assertion that arbitrary other channels stall simultaneously is made.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Combined fixed-scope firewall.} In the Boolean multilinear ring retain indicator equations $\delta_r=0$ and $\delta_r\delta_s=0$ for certified deleted rows and pairs, respectively. In the fiber of $r$, an affine equation $l$ on a target owner is derived using $\delta_r l=\sum_s l(s)\delta_r\delta_s$ over its complete local word cube: each nonzero summand is a known forbidden row/pair product or is identically zero from overlap inconsistency. The pivot pins and coordinate guards have analogous products. Linear combination derives $\delta_r=0$ in an inconsistent fiber, or, by multiplication with a violating target indicator, $\delta_r\delta_s=0$. Covered fusion sums $\delta_r l=0$ over a fully covered pivot cube, using earlier forbidden pivot equations on missing rows. For fixed maximum arity $k$, the resulting multilinear derivations have degree at most $3k+1$ (a conservative allowance for intermediate multiplication), and add to the I24/BD18 combined fixed-depth bound $\max(d,3k+1)+k\ell$. Thus the same fixed-arity, fixed-degree, fixed-depth deletion-only subportfolio on direct bounded-width CNFs is not universally refutationally complete, by the degree lower bounds cited in I24. This is an audit of a specified subportfolio, not a lower bound on arbitrary algorithms, physical compilers, arbitrary affine guards or scope-changing transformations. Integer and PSD channels not simulated by this argument.
\paragraph{Executed joint comparison.} On the immutable DR18/M24 reclosed tables (9ac7e9d) and the five actual BD18 exported pair states, run at most 128 pivot probes per chart and mode, with unconditional reclosure after accepted batches. On DR17/BW17/M23 root/M23 actual pin0/pin1, standalone conditional hull from ordinary overlap edges deletes 0/2861/4085/2129/2129 pairs; after BD18 deletes 4/0/0/16/16 additional pairs. No additional row deletions, no root terminal, all ten passes capped; all supplied residual witness rows and pairs and guards verified. Upstream acquisition not free, no speed or new source-witness claim. The seven-bit gate control forces the gate (16 rows deleted, 64 exhaustive reference models); opposite-label gated controls have 0/16 models and agree with the baseline/combined closure. Fano and deletion have 0/10 models and both stall. Exact checker and guarded symmetric pair-delta data: \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/b15192f292beaafbd9b30aed443447d9f77c1dbd}{code}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ecbbf26c6a5d9b2b53f5942b1889131c3ebe1fce}{results}. The covered affine-fusion extension is symbolic, not executed. No rerun of I24 or BD18 after these new edges, reverse physical lift, full solver, kernel check, or measured physical CM.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation and next dependency.} Use precisely these pair deltas with the same owner IDs, actual guards, GD24/BS17/S23 source maps. Algebra/Hub/MS: full covered fusion and joint integer-square cuts or genuine nonlocal seam; Matching/Interfaces: rerun actual joint moment-syndrome using pair nogoods; Wheels: authenticate native transport and lift; GD/BUILD DAG: reclose path compatibility with paid factored consumers; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: audit fixed-scope firewall and reserve resets, seek scope-changing hostile controls; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first and alternating operations on identical states, charging all failures. Requests only. The combined deletion-only obstruction means the universal theorem must establish either an additional uniformly acquired scope-changing/nonlocal normalization or a distinct exact decision method with the aggregate budget; no such theorem is currently proved. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Lifted selector-component cancellation and odd-wheel obstruction (RT24)}\label{sec:rt24}
Use exactly the BD18 five pair registries and MS24 symmetric pair deltas, with actual guards separate and original DR18/GD24/BS17/S23 source maps retained. The target is Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}; no new active scope is required for the following scratch calculation.
\begin{lemma}[Complete pair-local linear relaxation in row indicators]\label{lem:rt24-selector}
For live rows $R_i$, introduce scratch selectors $z_r$ and equations $\sum_{r\in R_i}z_r=1$ over $\mathbb F_2$. For each connected component $C$ of the bipartite allowed-pair graph between $R_i,R_j$, add $\sum_{r\in C}z_r=0$. Empty support rejects. These are exactly the affine-hull equations of the pair's one-hot edge vectors together with the owner odd-total equations. Their global combination admits polynomial bit-time discovery, replay and exact linear-relaxation projection to individual selectors and pairs, and gives sound row and pair deletions or contradiction under the actual guard.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
An actual selected pair is an allowed edge; its endpoints belong to one component and all other components have zero selected endpoints. For completeness of the pair-local linear equations, the span of the vectors $e_r+e_s$ of graph edges consists of precisely the even-weight words separately on each connected component: tree paths generate all endpoint differences. Intersect with left-total one to select precisely the odd linear combinations, which are the affine hull. Isolated vertices have zero selectors. Combine the component and owner equations by Gaussian elimination. Inconsistency rejects. Otherwise express each selector as an affine function of free selectors. Constant zero rejects its row; constant one forces its row and excludes the others of that owner. An allowed pair is impossible in this relaxation exactly if one expression is zero or their XOR is constant one (two distinct nonconstant linear parts over $\mathbb F_2$ are independent). Every actual legal transversal satisfies all these equations, hence deletions preserve exact guarded support and earlier witness lifts. Odd total does not imply exactly one, and consistent abstract selectors are not SAT witnesses.
For $R$ registered rows there are at most $O(R^2)$ pair incidences and component equations; conservative dense elimination costs $O(R^4)$ bit operations per sweep, with polynomial scratch and certificate storage. Apply guard filtering and unconditional semijoin closure first, after each accepted batch and at stagnation; retain actual guards on all conditional facts. At most $R+R(R-1)/2$ strict row/edge deletions occur on fixed scopes. With the fixed original-input cap for DR18 covering rows and edges, every accepted batch decreases $h$ at unchanged $q$ or qualifies for a separately proved base-credit decrease, with no reset. Charge failed scans, provenance replay, consumer verification and retained lifts. Admitted serial sweeps have polynomial $T,S$ and $CM\le(T+1)S$. No universal chart acquisition or subsequent progress follows.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Strict cooperative gain and infinite opposing-label fixed points]\label{lem:rt24-family}
There are fixed-arity explicit table charts with opposite-label single-owner neighbors on which raw coordinate hull, arc/path consistency and MS24 single-pivot coordinate-hull closure all stagnate, but selector-component cancellation rejects the UNSAT label. Conversely there are infinite opposite-label pair registries on which even selector-component, arc and path closure all stagnate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
On any connected simple cubic graph, each edge carries a three-bit word whose phase is its majority bit. A vertex owner permits the nine-bit words with XOR of its three incident phases equal to a charge $b_v$. Both phase fibers have four words and full three-bit affine hull (the zero fiber is $000,001,010,100$, and the one fiber its complement). Each owner has full coordinate hull; fixing a compatible pair of other owner rows fixes at most two incident words of any third owner, and the last realizes either phase. Hence arc/path consistency stagnate. A single pivot fixes at most one word of any neighbor, leaving full relative hull on the other words; MS24 single-pivot hull is exactly the pivot pins and covered fusion adds no equation.
For adjacent owners the pair components correspond to their exact common edge words. Sum component equations over majority-one words to identify the phase selector sums at the two endpoints. At each vertex the sum of its phase selectors is $b_v$ times its odd-total equation. Sum over vertices; each phase cancels twice, giving $0=\sum_v b_v$. Odd charge is UNSAT by Lemma~\ref{lem:rt24-selector}; even charge is SAT by the ordinary connected graph incidence parity system and choosing any word of each realized phase. For even charge the exact source count is $2^{|E|-|V|+1}4^{|E|}$. Prisms of arbitrary size give the first infinite family by flipping one charge. This recognized family is easy, not a lower bound; no assertion against arbitrary higher-degree moment or integer rules.
For the converse use a three-valued selector owner per vertex of an odd-rim wheel, with distinct colors on each edge and unrestricted nonedges. Each pair graph is connected, either $K_{3,3}$ or $K_{3,3}$ minus its diagonal, so the component equation is redundant with the two owner odd-total equations. Every row and allowed pair extends to that relaxation; arc/path closure also stalls since at most two colors are forbidden by two selected neighbors. The wheel is UNSAT: the hub consumes one color and the remaining two cannot alternate on an odd cycle. Deleting any rim edge permits alternation along a path; deleting any spoke permits its rim endpoint to take the hub color, and the remaining path alternates. All these SAT neighbors also have the stated fixed point. This is a restricted pair-registry obstruction, with an inexpensive structural wheel escape, not an obstruction to the entire cooperative portfolio or a strict native compilation theorem.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Independent same-chart audit.}
Before and after importing the exact MS24 combined pair deltas on the five BD18 charts, verified live-mask subset/symmetry, ran row arc closure, component elimination and one/two-selector tests. Live counts 898/452/1178/919/919 and ranks 150/163/175/159/159 are unchanged; all ten passes OPEN with no extra deletion or forced selector. Distinct M23 guards are retained. No independent certification of upstream deductions or source witness replay. Nine odd-wheel controls (rim 3,5,7, intact and two deletion types) independently enumerate three-color words: zero models on roots and six on each deletion, all selector passes stall. Two six-vertex prism controls use exhaustive 512-word phase enumeration: even 16 phase solutions and 4194304 coordinate solutions by multiplicity, odd zero; selector elimination returns OPEN and UNSAT respectively, on 1536 rows and 84 equations. Prism path and pivot fixed points are symbolic, not executed. No full solver, physical replay, manuscript compilation, kernel or measured physical CM. See \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/2c386af366a4e3e1ceb52bf39baf3dc4321d3246}{report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/ba2928377f66d6dec21d87dd1f5f3cf312b1bb8f}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/c83d5d3f97a35d5426899e1236bf96831e21fa32}{results}.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
Use the identical BD18 states 1fc8b6c/1e765e0/80b4721/37578c1/33f9839 and MS24 delta ecbbf26, with actual source and guard maps. Matching/Interfaces: enforce actual one-hot cardinality or joint integer/nonnegative marginals, not merely odd totals; Algebra/Hub/MS: nonlinear phase acquisition and coupled score identities; Wheels: structural odd-wheel escape and exact native selector transport; GD/BUILD DAG: factor component transport with charged coverage and consumers; Potential/Obstruction/Red Teams: audit the cap and seek simultaneous hostile charts after cheap wheel/phase normalization; Solver/Synthesis: compare direct-first against alternating actual deductions on these same inputs and replay source lifts. Requests only. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Helly projection, guarded no-fill covers and a high-girth obstruction (BH18)}\label{sec:bh18}
The load-bearing obligation remains \ref{obl:progress}. We extend DS18's tree-convex no-fill rule on the actual BD18 pair registry and MS24 deltas, without assuming complete projection-safety recognition. The following are exact typed reductions, not universal coverage.

\begin{lemma}[Exact Helly binary-fill projection]\label{lem:bh18-helly}
In an explicitly acquired binary owner-selector chart with all actual guards absorbed or exactly represented, let $D_i$ be a nonempty live owner domain and $S_i(r)=\{t\in D_i:t\text{ is compatible with exterior row }r\}$. After arc consistency, suppose the family of these supports is Helly: each pairwise intersecting subfamily has a common point. Delete each allowed exterior pair from distinct owners whose supports are disjoint, and remove $i$. The resulting chart is exactly the existential projection of the original chart onto surviving selectors. The hypothesis and the fill are deterministically recognizable in polynomial time in the explicit registry length.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Any original transversal survives. A transversal after fill selects pairwise intersecting, nonempty supports, and therefore has a common row of $D_i$, yielding a lift; the empty selection lifts by any row. Record domain and support maps for reverse reconstruction. At a path-consistency fixed point no new fill is needed. The Berge--Duchet criterion recognizes Helly families: for each three distinct elements $x,y,z\in D_i$, intersect all supports containing at least two of them, and require a nonempty intersection (empty collection intersection $D_i$). These supports meet pairwise, proving necessity. Conversely a minimal pairwise-intersecting family $E_1,\ldots,E_t$ with empty total intersection has $t\ge3$. Choose $x_j$ in all sets except $E_j$, for $j=1,2,3$. The $x_j$ are distinct and every $E_\ell$ contains at least two of them, contradicting the triple criterion. This is a classical Helly recognition argument; see Dourado, Grippo and Safe, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.12610}{arXiv:2201.12610}. The new integration is binary fill on authenticated owner charts, not a new general Helly theorem. Four supports $\{0,1\},\{1,2\},\{2,3\},\{3,0\}$, together with full supports, satisfy Helly but cannot all induce connected sets on a tree. Any nonempty domain of size at most two is eligible after AC.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Single-pivot guarded Helly no-fill cover]\label{lem:bh18-pivot}
For candidate owner $i$ and another actual owner $j$, for every $a\in D_j$ let $D_a=S_i(a)$. Use the nonempty restricted supports $S_i(r)\cap D_a$ of every row of any other exterior owner compatible with $a$. Require $D_a$ and all these supports nonempty, require intersecting restricted supports for every compatible pair of such rows from distinct owners, and require the family to be Helly. If all pivot rows pass, eliminating $i$ without fill gives the exact existential projection. Failed pair-intersection tests with three live pairwise-compatible rows $a,r,s$ give a certified ternary nogood, not an unconditional deletion of $(r,s)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every surviving exterior transversal selects one actual pivot row $a$. Its other selected rows have pairwise-intersecting restricted supports, hence have a common member of $D_a$. This is the required lift, retaining the actual guard and row IDs. The converse is deletion of an owner from a transversal. If $S_i(a)\cap S_i(r)\cap S_i(s)=\varnothing$, the three rows cannot occur jointly in any original transversal. This forbids $(r,s)$ only within the additional guard selecting $a$ (or symmetrically in either other orientation). A general non-Helly family alone does not prove its offending support sets correspond to compatible rows of distinct owners, much less a globally extendible exterior word.
For strictness over the unconditional Helly test, take $D_i=\{0,1,2\}$ and three exterior Boolean selectors with row-zero supports $\{0,1\},\{1,2\},\{0,2\}$ respectively and full row-one supports. Forbid the first and second exterior zero rows jointly. The full family is non-Helly, but conditioning on the first exterior row satisfies all hypotheses, so the center projects exactly without fill. Restoring the forbidden exterior pair invalidates this center projection. The no-fill condition is sufficient, not a complete safety oracle (cf.\ Lemma~\ref{lem:ds18-nofill}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Universal raw Helly and single-pivot progress fail]\label{lem:bh18-stall}
Every unpinned linear signed 3-NAE owner registry of minimum variable degree two has non-Helly support families at all owners. If the Berge girth exceeds four, every candidate and distinct pivot fail the single-pivot guarded cover. Both statements continue to hold if one owner admits one additional formerly forbidden word.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a center owner choose a forbidden word and one neighboring owner for each of its three coordinates, with row matching that coordinate of the forbidden word. The three neighbors are distinct by linearity; their supports are the three coordinate slices of the center domain. Every two intersect because all two-coordinate projections are full, but the total intersection is empty. If girth exceeds four, the three neighboring scopes are disjoint from one another (a shared vertex gives a Berge triangle). When the pivot meets the center, use the pivot as the chosen neighbor of that coordinate. Pick its matching row. The other two neighbor rows are compatible with it and each other, but their restricted supports have empty intersection. When the pivot is disjoint from the center, it meets at most one chosen neighboring scope (otherwise a Berge cycle of length at most four results). Select a pivot row matching that shared coordinate if necessary. The three neighbors are pairwise compatible and compatible with the pivot, but their restricted supports still have empty joint intersection. The required conditions therefore cannot all hold. The same argument uses a remaining forbidden word after the relaxation and full two-coordinate projections. DO01 supplies arbitrarily large critical UNSAT instances and single-budget SAT neighbors in this raw domain. This excludes these particular recognizers even without cap, not scope-changing rewrites, multiple pivots, alternative certificates or unrestricted SAT. In particular the raw statement is not automatically a statement after the BW18 complement quotient.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Aggregate integration.}
For $R$ explicit registered row IDs all pair/support arrays and reverse maps have polynomial size. At most $R$ owners, $R$ pivots per owner, $R$ pivot rows, $O(R^2)$ exterior pair tests and $O(R^4)$ elementary support-set tests per fiber suffice; each dense bitset operation costs polynomial bit work. Repeated scans and reverse lifts are polynomial in explicit length $L$ (conservatively $O((L+2)^{10})$), including failed tests, AC, replay, reconstruction and all consumers. In the DR18/BD18 typed cap, keep declared span $q=(1+r)^4$ or lower it only with a certified transport, and $h=\#\text{live rows}+\#\text{live pair edges}$. Each accepted elimination or necessary pair deletion strictly decreases $h$ at fixed $q$; no row or edge is revived. The same fixed original-input polynomial reserve $W=(B+1)(q-1)+h+1$ pays the unary move. No branching is needed here; projection retains the original guard and reverse source maps. Full acquisition and alternative closure costs are not free, and no universal polynomial bound follows. Serial same-realization $T,S$ are polynomial under the explicit chart cap, and $CM\le(T+1)S$. Reclose available certified deductions after each accepted batch and at stagnation; the checker executes AC, not full semantic or cooperative closure.

Use BD18 immutable states 1fc8b6c/1e765e0/80b4721/37578c1/33f9839 with exact MS24 delta ecbbf26, actual separate root/pin guards and original GD24/BS17/S23 maps. Plain Helly and single-pivot tests before/after the delta on the five states (898/452/1178/919/919 rows) make zero eliminations, all twenty calls OPEN. Plain four cap hits; pivot scans two smallest domains per candidate, 420 attempts, no cap hit, not exhaustive on these charts. Independent small tests check complete projected support and all reconstructed witnesses: 104 plain controls (97 SAT/7 UNSAT by exhaustive reference; terminal 80/6/18 OPEN), 102 pivot controls (94/8; terminal 62/8/32), 755 eliminations total. Fano UNSAT and single forbidden-word relaxation SAT (five models) both stall. Non-tree Helly and strict pivot controls verify. Across small and shared controls, 520826 plain support tests, 5008 fill tests, 273 fill deletions; 7865058 pivot pair tests, 18071 support tests.

The actual failed pivots yield 86 unique revalidated ternary nogoods (24/9/15/21/17), with center IDs. Conditional AC on each participating row, with and without these exact additional guarded pair restrictions, gives the same 238 new symmetric pair deletions on DR17 (192 changed bitmap entries), zero row deletions and five OPEN. Global AC reclosed after each of eight accepted DR17 batches. The guarded triples add no extra deletion over conditional AC on these probes; no speed claim. These are useful exact corrections after capped upstream closure, not a new witness acquisition. No new native/source replay, full portfolio, formal kernel, physical peak/CM or manuscript compilation. \section{Representative row folding and guard-transport firewall (DP19)}\label{sec:dp19}
The target remains Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}. DS18 and BH18 address exact owner projection; BD18 and MS24 provide necessary pair restrictions. We distinguish a decision-preserving representative restriction from a source-support-preserving deduction before combining these operations with DR18's reserve.
\begin{lemma}[Paid single-pivot conditional row replacement]\label{lem:dp19-fold}
In an explicit binary selector registry with all actual guards absorbed or exactly encoded, let $D_i$ be the live owner domains and $N(r)$ the live compatible rows of other owners. For $r\in D_i$, suppose another owner $j$ has the following certificate: for every $a\in D_j\cap N(r)$ there is $s_a\in D_i\setminus\{r\}$ with $a\in N(s_a)$ and
\[
 (N(r)\setminus N(s_a))\cap N(a)=\varnothing.
\]
Deleting $r$ preserves the exact feasible projection onto all owners other than $i$, hence preserves decision. The criterion admits polynomial bit-time discovery and verification in the explicit registry.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every feasible transversal using $r$ selects some compatible $a$ of owner $j$. All other selected rows $t$ belong to $N(r)\cap N(a)$, hence to $N(s_a)$; the pivot row is compatible separately. Replace only $r$ by $s_a$. Every surviving transversal already satisfies the original registry, proving the converse. If there is no compatible pivot row, arc closure already rejects $r$. A completed scan checks all compatible pivot rows, not merely sampled ones. Global neighborhood inclusion is a special case. With $R$ registered rows, enumerate candidate triples $r,s,a$, partitioned by the owner of $a$, using bit-vector containment on $R$ bits; even a naive implementation is polynomial, $O(R^4)$ bit operations per scan with indexed arrays. A cap hit is not a completed certificate. The actual current guard is part of the registry identity.
At fixed declared observation span and original-input cap $B$, the secondary measure $h=$ live rows plus live pair entries strictly decreases; $W=(B+1)(q-1)+h+1$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:dr18-credit} decreases by at least one at unchanged $q$. There are at most $R$ folds, with no reinitialization of the reserve. Apply certified unconditional closure initially, after each accepted move and at stagnation, relative to the actual current chart. Charge all failed scans, certificate verification, pair accesses, retained source and guard maps, and witness replay. A surviving SAT witness is already a pre-fold legal transversal and can use earlier lifts; UNSAT follows by exact projection induction. No count preservation or all-source-model preservation is claimed. With the original polynomial chart cap, serial work, space and cumulative memory are polynomial on this phase; other recursive occurrences must still satisfy the aggregate theorem.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Guard transport and all-shared uniqueness]\label{lem:dp19-firewall}
The fold commutes with every subsequent restriction on exterior rows alone, but does not in general commute with restrictions on the folded owner. Consequently no source impossibility or necessary equation may be exported from a representative chart solely because it holds there. If every coordinate of a raw table owner is observed by another owner, then each feasible exterior selection uniquely fixes its row. A single-owner replacement preserving all exterior rows cannot compress any actually feasible row of that owner.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Exact exterior projection gives the first assertion by intersecting with the exterior restriction. For noncommutation take four owners $X,Y,Z,W$ with domains of sizes $3,2,3,3$, values numbered from zero. Allow $X$--$Y$ when $x=0$ or $x=y+1$; $X$--$Z$ when $z=0$ or $x=z$; $Y$--$Z$ when $z=0$ or $z=y+1$; $Z$--$W$ when $w\ne(z+2)\bmod 3$; other pairs unrestricted. Replace $x=0$ by $x=y+1$. This is certified by the previous lemma, though no global domination exists in the initial registry. There are twelve transversals before and eight after the fold; imposing $x=0$ is satisfiable before and unsatisfiable after. Thus source-wide necessary facts require independent source certificates or a proved transport invariant. The uniqueness assertion follows from equality on the actual overlaps and distinct row words: every coordinate of the owner is already fixed by the exterior. This is why multiowner or scope-changing normalization, not merely row substitutions, is necessary for genuine representative compression in all-shared raw charts.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Raw linear NAE folding obstruction]\label{lem:dp19-stall}
An unpinned linear signed three-uniform NAE registry of minimum variable degree two has no single-pivot folding certificate of Lemma~\ref{lem:dp19-fold} in its raw six-row selector representation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix distinct candidate rows $r,s$ of one owner and a compatible pivot row $a$. Choose a coordinate $v$ where $r,s$ differ and another owner $k$ containing $v$. If $k$ is the pivot owner then $a$ agrees with $r$ at $v$, excluding $s$. Otherwise a row of $k$ may be chosen agreeing with $r$ at $v$ and with $a$ on their at-most-one common coordinate. The at-most-two pins are consistent and extend to signed NAE. By linearity this row belongs to $N(r)\cap N(a)$ but not $N(s)$. Thus no alternative $s$ passes. This proof concerns raw tables, not arbitrary learned pair registries; other certified nonlocal rules may escape the obstruction. It is not an unrestricted lower bound.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed comparison and provenance.}
On exactly the five BD18 states and MS24 combined symmetric pair deltas (with original GD24/BS17/S23 lift maps and the three distinct M23 guards), check mask subset and symmetry, then run arc closure before each scan. No new arc deletions. All ten full global-dominance scans found none; all ten single-pivot scans reached the cap of 250000 replacement tests each without a certificate. No peer fold was applied, no independent source-lift replay or post-MS24 path/affine closure rerun. On 100 seeded five-owner ternary binary CSP controls (74 SAT, 26 UNSAT), 227 folds including 58 conditional preserve exact decision by exhaustive reference enumeration; the four-owner example has twelve then eight then five transversals, and the opposite-label pinned control verifies noncommutation. Fano and deletion have zero and ten transversals and no fold. The 4092040 assertions mostly check pair-mask symmetry, not independent proof steps. No formal kernel, complete portfolio, native compiler or physical cumulative memory measurement. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/2cc714cf9bca3ac152eb36b47d27f1d77044b147}{Report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9f4ff3c421300730cff9837610414fd457c001b7}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/5f38a442dd8e5d418e1eb80c5ffa7c0114dd63aa}{results and immutable input references}.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.}
Use the identical BD18/MS24 source maps, actual guards and row IDs. Algebra/Hub/MS: multiowner replacement with verified exterior invariance; Matching/Interfaces: joint occupancy/syndrome moves and fixed reserve; Wheels: native hub block replacements preserving actual exterior words; GD/BUILD DAG: separate representative restrictions from impossibility nogoods, prove guard transport; Obstruction/Red Teams: hostile all-shared cases and multiowner credit; Solver/Synthesis: compare support-preserving closure against versioned decision-only normalization, charge all failed scans and verify source lifts. Reclose after accepted deductions and retain guard provenance. Requests only; no peer work presumed executed. U-PROGRESS, universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST remain OPEN.


\section{Two-alternative exterior cover and block representative normalization (BS18)}\label{sec:bs18}
DP19's pivot-dependent replacement preserves exterior projection but not all original support. The following complementary certificate permits multiple alternative rows or bounded blocks without a designated pivot.
\begin{lemma}[Certified bounded block cover]\label{lem:bs18-block}
In an explicit binary selector CSP with actual guards absorbed or encoded, fix an owner block $H$ of size $h$ and a compatible internal tuple $a$. Let $N(a)$ be the live exterior rows compatible with every row of $a$. For distinct compatible alternatives $b_1,\ldots,b_t$ on $H$, put $B_i=N(a)\setminus N(b_i)$. If no selection of one row from each $B_i$ is mutually compatible (allow the same row to be reused, forbid distinct rows of the same owner), deleting the internal tuple $a$ preserves exact feasible projection onto the exterior owners. For fixed $h,t$ this sufficient certificate admits polynomial discovery and replay in the explicit encoded chart.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A feasible exterior transversal compatible with $a$ but with none of the alternatives would select a blocker for every alternative, and the selected blockers would be mutually compatible. Thus every exterior word formerly extended by $a$ extends by a surviving alternative. Every retained transversal was previously feasible. Enumerate at most $R^h$ compatible internal tuples, at most $R^{ht}$ ordered alternatives, and at most $R^t$ blocker choices for $R$ registered rows; all pair accesses, membership and guard checks use explicit bit-polynomial work. If $h=1$, deletion removes a row; if $h=2$, it removes a symmetric pair. At fixed declared span and fixed polynomial cap, these are strict DR18/BD18 secondary-credit reductions without reserve reset, with closure after each accepted move. For $h>2$ a paid higher-arity representative registry and its exact consumers are additional obligations; no free translation to pair masks is asserted. A surviving witness needs only the earlier authenticated source lifts. Representative-only restrictions must be versioned separately from necessary deductions, by DP19's firewall. No count preservation is asserted.
\end{proof}
For $h=1,t=2$, blockers $B,C$ have no compatible selection exactly when $C$ is disjoint from $B\cup\bigcup_{z\in B}N(z)$. This bitset test includes single dominance ($B=\varnothing$). For a strict fixed point of global single dominance, use center $A=\{a,b,c\}$, binary $X,Y,Z$: $a$ accepts either $X$ but only $Y=0$; $b$ accepts $X=0$ and either $Y$; $c$ accepts $X=1$ and either $Y$; impose $Y=Z$, other pairs unrestricted. No row has a same-owner support superset. Nevertheless deleting $a$ preserves every feasible exterior word, by the alternatives $b,c$. Exhaustive control: six then four transversals, all four exterior words retained. This is not a claim of strict superiority to DP19's pivot rule.
\begin{lemma}[Unbounded complete cover recognition barrier]\label{lem:bs18-star}
Deciding whether deleting a specified universal center row preserves all exterior words is coNP-complete on explicit binary stars with independent Boolean leaves.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Given a 3-CNF $F$, take one binary leaf per variable, a center row $a$ compatible with every leaf value, and an alternative center row per non-tautological clause, compatible exactly with its falsifying assignments (one allowed bit on each occurring variable, both on absent variables). All leaf pairs are unrestricted. After deletion of $a$, a leaf word extends exactly when it falsifies a clause. Thus preservation of every exterior word is equivalent to UNSAT of $F$. A missing exterior word can be checked against every remaining center row in polynomial time. The star before deletion is trivially SAT, so this is not an unrestricted decision lower bound. Complete coverage acquisition cannot be assumed polynomial in a proof of P=NP.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Raw bounded-cover obstruction]\label{lem:bs18-stall}
Every unpinned linear signed 3-NAE registry of minimum coordinate degree two defeats the $h=1,t\le2$ certificate in its raw six-row selector representation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For each alternative choose a coordinate on which it differs from the target row. If the chosen coordinates agree, choose one exterior owner containing that coordinate and a row agreeing with the target; it blocks both. If they differ, choose respective exterior owners, distinct by linearity. Each agrees with the target on its sole shared coordinate. If these exterior owners intersect, their at-most-one common coordinate is not either target coordinate, and one can prescribe a common bit on it. Signed NAE has full two-coordinate projections, so both compatible blocking rows exist. The argument with one alternative is immediate. This includes DO01 critical raw cores under the degree hypothesis, not arbitrary post-normalization charts or unrestricted algorithms. More generally, if all coordinates of a block are observed outside it, a feasible exterior transversal uniquely determines its internal tuple, so no actual feasible tuple can be replaced while preserving that exterior. A scope-changing or sufficiently large block must escape this invariant.
\end{proof}
\paragraph{Executed cooperative comparison.}
On the SAME five immutable BD18 pair states, import MS24 combined deltas and BH18 mode-true pair deltas with actual distinct root/pin guards. Check live-mask symmetry, run AC before scans and after accepted deletions, compare direct-first, single-dominance, and two-alternative scans. All five root states remain OPEN, zero deletions, full single scans total 454896 alternatives; each two-cover scan caps at 200000 pair tests (1000000 total), 26247 alternatives and 983 union reads. No exhaustive two-cover failure is claimed. Subsequent bounded row search has 42 nodes per mode; all fifteen SAT source lifts verified via GD24/BS17 maps against S23 actual owners and guards. 124 small controls (112 SAT, 12 UNSAT), direct vs reduced labels agree; Fano UNSAT/deletion SAT and odd triangle UNSAT. Strict two-cover example and separate fixed-point exhaustive check as above. Imported peer deltas not independently replayed; BH18 triples not separately consumed. All upstream acquisition, failed attempts, AC, pair accesses, masks, source and guard maps, and reconstruction are charged. At most $R$ unary row deletions or $R^2$ pair deletions before reserve exhaustion; fixed polynomial chart cap gives polynomial same-execution $T,S$ and $CM\le(T+1)S$ for this phase, not the complete solver. No full affine closure, native replay, complete portfolio, kernel/LaTeX compilation or physical memory instrumentation. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/053b046be4fc59e40030d4afe6e621d97c74ddaf}{Report}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/9cce78a45624abd4c796e9d41c20827c6c7a66fa}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/f09d79ee4e62a8a34930a826cd2c9d731f1d5cc6}{results with actual source witnesses}.

\section{S24: Charged pair-block fusion and an explicit-fusion barrier}\label{sec:s24}
The target is U-PROGRESS (Obligation~\ref{obl:progress}), not a further fixed-scope terminal catalogue. Extend DP19 exterior-preserving replacement and BH18 Helly elimination to an authenticated scope-changing operation on the same BD18/MS24/BH18 charts. All prior guards must already be enforced by the exact selector registry.
\begin{lemma}[Exact pair-block representative fusion]\label{lem:s24-fusion}
In an explicit binary owner-selector registry, merge distinct owners $A,B$ by enumerating their allowed row pairs $(a,b)$. Give each pair the exterior neighborhood $N(a)\cap N(b)$ restricted to other owners. Retain one actual pair per maximal neighborhood under inclusion, leaving all exterior-exterior edges unchanged. The merged chart has exactly the same existential projection onto all exterior rows, and every satisfying merged transversal lifts to an original one.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
An original transversal chooses a compatible pair and exterior rows contained in that pair's neighborhood. The neighborhood is contained in some retained maximal neighborhood, so substituting the retained actual pair preserves all exterior rows. Conversely, each retained row is an actual compatible pair and each exterior edge certifies both old edges, hence expansion is legal. Store the actual original row IDs and concatenate disjoint owner-lift lists. Counts and full internal support are not preserved in general. As in DP19, new guards on removed representatives are not transportable without separate proof; no representative restriction is a source impossibility. Reclose with certified unconditional rules initially, after each accepted deduction/merge and at stagnation.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Fixed-cap growth credit]\label{lem:s24-credit}
Fix a polynomial row cap $C=C(N)$ in original input length before dispatch. For nonempty admitted charts let $B=C+\binom C2$, $h$ be live rows plus undirected live pair edges, and $m$ the live owner count. The integer
\[
\psi=(B+1)(m-1)+h+1
\]
strictly decreases on support deletions and on every accepted pair-block fusion staying within $C$, even if $h$ increases to $B$. Empty domain is a terminal. With outer base credit $q$ from S23 and $M=(B+1)C+1$, $W=M(q-1)+\psi$ contracts on same-$q$ moves as above; if $\sum_i q_i\le q-1$, then $\sum_i W_i\le M(q-1)<W$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$1\le\psi<M$ since $m\le C$ and $h\le B$. A merge lowers $m$ by one and increases $h$ by at most $B$, so lowers $\psi$ by at least one; a deletion lowers $h$. Each trial enumerates at most $C^2$ pairs, and comparing all their bit-vector neighborhoods costs $O(C^5)$ bit operations, plus polynomial graph rebuilding, closure, guard/source verification and provenance. There are at most $C^2$ owner pairs per complete scan. Charge every failed/capped scan, old and new graph simultaneously, reconstruction lists, all accesses, and each actual outer occurrence. On the admitted phase this gives polynomial serial time and peak space on the same execution and $\mathrm{CM}\le(T+1)S$. The cap cannot be reset at fixed $q$; no other same-$q$ owner increase is covered. The outer inequality follows from $W_i\le Mq_i$. A decline is not a decision.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}[Explicit fusion-only obstruction]\label{lem:s24-fusion-barrier}
Even for satisfiable explicit binary selector registries of polynomial size with no forced bits, every sequence that completes by pair fusion of actual row tuples, exterior-support-preserving maximal-neighborhood deletion and support-preserving closure can require superpolynomially many live rows. More precisely, for the family below every complete binary merge tree has a block requiring $2^{\Omega(n)}$ rows.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use one owner per edge of $K_n$, with all four assignments to its two endpoint bits; compatibility is equality on shared endpoints and unrestricted otherwise. All global words are legal, and initial arc closure changes nothing. A binary tree on $L=\binom n2$ edge owners contains a block of between $L/3$ and $2L/3$ leaves (descend through a child exceeding $2L/3$; then take the larger child). Let $D$ be vertices touched by both block and complement. Vertices exclusively inside and exclusively outside cannot coexist, because their connecting edge would violate one exclusivity. Thus one side has all its edges inside $D$, so $\binom{|D|}2\ge L/3$ and $|D|=\Omega(n)$. Inductively all assignments to bits occurring in at least two current blocks remain attainable: extend each new shared-bit assignment to old shared bits, take a premerge transversal, and retain its entire exterior selection using Lemma~\ref{lem:s24-fusion}. Every new shared bit occurs in some unchanged exterior block, so its value is preserved. Support-preserving closure cannot remove these transversals. At the balanced block every boundary-bit word occurs, and every actual row tuple fixes one such word. At least $2^{|D|}$ rows are necessary, even after dominance. Initial explicit input is polynomial in $n$. This refutes universal completion of this explicit fusion-only procedure under any fixed polynomial row cap, not arbitrary SAT, factored projections, or native tetravalent normalization; the constructed instances are trivially SAT.
\end{proof}
A separate nonincrease obstruction applies to raw unpinned linear signed NAE3 with minimum variable occurrence degree three: two owners have 18 compatible tuples if they share one bit, 36 otherwise. Every pair coordinate is seen by an exterior owner. That owner intersects the pair in at most two coordinates, and any consistent two pins extend to NAE. Distinct pair tuples differ on such a coordinate; their nonempty neighborhoods at the exterior owner are disjoint, so no containment is possible. The resulting domain has more than the original twelve rows. Thus row growth or another operation is necessary even at the initial raw stage; learned charts are not covered by this obstruction.
\paragraph{Executed comparison.} Same five immutable BD18 charts, MS24 and BH18 exact deltas (separate root and opposite pin guards), prior DR18/GD24/BS17/S23 lifts. Verified live pair symmetry and delta subset; upstream proof/replay is a dependency. Actual AC before, after each merge, and at stagnation. With cap 256 compatible pair trials per candidate, the nonincrease mode finds no merge on ten baseline/combined runs. With fixed twice-initial total row cap and four-merge experimental limit, all ten runs perform four merges and remain OPEN. Final live rows on the five distinct charts: 908, 472, 1202, 939, 939; owner counts 26, 7, 20, 16, 16. No selected shared-chart pair is deleted by dominance; two standalone DR17 AC row removals, none combined. Growth mode 4915578 pair tests, 46230636 dominance tests, 16708842 graph tests, 3427 cap declines, 1023272 AC checks. On 100 seeded small binary CSP controls, 79 SAT/21 UNSAT, 238 merges and 179 reference decision/lift checks. No new source witness replay, native compiler, full closure portfolio, measured CM or formal kernel execution. No speed or universal eligibility claim. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/c3e7c258cdd99f17aca1bd328de9930a91ea0133}{Report}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/16f8cf4d9a1aa45bd44d76b2acc81860b9432757}{nonincrease results}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/206acaf61155fa806cd17029f9cc6f05d2312828}{growth checker}; \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/5ff7e85d628feff01eb5853881093b68a7de86bc}{growth results and actual row lifts}.
\paragraph{Parallel stagnation.} Use precisely those five guarded charts and their four-fusion versions. Algebra/Hub/MS: factor exact exterior support or derive nonlinear scope reduction; Matching/Interfaces: preserve tuple IDs and joint occupancy/syndrome; Wheels: native transport and actual private-coordinate contraction; GD/BUILD DAG: factored block compatibility with paid identity and consumers; Structural/Obstruction/Red Teams: simultaneous hostile post-fusion examples after easy escapes; Potential/Solver/Synthesis: compare same-chart adaptive fusion and exact projection under fixed cap and paid outer descent, reclose and replay source lifts. Requests only. The universal discovery of an eligible cap-preserving move or alternate terminal on every reachable state remains OPEN, as do universal SEM/CLOSE and unconditional COST. No publication authorization.


\section{DO19: Basis-invariant disjunctive factor discovery and arbitrary affine guards}\label{sec:do19}
The S24 explicit-fusion barrier motivates changing representation rather than growing row tuples. DO15's coordinate-support graph need not discover independent dual observation spaces in a dense basis. Here we prove a paid acquisition rule for a specified class, not U-PROGRESS.

\begin{lemma}[Basis-invariant full product acquisition]\label{lem:do19-atoms}
Suppose an explicit deduplicated nonempty registry consists of all Cartesian tuples of $s\ge2$ lists of nonempty positive-rank forbidden affine packets. The factor observation spans $U_j$ form a direct sum in the dual vector space. Each factor has at least two distinct packet row spaces, pairwise intersecting only in zero within that factor, with one affine packet per space. Then the factorization can be recovered in deterministic polynomial bit time in any invertible coordinate basis, without knowing the factors.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Form and deduplicate all nonzero pairwise intersections of parent packet row spaces, retaining the inclusion-minimal ones. Each intersection is the direct sum of the factor spaces on which the two tuples agree. Every individual factor space is attained alone by holding that factor fixed and changing all the others. Therefore the minimal nonzero intersections are precisely those spaces. Two different atoms occur together in a parent space iff they belong to different factors. Noncooccurrence, with equality, is the equivalence relation giving the groups. Verify the equivalence relation, the direct-sum ranks of the group spans, exactly one contained atom per group in each parent, and that the sum of the restricted equations including their affine right-hand sides reproduces the original canonical packet. A failed verification declines. Deduplicate the resulting labeled tuples and verify that their cardinality equals the product of their projected list cardinalities. As every tuple belongs to this product, equality proves full product coverage without enumerating more tuples than the input. The tests themselves are sound on arbitrary input; the stated promises guarantee discovery. There are quadratically many pair intersection candidates in the number of parent packets, with polynomial Gaussian elimination, containment/cooccurrence, dictionary and integer arithmetic costs. Reconstruction uses actual dual basis maps and Gaussian solution, not an assumed coordinate-disjoint representation. No complete semantic key is required. Missing tuples or overlapping atom spaces can cause decline.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Arbitrary affine guard on a disjunction]\label{lem:do19-guard}
Let $F=\bigvee_j F_j$ with each $F_j$ depending only on its observation space $U_j$. Let a consistent affine guard have rowspace $H$ and right-hand-side functional $g:H\to\mathbb F_2$. Then guarded decision is exactly the disjunction of local decisions with projected guards $g|_{H\cap U_j}$. Projecting each guard and lifting a winning local word costs polynomial Gaussian operations, independent of the numerical rank of the guard.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A local word is a linear functional $f$ on $U_j$. It extends to the original guard iff $f=g$ on $H\cap U_j$. Necessity follows by restriction; sufficiency follows by defining the functional $h+u\mapsto g(h)+f(u)$ consistently on $H+U_j$ and extending it to the full dual space. Gaussian elimination computes both the intersection and a witness lift. In a disjunctive branch the other $F_i$ need not hold; the extension imposes no other regional constraints. The disjunction of the local guarded decisions is therefore exact. This argument does not permit independent guard projection for conjunctions or for the all-block sparse-hole terms of GD21. Indeed arbitrary 3-CNF is uniformly equivalent to independent single-clause blocks on fresh occurrence copies subject to an affine equality guard identifying copies of each original variable. Each block individually is trivial; joint guarded conjunction is NP-complete. Independent projected guard checks on conjunctive blocks need not compose.
\end{proof}

For Lemma~\ref{lem:do19-atoms}, the parent conjunction of tuple avoidances equals $\bigvee_j F_j$. Keeping the authenticated unabsorbed parent chart with actual guard, write $M$ for parent rank mass, $M_j$ and $m_j$ for factor mass and list size. Then
\[
 M=\sum_j M_j\prod_{h\ne j}m_h\ge2\sum_jM_j,\qquad
 \sum_j(1+M_j)\le M=(1+M)-1
\]
since every $m_j\ge2$ and $M_j\ge m_j$. This pays all children with $c=1$ on the stated chart domain; projected guards add no avoidance packets and substitution/closure cannot increase child avoidance rank mass. This inequality is not claimed relative to an already rank-reduced parent. Preserve exact guard and source maps, charge retaining/reconstructing the unabsorbed representation and all failed scans, and do not reset any outer credit. Run certified forced-information closure initially, after deductions and at stagnation; quotienting or other rewrites may leave the recognition domain, in which case an equivalent retained representation requires paid provenance. Under a fixed original-input polynomial size cap the admitted step costs polynomial bit time and peak space; $\mathrm{CM}\le(T+1)S$ on the same execution. Universal availability of such a step is OPEN.

\paragraph{Executed controls and same-chart cooperation.}
Three seeded dense invertible basis changes each on the original Fano double-product and double single-sign-neighbor product recovered 14 atoms, two groups of seven and ranks 6/6. Parent mass 196, total child credit 30. Four guards per chart (empty, rank one, rank two, inconsistent), 24 complete reference decisions (9 SAT, 15 UNSAT), all agreed, nine reconstructed SAT words verified against actual transformed packets and guards. 17859 Gaussian calls, 1347 candidate guard-lift trials. Child observation words were enumerated for finite reference checks, with Gaussian full-guard extension; no general polynomial child solver. All six one-hole controls declined. Code and results: \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/06d84123cc4b3692b3d51a339c32b0e431b12f8b}{checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/3b4b6b5233aac8eeb08ecbede54610e80ef70dcf}{results}.

On the SAME five immutable BD18 pair charts, retain actual guards, import MS24 and BH18 pair deltas in the combined mode and rerun arc closure after each batch. The exact conjunctive factor test joins two owners iff some live pair is forbidden. Distinct components would be independent and their actual row-ID witnesses could be concatenated; every live pair across components is allowed. All ten baseline/combined jobs remain OPEN, one component each with 30/11/24/20/20 owners; constrained edges 223/54/186/164/164 before and 225/54/186/164/164 after import, live rows unchanged, no additional AC deletion. Live pair symmetry and separate guards verified. No upstream certificate replay, new source lift, full closure, native replay, kernel or physical cumulative-memory measurements. \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/c99565d4aba58b839cb244d4020903ae1f78f013}{Peer checker}, \href{https://github.com/j15marti/pnp/commit/b96ef71b0da78092238e0fa85266613c485a6daf}{exact peer result and input references}. This is a complementary test on the actual chart, not a claim that the raw packet recognizer ran on its pair-mask representation.
